# Backlog — Open-Loops Ledger

> OPEN LOOPS is reconciled at every session open and close. Nothing is tracked only in chat.
> An item leaves OPEN LOOPS only by meeting its close-criterion or being explicitly tombstoned.
> When writing a close criterion, state what its instrument reads if the work never happened. If that's the same as success, it isn't an instrument. See oddities, "a close criterion whose instrument can't fail."
> A backlog line's stated **mechanism/premise** is a claim, not a fact — probe it before building on it, exactly like a logged bug's repro. Two lines in three HOs were wrong on inspection (HO 512's "`getNewsFeed` already joins `observation_entities`"; HO 514's "the spine is SOURCES"), both caught by checking, not trusting. A third would make it a convention problem, not coincidence.
> Sections: OPEN LOOPS (dated, owned) · QUEUED (next builds) · BANKED (gated) · WATCH (standing observations) · FIT & FINISH (audit findings) · DONE (tombstones, append-only).

Owners: **Corey** (human action) · **Code** (a build/run) · **cron** (happens on schedule).

---

- **Markets tape read burn (HO 577 — found during the Turso-outage diagnosis, unrelated to the primaries arc).** *Owner:* Code. `getLatestMarketTicks` is `unstable_cache(..., { revalidate: 60 })` and `MarketsTapeClient` polls it at `POLL_MS = 60_000` — **the TTL and the poll interval are the same number, so effectively every poll regenerates.** Each regeneration runs an unbounded `SELECT symbol, MAX(ticked_at) … GROUP BY symbol` over `market_ticks` plus a second scan whose `julianday(ticked_at) >= julianday('now','-9 day')` predicate **wraps the column in a function and defeats any index on it**. Runtime logs showed ~4 requests/minute sustained (consistent with ~4 tabs left open); the Jul-25 step plus flat writes fit polling, not crawling or cron. **The arithmetic is inference, not measurement** — derived from the log and the code, and this session produced two confident read-cost claims both wrong on inspection (see the read-budget WATCH). Also record that `app/api/markets/latest/route.ts`'s own header comment asserts **no per-request DB hit between writes**, which is **false**. *Close:* a probe HO sizing `market_ticks` and measuring rows-read per regeneration, then a fix — **both are code and belong to that probe HO, not here or the route comment.** *Instrument:* Turso rows-read against tab count. **HO 582 update — partly discharged, not closed (the REPLACED-criterion pattern).** STEP 0 (`markets-read-burn-582.ts`, `3baf98c`) MEASURED it — 12,240 rows/regen → ~17.6M/day per open tab, ~20% of the ~88M plateau — and C1–C4 shipped (`cf1516d` TTL 60→86400 · `8396068` sargable spark window · `2a86c3f` per-symbol seeks replacing the covering-index scan · `d0feb8f` visibility-gate the poll), so the original close criterion (a probe sizing the table + measuring rows-read per regen, then a fix) is MET. **Criterion REPLACED:** the loop closes on the **Turso per-DB rows-read slope over ~24h vs tab count** (prediction on the record: ~a fifth cut, a level shift not a resolution — roadmap "Also (HO 582)"; if the slope barely moves the burn model is wrong and *that* is the finding). The broader **read-budget attribution** item (WATCH) stays open aimed at summarize / LDA / traffic for the residual ~80%. Two children spun out below. **HO 683 annotation, under the same attribution as the `:71` strike (Corey's 2026-09-02 6h dashboard capture, avg × count):** the tape's measured steady-state share is **~570k rows/day** (65 execs per 6h × 2,220 avg), making it the **second-largest total burner on the first clean ledger**, behind the committees rollup. Read as a reading, not a constant — and per the HO 683 narrowing it is attribution, not a sizing figure: a probe aimed here re-measures on its own table.
- **New (HO 582) · markets cron duplicate write (P3, instrument stated).** *Owner:* Code. Two rows per symbol tied **to the millisecond** on `ticked_at`, observed for **CPI (ids 6580/6582)** and **WTI (6581/6583)** at `ticked_at` **2026-07-31T20:00:35.801Z / :36.009Z** (read directly from the duplicate rows in STEP 0's dup probe, not derived from the ids). **Render impact is nil in every traced path** — duplicate spark points share a coordinate, the 1W anchor re-assigns the same value, and `change_pct` uses a strict `market_date < ?` prior-reference — and HO 582 C3's `LIMIT 1` now dedupes the tape read, so the symptom is invisible (which is *why* it needs its own instrument: a read-time dedupe removes the symptom of a write-path defect — oddities). **Byte-identical `ticked_at` is itself the strongest evidence, and it points AWAY from a second execution.** `ticked_at` is `new Date().toISOString()`, evaluated when the insert args are built — so concurrent invocation, a retried body, and a double `processSymbol` would each recompute it and land *different* milliseconds. Two rows sharing a timestamp to the millisecond means the **same args were sent twice**, i.e. a **transport/client-level retry of one `db.execute`** (libsql client or network layer), not a second run of the code path. That also explains the otherwise-puzzling symbol set: a transient retry hits arbitrary statements, so CPI+WTI sharing no source/cadence subset is the expected signature, not a constraint needing explanation. **The concrete candidate in this codebase is `lib/db.ts::boundedFetch`** (HO 238): it retries a timed-out/aborted request exactly once with a fresh clone, and an `INSERT` is non-idempotent — a first insert that committed server-side but whose response was slow enough to trip the 10s abort would be retried and land a byte-identical second row. Verify against `boundedFetch`'s retry path before assuming concurrency. **Byte-identity is already confirmed (this sweep, ids 6580/6582 + 6581/6583): `ticked_at`, `price`, `change_pct`, and `market_date` are all byte-identical within each pair** — the retry signature, so the DB-side evidence already favors retry over two real executions. **Revised instrument reading — the outcomes INVERT from the earlier draft:** `cron_runs` rows for `/api/cron/markets` around those ids still discriminate — `payload.outcomes` carries the per-symbol set (verified: `payload = {ticked, failed, outcomes}`) — but **one row** is now the PREDICTED result and CONFIRMS the retry hypothesis (NOT a falsification, and not a cue to check Vercel's cron-invocation log — a client retry never appears there); **two rows** would resurrect concurrency and require explaining how two independent invocations computed identical millisecond timestamps for two different symbols. The byte-identity check above is the paired second signal (any divergence within a pair would mean two real executions, not a retry). **Fix shape follows the answer, and a cron lock is probably NOT it:** a lock addresses concurrency and does nothing about a client retry — if the retry hypothesis holds, the options are an **idempotency guard on the write** (`UNIQUE(symbol, ticked_at)`, exactly the tie the duplicates violate) or **accepting duplicates as harmless** now that C3 dedupes the read; do not scope either until the instrument runs. **A lead, now a coincidence:** the ids sit at a **collision hour** — `0 */4` and the weekday `0,30 13-21` fmp pass both fire at 16:00 and 20:00 UTC, and 2026-07-31 was a Friday — but under the retry hypothesis this is coincidence, and recording a falsified lead is the point: the naive collision doesn't fit anyway (CPI is fred/monthly/units:pc1, WTI is fred/daily, `?source=fmp` filters both out, so the fmp pass cannot have written them; **no `?source=` filter produces CPI+WTI alone** — `?source=fred` would also write TNX and UNEMP, and two overlapping bare runs would double *many* symbols, not two). **The mechanism is documented as safe, and the duplicate is the counterexample.** `lib/db.ts:35-37` justifies the retry with "Writes are upsert/UPDATE/DELETE-shaped or benign append-only inserts (HO 238 idempotency grep), so a retried-after-timeout write is safe." The markets write is a bare `INSERT INTO market_ticks` with no conflict clause — append-only, and classified benign under that grep. **Both halves of the classification are individually true and the conclusion still doesn't follow:** append-only inserts are not *idempotent*, they are merely *harmless when duplicated*, and for the tape that harmlessness only became true at HO 582 C3 (before it, the duplicate rendered twice). The retry replays `retryInput`, a clone of the original Request (`lib/db.ts:44`), so the identical serialized args go out — which is why the duplicate is byte-identical rather than merely close, and why this fits the evidence better than any second execution. **Second instrument, cheaper than `cron_runs`:** the retry logs `[db] timeout, retrying` (`lib/db.ts:52`). A hit in the 20:00 2026-07-31 runtime logs confirms it directly; absence is weaker evidence (log retention), so check `cron_runs` regardless. **Check `cron_runs` retention/payload survival first** (the route comment references a 30-min window; `cron_runs` is the durable record, but confirm the old row + its payload persist before relying on it). *Close:* one run or two, named. **Scope note — this is not markets-only.** If it holds, the HO 238 idempotency argument under-covers every bare append-only `INSERT` reachable through `getDb()`, not just this one. Re-running that grep with "duplicates are harmless" separated from "the write is idempotent" is its own item; do not widen this P3 into it.
- **New (HO 582) · 13 retired legacy symbols in `market_ticks` (P4, hygiene).** *Owner:* Code. - **`pageErr` intermittent on the `smoke.spec.ts` prod crawl — STILL OPEN; every prod fire is UNATTRIBUTED and the cause COUNT is UNKNOWN (HO 572 → 574 → 589 → 590; partition retracted 591).** *Owner:* Code; opened 2026-07-29 (first observed run). **Two 574-era claims corrected in 589 (do not re-inherit them):** (a) `args[]=HTML` is the **structural / markup** variant, **not** "text-content" — `react-dom-client.development.js:5240` fills the first `%s` with `fromText ? "text" : "HTML"`, and CI captured `HTML`; a text-content mismatch would read `args[]=text`. (b) The "identical message across all four runs ⇒ **single cause**" inference is **withdrawn** — the component-naming diff (the second `%s`) is **DEV-only** (prod `react-dom` bundle: 0 occurrences of the `hydration-mismatch` link/template), so the minified prod string is **byte-identical for every structural mismatch anywhere in the app**; message identity carries **zero** cause-count information (589 M1/M2; oddities). Raising the HO 574 200-char cap buys nothing — the prod crawl can **never** self-identify a component. **What 589/590 found and fixed — two DISTINCT latent bugs, both real, both correctness hardening, and NEITHER proven to be a prod cause (deliberately UNNUMBERED; see the partition retraction below):** **(1) the markets tape (structural, `args[]=HTML`), FIXED HO 590 (`626c251`):** `MarketsTapeClient` seeded its clock with `useState(() => Date.now())`, a render-time read that gates the `<span class="markets-tape-arrow">` node via `showSlots`; under a manufactured clock skew across the 26h stale edge / 09:30–16:00-ET market boundary the arrows collapse → `args[]=HTML`. Named via the DEV owner tree (`TickItem` → the arrow span), fixed by prop-drilling a server `nowMs` (HO 489/490 shape). **(2) the `lob-age` text drift (`args[]=text`) — a SEPARATE defect, FIXED HO 590 (`601f54a`):** `FilingRow.relAge()`'s render-time `Date.now()` drifts the "Nd ago" string across a day boundary; distinct variant, kept distinct in the record. **The load-bearing finding (589 M4 + 590 C5 — why this does NOT close):** prod `/` is served **fresh per request** (`X-Vercel-Cache: MISS`, `Age: 0`, `no-store`, `Date` advancing), so the SSR→hydration gap is **sub-second** and **cannot straddle** a 26h/market boundary; the harness confirms `control(+0)` (that same sub-second gap) **never fires**. So the tape — the obvious structural candidate — is a real latent bug but is **NOT the mechanism of prod's ~42 `args[]=HTML` fires**, which **remain unattributed**. `isMarketOpen` is TZ-explicit (`America/New_York`), ruling out the locale variant too. **The "cause #1 / cause #2" partition is RETRACTED (HO 591) — it rested on a premise C5 destroyed.** That split existed for exactly one reason: `/` carried the tape and the inner pages didn't, so they *had* to be different bugs. C5 measured the tape out of prod, and the reason to separate them went with it. **The corrected statement: all ~42 prod `args[]=HTML` fires are UNATTRIBUTED** — `/`, the **six `?stage=` home variants**, **`/president`**, and **`/members`** alike — **not "one fixed, one remaining."** **Whether that is one cause or several is now UNKNOWN:** message identity carries **zero** cause-count information (589 M1), and the evidence that once separated the routes is gone. **`/president` stays named in the firing set** — it fired in prod (HO 574) and is **tapeless**; narrowing the record to "the members surface" would silently drop a route from a firing set whose *growth* is the very thing HO 574's scoping guard warns about. **Checked and DEAD as a single-cause candidate — a shared client island spanning the firing set (HO 591):** there is **no `SessionProvider` and no `useSession` anywhere in the repo** (auth is resolved **server-side** in `HeaderBar` / `DashboardV2Header` and prop-drilled into `AuthButton`), and **`app/layout.tsx` is bare** — no shared client wrapper. The *directly imported* client islands of `/` and `/members` are **disjoint** (5 vs 9, zero overlap); the **three** they do share arrive through their respective server headers — **`AuthButton`** (server-resolved `user` prop; `useTransition` its only hook), **`CyclingTimestamp`** (HO 489-verified hydration-safe by construction), and **`MobileNavDrawer`** (`useState(false)`, a constant seed, no non-deterministic read) — and **all three are deterministic at render, so none can skew.** *(Note the precise form: it is **not** true that `/` and the inner pages share no client component — they share three; the point is that all three are safe.)* Record this as a **ruled-out lead**, not as support for either partition. **Also RULED OUT, written down because it is a false lead a future reader will re-derive from scratch:** `/` **307-redirects an anonymous visitor to `/welcome`** (the `ct_seen` gate in `app/page.tsx`) — but that does **NOT** mean the `/` fires were on the landing page. `smoke.spec.ts` sets `ct_seen` **context-wide** (`:40` defines `GATE_COOKIE`, applied at `:143` for the route crawl and `:270` for the targeted interactions), so **every `pageErr`-collecting visit renders the terminal, not the landing.** The single no-cookie visit is the deliberate `gate / deep-link param drop` test (`:246`), which asserts the anon bounce and **feeds no collector.** **What stands UNEXPLAINED (an observation, not a lead):** `/members` fired at `control(+0)` in the 589 sweep and prod fired `committees-redirect`→`/members` (`/members/pass-rate` 307-redirects there, so it's one surface), but **25 + 12 fresh-context re-tries produced 0 fires** and the single-page-bleed hypothesis **failed to reproduce** (590 C2) — the sharpest unexplained observation in the set, and **not** a named second cause. **Reproduction lever (HO 590, committed):** `scripts/diagnostic/hydration-clock-harness-590.ts` — a **local-only** clock-skew rig (needs `next dev` for React 19's DEV describeDiff; never rides `e2e-prod.yml`) that manufactures the SSR-vs-hydration skew and captures the DEV owner tree + `args[]=HTML`/`text` variant. **HO 592 — H5 (data-dependent invalid HTML nesting) FALSIFIED, cleanly, in one pass.** The browser's tree-construction algorithm restructures invalid nesting before React hydrates, which would produce `args[]=HTML` with no time dependence at all — it fit every constraint the clock hypothesis failed. A static parse5 diff over **all 37 `ROUTES`** found **0 reparented / dropped / synthesised nodes**, with the detector self-validated first (6 positive fixtures + 2 negative, incl. a regression case pinning `<div>` inside `<a>` as VALID HTML5) and scanner coverage checked (1460 authored elements = 1460 parsed on `/`, every one matched by source offset — so the clean result is a real read, not a short one). The **HO 473 SVG `<title>` class is clean at HEAD both statically (4 sites, all single-child) and at runtime (0 multi-child titles in rendered prod HTML across 37 routes).** One hypothesis fewer; nothing named. **HO 593 — the intermittent `/members` 500 is a SEPARATE FAULT and is now split out of this loop.** The discriminator is the pageErr MESSAGE, which the crawl already logs: over 54 runs / 4,230 route-hit cells (one site-wide outage run excluded, see below), **87 `#418` instances vs 12 "Server Components render" instances**, and the 12 land only on `committees-redirect` / `members-pass-rate` / `member-detail` / `stale`. Every `/`-family route (home, all six `?stage=`, dashboard-v2/classic) and **`/president`** carry `#418` and have **never** 500'd. The 500 is a whole-route `__next_error__` shell, not a segment failing after a flushed shell, so it cannot be producing a structurally different tree. That 500 is now separately named, fixed and tombstoned (DONE, HO 593-595) — **this loop keeps only the `#418` fires, exactly as open and as unattributed as before.** **Standing instrumentation requirement (HO 593/594, oddities):** this class must be measured at the **QUERY level, never the route level** — `lib/db.ts` retries once, so a route failure needs *both* attempts to breach and the route-failure rate is roughly the **square** of the query-breach rate. Route-level counts systematically understate it; HO 593's M2 stage table is the worked example and is recorded unranked for that reason. *Close (unchanged, and now sharper — an intermittent's **absence is not evidence of a fix**; and since C5 proved neither fixed bug was ever the prod mechanism, a green crawl after them is **doubly** ambiguous and verifies **nothing** in this arc):* a **named cause for the prod fires PLUS green runs**, never "the crawl went green" alone. **HO 656 §D — THE RATE HAS NOT STEP-CHANGED, measured over the scheduled daily prod crawls from HO 590's deploy (2026-08-02) to 08-12.** Read-only; no fixes, no new crawls, no attribution attempts. **One day's log is GONE** (`gh run view`: `log not found`, retention), so the span is **10 observable days, reported as such rather than extrapolated**. Fires on **7 of 10**; events/day **08-02:2 · 03:1 · 04:4 · 05:1 · 06:— · 07:0 · 08:5 · 09:10 · 10:0 · 11:0 · 12:1**. **The clean days are scattered, not a step:** 08-07 (0) is immediately followed by the two heaviest days (5, then 10), and the 08-10/08-11 pair is broken by a fire on 08-12. **No deploy in the 604–655 train moved the rate in either direction** — which is the datapoint the entry wanted, and it is what this check reads **if nothing happened**, stated up front so a null is not mistaken for a finding. Affected routes are the age-rendering feed set throughout (`home`, `home-stage-*`, `dashboard-v2`, `bills`, `stale`, `committees-redirect`, `member-detail`), recorded as an OBSERVATION and not a hypothesis. **METHOD NOTE, because it inverts the obvious check: run CONCLUSIONS under-report fires.** `expect.soft` fails the test, `--retries=2` reports it **flaky**, and the workflow still concludes **`success`** — so a green run list reads exactly like a fixed bug. The counts here come from the per-route `pageErr=` log lines, not from conclusions. **Fire count from the HO 683 proof crawls (four runs, all against prod, named so this WATCH's denominator stays honest):** `33673524519` **3 flaky / 38 passed**; `33674297359` **5 flaky / 36 passed** (+1 real failure, the synthetic FORCED-RED); `33675017384` **0 flaky / 41 passed** (+1 FORCED-RED); `33675423784` **1 flaky / 40 passed**. Every flake carried the same `pageErr#N=[Minified React error #418 … args[]=HTML]` string, and **`--retries=2` absorbed all of them, so all four runs conclude green** — which is this entry's own point about conclusions, now with four more data points. The variance across runs (3 / 5 / 0 / 1 on the same corpus within 20 minutes) is itself the finding: whatever fires it is not route-stable. **HO 685's crawls, same convention, denominator kept honest:** `33709162259` (STEP 2 dispatch, armed) **1 flaky / 40 passed**, the single fire on `[home]` carrying the same `pageErr#N=[Minified React error #418 … args[]=HTML]` string, absorbed by `--retries=2`, run concludes green. The FF and STEP 5.5 crawls are added on the close. **One thing HO 685 changes about this entry's evidence base, worth stating because it cuts the other way:** a cancelled run produces no per-route log lines at all, so every crawl the old concurrency group killed was a *silently missing sample* here — not a zero, an absence. The four cancellations in the HO 685 sweep are four crawls this WATCH never got to count, and the fix means future ones arrive. **HO 687 — THE INSTRUMENT ARRIVED, AND THE FIRST TWO PROD FIRES ARE LOCALIZED. This entry is UPDATED, not struck: attribution evidence is in hand, a named cause is not.** **The ledger, extended (2026-08-15 → 09-03, counted from per-route `pageErr=` lines):** 20 scheduled runs, **17 complete / 3 cancelled**, **50 fire-lines**, mean **2.94/run**, and **12 of 17 (70.6%) carry at least one fire**. Per run: 09-03 **8** · 09-01 1 · 08-31 0 · 08-30 3 · 08-29 **7** · 08-28b 2 · 08-28a 4 · 08-26 0 · 08-25 2 · 08-24 0 · 08-23 4 · 08-22 0 · 08-21 2 · 08-20 4 · 08-17 2 · 08-16 **8** · 08-15 3. **08-12 → 08-14 are UNHARVESTED (outside the 100-run API window), not zero.** **The conclusion under-reporting is now demonstrated rather than asserted: 2026-08-29 concluded `success` carrying SEVEN fire-lines; 08-31 concluded `success` carrying none.** **Cancellation has TWO shapes and this entry previously had one:** *zero-lines* (the crawl never started — 08-18, 08-19) versus *partial* (killed mid-crawl — 09-02 at **29 of ~37** route lines, a **partially sampled** run whose completed routes are real samples). All three predate the HO 685 concurrency fix; the first post-fix daily (09-03) ran to completion. **OBSERVATION, NOT HYPOTHESIS (this entry's own convention): fires are hit-2 dominant** — the 09-03 daily fired on hit 2 in **6 of 7** route-attempts, the HO 548 cache-hit path. Recorded because it shaped the instrument (a single-load probe cannot see this class at all and would have read a clean prod on a day the daily failed three attempts straight), **not** as a cause claim. **What now exists:** the crawl dumps, per hit, the served bytes + settled DOM + **`err.stack`** + an insertion-tolerant SSR↔DOM alignment, gzipped under `test-results/`, uploaded on **file presence** (never `failure()` — see 08-29). SKILL, *Playwright smoke crawler*, carries the contract and the measured floor. **THE FIRST LIVE CAPTURES (dispatch `33794231177`, 2026-09-03, the N=1 attended shakedown; run concluded `success` — 2 flaky / 39 passed — with both dumps kept, which is the file-presence keying proving itself):** `[home]` and `[dashboard-v2]`, **both on hit 2**, identical signature. `home` reads `lcs(+127/-38)`, `dashboard-v2` `lcs(+121/-32)`, and **both carry `removedFromSsr` EMPTY** — so the nonzero `lcsDel` is **reordering, not deletion** (consistent with the HO 376 client-side tape shuffle). Added in the DOM on both: `span.markets-tape-item +6` · `span.markets-tape-symbol +6` · `span.markets-tape-price +12` · `div.markets-tape-set +2` · `span.markets-tape-pair-grp +12` · `span.source-tag +12` · `span.rbx-badge +2` · `span.rc-new +6` · `span.rc-chip-label +9` · `span.rc-chip-sep +9` · `span.rc-chip-body +9` · `span.rc-moved +3` · `next-route-announcer +1`. **So the divergence localizes to TWO regions — the markets tape and the HO 684 races NEW/MOVED chips — and to neither `/president` nor `committees-redirect`, which have no tape and did not fire in this run.** The non-tape fires in the ledger above therefore remain **unattributed**; do not read this capture as covering them. **THE CAVEAT THAT BOUNDS EVERY READING, stated because the added-set invites over-reading: the DOM side is the SETTLED DOM, so `addedInDom` is a SUPERSET of the hydration mismatch** — it also contains legitimate post-hydration effect-driven renders (the tape's marquee `copies` fill and the races chips' post-mount localStorage read are both expected to appear there). The SSR side is exact. Separating mismatch from legitimate post-hydration update needs a snapshot taken at fire time rather than after settle, and that is the successor's first question. **`err.stack` is captured for the first time in this arc and it names nothing:** 7 frames, every one inside the minified React chunk (`4bd1b696-…js`), no app frames — which is the measured basis for the sourcemap flip staying declined (prod serves no maps: chunk 200 / `.map` 404 / no `sourceMappingURL`). **The close criterion is unchanged and is NOT met:** a captured fire with its mismatch located is in hand; a *named cause* is not, and the non-tape routes are not covered.

- **ESLint absent — no dependency, no config** — opened HO 491 (Jul 2026); *Owner:* Corey/Code. The repo has **no ESLint dependency and no config file** of any kind, so HO 488's CI runs typecheck + build only; `lint` was deliberately omitted so CI could land clean. Wiring it is **its own arc**: install `eslint` + `eslint-config-next`, add a config, then **triage the likely wall of pre-existing violations** across the runtime surface (app/components/lib) before a `next lint` step can go green. *Close:* an ESLint config exists and a `lint` step runs green in CI (or the pre-existing findings are explicitly waived). *Blocker/why-deferred:* the unknown-size cleanup — forcing it into HO 488 would have blocked CI from landing.
- **CI action runtime — bump `actions/checkout` + `actions/setup-node` to v5 when the Node 20 deprecation bites** — opened HO 491, scope widened HO 504 (Jul 2026); *Owner:* Code. **The annotation now spans BOTH workflows — `ci.yml` (Phase 1) and `e2e-prod.yml` (HO 504); both `e2e-prod` runs (`deployment_status` + `workflow_dispatch`) carried the same one.** Every run carries a GitHub-infra **Node-20-deprecation annotation on the actions themselves** (the `checkout`/`setup-node` JS actions run on the runner's Node, forced to 24 today — unrelated to our `node-version: 20` *app* toolchain). Harmless now, but it's a **countdown, not a steady state**: when GitHub drops the Node 20 runtime, CI fails for a reason that has nothing to do with the repo. *Close:* bump both actions to `@v5`. **Symptom to recognize:** an **unexplained CI failure on an unchanged codebase** — check the action versions before debugging anything else.
- ~~**News ingest cron timing out**~~ — CLOSED HO 369. `/api/cron/news` (`0 14 * * *`) had failed 5 straight days (since Jun 21): `getCandidateBills(30)` mis-planned into a `MULTI-INDEX OR` over `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` (`is_ceremonial = 0` ≈ the whole 16.5k-row corpus) + a temp-b-tree sort, ~20s, past the HO 238 10s bound + retry-once → route abort at ~20.2s (the `cron_runs` `status=error`, `elapsed ≈ 20,200ms` signature). **Fixed** by forcing `INDEXED BY idx_bills_latest_action` on the query (commit `95d0939`) — it drives the ~30-day window off the date index in DESC order, no temp sort, no MULTI-INDEX OR. Verified: EXPLAIN shows `SEARCH bills USING INDEX idx_bills_latest_action`; a local `sync:news` inserted rows with no `[db] timeout` line. Timeout left at 10s — HO 238 stands. *(Deployed-tick confirmation tracked as a soak below.)*

- ~~**`members.party` "wrong" for Kiley (opened HO 420)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 422 — NOT A BUG.** The premise was wrong: `members.party='I'` for **K000401 Kevin Kiley (CA-03) is correct.** He **switched Republican → Independent on 2026-03-09**, confirmed by **both** Congress.gov `partyHistory` (`{I, Independent, 2026}` ahead of `{R, 2023–2026}`) **and** congress-legislators `party_affiliations` (`2026-03-09 → 2027-01-03: Independent, caucus: Republican`). He **caucuses Republican**, which is why **Voteview `party_code=200` (R)** reads him R — DW-NOMINATE's `party_code` follows **caucus, not registration** (oddities). So `sync:members` derived `I` correctly from a `partyHistory` that correctly says Independent — **no derivation bug, no override, no code shipped** (Part 1 of HO 422 halted on the corrected premise). The `ideology-coverage-419` diagnostic's `party_code`-vs-`members.party` "disagreement" is a **registration-vs-caucus semantic gap**, not an error — a structural false-positive for any independent caucusing with a party (see the QUEUED diagnostic fix below). *(This also corrects the HO 419 DONE tombstone's "Kiley a CA Republican, members wrong" wording.)*
- ~~**HO 398 race→news surface not reflected in roadmap News theme**~~ — **CLOSED HO 404.** The HO 404 audit folded it into the roadmap STATUS block as an "Also (HO 398)" note (within-theme Races overlay, powered by the already-CLOSED news layer; Races held at 99 — the incumbent-only hub v1 doesn't complete the theme). Adjudication: it **partially** discharges the QUEUED "race→news / member→news surface" — race→news (hub, incumbent) is DONE; **member→news is still unbuilt** and stays in QUEUED (see below).
- ~~**Summarize backlog + `/api/cron/summarize` reliability — one loop, cause + symptom (HO 404)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 406** (commit `82fb6bc`, live on prod). Both halves done. **Cause:** HO 383 had dropped the queue read's SQL LIMIT to fetch the full null set for the app-side stage-priority sort, so at 1,374 nulls it became a `SCAN` over all 16,623 `bills` rows and tipped the 10s DB wall (the 07-02/06-29 55s timeouts + the 06-30 20s error) — fixed with the partial index `idx_bills_summarize_queue ON bills(update_date DESC) WHERE summary IS NULL`, forced `INDEXED BY` (the statless planner mis-plans into a MULTI-INDEX OR unhinted). **Queue read is now a bounded partial-index `SCAN … idx_bills_summarize_queue`: 48ms drained (was the 16,623-row scan).** **Symptom:** serial ~1.9s/bill → a bounded **C=5** worker pool, and the cron per-tick cap raised **50→200** so the 45s budget is the wall — one real prod tick did **80** summarized (was ~23), 0 fail/timeout, `budgetStopped`, 49.6s. The one-shot `npm run summarize` drained the standing backlog **1,374 → 7** (the 7 are 15s per-bill timeouts, deferred 24h → retry next tick). `summary IS NULL` now trends down, and the **cron self-heals at cap 200 / C=5 against ~30/day inflow** (~80–120/tick clears a day's inflow with room to spare) — a manual drain is no longer the only thing that lowers the count. **Foundation's roadmap 96 hold is no longer fused on this cron:** the growing summarize backlog was the last correctness drag tying Foundation to this cron's health; with the read bounded and the cron self-healing, the score no longer depends on it. See DONE → HO 406.
- ~~**`/api/cron/weekly-report` erroring — reports 2 weeks stale (HO 404)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 407** (commit `33cc707`, live on prod, verified). Gate verdict was §C, not the suspected transient: **six `gatherReportData()` queries over the fat `bills` table `MULTI-INDEX OR`'d onto `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` and cold-aborted at the 10+10s `boundedFetch` double-timeout** (`cron_runs` #559 06-29 @ 23.8s and #287 06-15 @ 20.1s are both that DB double-timeout — NOT the Gemini 503s #82/#74 that the catch-up already absorbs). This took down the Monday cron AND the HO 285 daily catch-up's gen identically (throws → row stays missing → "regenerates daily, never lands"), which is why the net wasn't covering it. Ruled out: §A delivery (the Monday row was present-and-errored; it's a Vercel cron) and §B placement (the catch-up already runs FIRST in `/api/sync`). **Fixed hint-only** — six `INDEXED BY` hints onto EXISTING indexes (`idx_bills_stage_changed_at`, `idx_bills_introduced_date`, `idx_bills_latest_action`), **no new index, no `migrate.ts`**. Cold-verified from idle: all six 20s TIMEOUT → 156–1948ms (Q6's `json_each + GROUP BY` hint held cold — the flagged fork didn't trigger); full cold `generateWeeklyReport` 18.6s. On prod the erroring cron flipped **#559 error 23.8s → #673 success 13.5s**; Jun 22–28 (the stale week) regenerated + `/reports` revalidated. **Catch-up hardening:** its once-swallowed failure is now surfaced machine-keyably (`payload.reportCatchup.ok` + a `report-catchup-failed:`-prefixed `cron_runs.error_message`), so the two-week silent-failure gap is now status-level diagnosable. See DONE → HO 407.
- ~~**`members` ↔ congress-legislators integrity sweep (HO 404)**~~ — **CLOSED HO 411 (`db9d910`) + HO 412 (`a3a2a18`), both verified live.** It was **systemic, not the appointee churn HO 404 implied**: the read-only audit (HO 410, `scripts/diagnostic/senator-year-audit-410.ts`) found **18 of 100 current senators** carrying a `next_election_year` that disagreed with their Senate class — a wrong-anchor derivation in `sync:members` (`senateTermStart` / start-year+6), hitting long-serving elected members (Cornyn, Markey, Booker, Wyden, Murkowski, Scott, Kelly, Schiff), not just appointees. **Fixed by class-derivation (411):** `members.senate_class` is now ingested from `legislators-current.yaml` (Congress.gov carries no class) in the `sync:members` run, and the year-pair derives from it via `lib/derive-term.ts` residue math (class 1≡2, 2≡4, 3≡0 mod 6, computed from `now` so it doesn't rot at the November boundary), with a cycle-scoped self-expiring special-election list (`data/senate-special-elections.json`) for the OH/FL 2026 specials, checked ahead of the class default. The 20 drifted rows (18 + OH/FL) corrected; audit re-run **fully green** (§1=0, §2=0, §3=0, §4=0/0). **Race-table reconciled (412):** the CO card corrected in place (Bennet→Hickenlooper) via a self-healing `backfill:races` upsert scoped to `incumbent_bioguide_id` (which also healed 16 stale future-cycle cards), the `S-CA-2026`/`S-NY-2026` phantoms deleted (0 dependents), and the `fix:senate-term-years` footgun removed. **`backfill:races` now filters `is_current=1`** so a departed senator's fallback year can't recreate the two-at-2026 ambiguity. The 6 missing 2026 seats (NJ/MN/MS/NE + OH/FL) were created by 411's backfill and all carry ratings → all on `/races`. **Correction to the HO 404 flags:** the "impossible year" (Armstrong `A000383` `2031`) and "class drift" (Lankford `L000575` `2026`) were real and are fixed; the **`is_current` flag was a MIS-FLAG** — Mullin `M001190` `is_current=0` is **correct** (he resigned 2026-03-23, is absent from `legislators-current.yaml`, and Armstrong holds the seat), which HO 410 §4 proved (`is_current` clean, 0/0). The permanent regression guard is the HO 410 audit (now committed). See DONE → HO 411/412.
- ~~**`/lobbying` VOLUME sort — PROMOTED WATCH -> OPEN LOOP (HO 544; promoted HO 596)**~~ — **SHIPPED HO 597 (`82eaa24` guard split · `daf6dcc` migration · `387c56f` writer · `f2f4915` rewire · `418e80c` falsification), live on prod at `418e80c`, prod-verified. CLOSED FOR THE PATH IT NAMED; two residuals split out as their own entries below, one of them NEW and worse.** **Two defects, not one.** (1) *The one this entry named:* VOLUME ordered by a request-time `COUNT(*) FROM lda_activities GROUP BY filing_uuid`. Materialized to `lda_filings.activity_count` (+ `idx_lda_filings_activity(activity_count DESC, dt_posted DESC)`, forced via `INDEXED BY`), so the subquery and the JOIN are gone and it is a pre-ordered single-table walk. (2) *Found while fixing (1), and it is why the degradation lasted:* **the HO 440/448 guard sat INSIDE the `unstable_cache` callback, so its degraded `{items:[],…}` was the callback's RETURN VALUE and was CACHED FOR AN HOUR** — one cold miss poisoned that key for 60 minutes and the retry that would have succeeded never ran (the HO 552 class: a transient failure persisted by an exit path that records instead of deferring). The cached function now throws and the guard catches in an exported wrapper; the guard is NOT stripped. **FALSIFIED FOUR-SIDED.** *RED, co-located pdx1, shipped LIMIT 13:* the old shape breaches EVERY VOLUME variant and **does not finish in 55 SECONDS** (55002ms / 55001ms at a 55s ceiling) — worse than STEP 0 could see, because **every `20.00Xs` figure in this arc is the INSTRUMENT'S CEILING, not a measurement**: `lib/db.ts` aborts at 10s and retries once, so any query slower than 10s reports ~20.00s whether it takes 21 seconds or five minutes. STEP 0's RED is therefore **>20s (abort ceiling)**, and the true cost measured here is **>55s co-located** (itself a 55s ceiling). The data is RIGHT-CENSORED at the bound — a figure at the ceiling is a lower bound, only figures under it are durations. *GREEN, same conditions, worst of 3-5 cold runs:* volume **564ms (+9.44s margin)** · volume page-40 **1.48s (+8.52s)** · volume+linked **2.97s (+7.03s)**. *CONTROL* (`scripts/diagnostic/lda-volume-falsify-597.ts` — runs the OLD and NEW SQL against the SAME live state and diffs them, not a temporal before/after, because the corpus grows daily): the visible range **1,000 rows IDENTICAL position-for-position**, the clamp boundary identical in three forms incl. the shipped real LIMIT/OFFSET, the bill-linked branch identical, `SUM(activity_count)` == `COUNT(lda_activities)` exactly, **0 of 429 zero-activity filings visible** (rank-1000 `activity_count` = **11**, they carry 0), and the boundary key is unique so the visible set is fully determined. *DEGRADATION PATH FIRED:* matched RED/GREEN pair on one cheap cache key with one injected failure — RED served the stored empty on hits 2-3 in <600ms without touching the DB, GREEN re-queried and recovered 13 rows; the guard fired exactly ONCE in both, so the caching was the difference, not the instrument. **The comment that justified the join shape said "~295" zero-activity filings; measured 421 at STEP 0 and 429 now** — fixed in the rewire commit, since a stale number in a comment justifying a query shape is how this family gets through. (I then made the same error one line over — "~421k rows" for the scan size, conflating the zero-filing count with a row count; corrected to the measured 278,095 in `4051f9b`.) **The writer is proven end-to-end, not just by backfill:** a live `sync:lda` after the deploy ingested 63 filings / 97 activities through the new upsert, after which `SUM(activity_count)` 278,192 == `COUNT(lda_activities)` 278,192, **0 NULLs across 129,401 filings**, and 0 mismatches in the newest 80 rows. **Original WATCH text and the HO 596/597 correction history are preserved verbatim in git** (`git show d7fa0c2:docs/backlog.md` and `f190c38`) rather than re-copied here; the load-bearing part of the correction survives above, namely that "degradation, not a crash" was an accurate description of a working guard and it was the COST ESTIMATE that measurement contradicted.
- **`/lobbying` VOLUME x bill-linked x deep page still breaches — the residual HO 597 did NOT clear (opened HO 597).** *Owner:* Code. Three of four VOLUME variants clear the 10s bound with +7.03s to +9.44s of margin after the materialization. The fourth does not: **`?sort=volume&linked=1&page=40` measured 10003ms\*, 5097, 6022, 7582, 15620 co-located — worst 15.62s, margin -5.62s, breached 2 of 5 cold runs** (\* aborted at the ceiling). That is a large improvement — RED was ">55s, breaches always" — but it is **not a clear, and rounding it to one would be exactly the over-claim HO 594 had to retract.** **Reachable:** 33,800 filings are bill-linked and the pager offers 40 pages regardless of filter (the total comes from the rollup blob's corpus count, not the filtered set). **Mechanism:** the walk is pre-ordered, but the `EXISTS` is probed PER ROW, so reaching OFFSET 507 costs ~520 correlated seeks whose cold page fetches dominate — the **HO 594 page-residency residual class, not a plan shape**, so an index cannot save it either. *Named fix:* a second materialization — a `bill_linked` column on `lda_filings` + `(bill_linked, activity_count DESC, dt_posted DESC)` — which is beyond what HO 597 was scoped and priced for, so it was **filed rather than silently widened into**. *Severity is genuinely lower than it was:* after the guard split, a breach here degrades ONE request and the next re-queries, instead of poisoning the key for an hour, and the log line names the exact key. *Close:* co-located cold worst-case under 10s on that combination, margin stated as a number.
- **`/lobbying?q=` search — NARROWED, NOT CLOSED (opened HO 597, fixed HO 598).** *Owner:* Code. **The wrong answer is gone and the loop stays open.** The defect was that `?q=boeing` rendered *"No filings match"* against **78 real matching filings** — a wrong answer with a 200. Prod now returns **13 rows, count 78**. **Four things shipped.** (1) `79d1276` — a timed-out read renders *"Search timed out — try a narrower term"*, DISTINCT from the genuine *"No filings match"*; the count line renders `—` not `0`. The guard always knew the difference and never said so. (2) `1afdd02` — the pager `COUNT` is bounded to the clamp it feeds (any total past 520 was already discarded by the code asking for it); common term 58.1s → 2.25s, rendered `520+`. (3) `4fd9b48`/`e7fdf30`/`85a173f` — `lda_names` (33,620 distinct `(kind, entity_id, name)` rows + `filing_count`) and the two id indexes, maintained by the sync. (4) `e4f09ba`+`06d03dd` — **the routed read**: leg 1 resolves the term against `lda_names` (ids AND density in ONE covering scan), then **empty → return without touching `lda_filings` at all** (a proof, since `lda_names` is complete), **sparse → seek by the literal ids**, **dense → hand back to the `dt_posted` walk**. **Numbers, co-located, 8 runs per regime:** sparse median **3510ms** worst 7735ms (was 19.8s) · dense median **3378ms** worst 6491ms · zero-match median **3119ms** worst 6461ms (was **91.3s**, now typically **~400ms** on prod because it never reaches the table). **All 24 runs under the bound.** **Threshold `m* = √(k·N) ≈ 1,297` is DERIVED, not tuned** — a formula over `PAGE_SIZE` and the blob's `stats.filings`, so it re-derives as the corpus grows; verified on a STRADDLING pair (`insur` 0.86× → seek, `hospital` 1.16× → walk), both row-identical to the shipped predicate. **SHIPPED OVER A FAILED GATE, recorded as a judgment call.** Gate statistic amended to upper-tail (worst − median), since a fast outlier is evidence of safety: dense **+3.51s vs 3.11s PASS**, empty **+3.54s vs 3.34s PASS**, **sparse +2.27s vs 4.23s FAIL**. Shipped on strict dominance — the replaced path breached on BOTH `lib/db.ts` attempts at its *best* observed (19.8s), so withholding preserved a ~certain failure to avoid a rare one, and the rare one now renders honestly. **HO 594's retraction was for CLOSING on a thin margin; this improves on one.** **WHAT REMAINS, and why this entry stays open:** the residual is **leg 1** — a ~33,620-row / ~1 MB covering scan ranging **0.3–6.6s**, dominating every path. It is ~1/23 the bytes of the walk this arc started from and the cost fell roughly linearly, *and the ~2-15× run-to-run spread came along at every size*. **The residual is OWNED BY THE VARIANCE THREAD**, not by more query design — there is very little data left to remove. **The honest-timeout render is the safety net and stays regardless of what happens to the residual.** *Close:* only when a sparse search clears the bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail — i.e. when the variance question is answered. **Instruments:** `namesUnreachable` in the LDA sync payload + `chronicErr` + a greppable `[lda] names-unreachable:` (a gap makes a filing INVISIBLE to search and renders as a confident "No filings match"); `scripts/diagnostic/lda-names-maintenance-598.ts` proves the maintenance path; `scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts` re-runs equivalence. **A bug shipped inside this fix and was caught in prod in minutes — record both halves (HO 599).** `e4f09ba` rewired the page query onto the routed predicate but left the bounded `COUNT` binding **2 args against the 17-arg routed predicate**; prod degraded to an empty feed and the HO 597 log line named the failing cache key, fixed at `06d03dd`. **What let it through is a coverage gap worth its own note:** the page query and the pager `COUNT` run in **parallel inside one guard**, and the equivalence harness compared only the page query's rows — so it never executed the COUNT and reported green (oddities: *verifying one branch of a `Promise.all` is not coverage of the pair*). The equivalence harness above still has that gap; **extend it to execute the COUNT branch before it is trusted again** — that is a code change, filed here rather than done in a doc sweep. The offsetting fact, and the reason this reads as the system working: both safety nets that caught it — the honest-timeout render (`79d1276`) and the failing-key log line (HO 597 `82eaa24`) — were built in the preceding four HOs.
- ~~**Query-cost VARIANCE — the 2-15× run-to-run spread on byte-identical work; PROMOTED to its own loop (opened HO 599, the residual of HO 598).**~~ **CLOSED by the Aug 7 platform read — the DB tier is EXONERATED and the spread was CLIENT-SIDE PATH; the close paragraph is at the end of this entry, and the body below is kept because it records what was believed on the way.** *Owner:* Corey (the platform read) / Code (the re-measure). **Three HOs in a row were decided by this rather than by the query each was scoped around**, which is why it stops riding inside them: **594** (a covering index reached 140ms-1.5s steady state and the first touch after a >60s gap still cost 8.2-14.1s), **597** (`?sort=volume&linked=1&page=40` improved hugely and still breached **2 of 5** cold runs, worst 15.62s), **598** (the entire residual of `/lobbying?q=` is leg 1 — a ~33,620-row / **~1 MB** covering scan ranging **0.3-6.6s**, at ~1/23 the bytes of the walk it replaced, carrying the same spread). **The bytes model buys the median and does not touch the variance** (oddities), and there is very little data left to remove on this path — so more query design is not the lever. **The standing explanation is sub-60s page residency on a remote store, and it has never been probed on its own terms:** it was inferred from HO 594's `?order=` / `?warm=1` controls, which pinned *that* cold spike to pages that aren't there but say nothing about why byte-identical work varies 2-15× run to run. **Distinct from the read-budget WATCH below** — that asks *who is spending rows*, this asks *why identical work takes 2-15× longer on some runs*; they share a blocker, not a question. *Blocked on a platform read only Corey can run:* `turso org usage`, the plan tier, the **primary region against the Vercel function region**, and whether the DB carries replicas (the `turso` CLI is not installed in this environment and `.env` holds only the DB `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN`, no platform API token — the same block that skipped HO 582's M4b). *Close:* the spread is **explained** (a named mechanism, with those platform facts on the record) or **bounded** (a co-located worst case with the margin stated as a number) — and, concretely, a sparse `/lobbying?q=` clears the 10s bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail, which is also the close criterion of the search loop above; the two close together or neither does. *Instrument:* re-run `scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts` co-located and report **worst − median per regime**, never a range (oddities: gate on the upper tail, since a fast outlier is evidence of safety). A run that reads a materially narrower spread than 2-15× on identical work is itself the finding — it would mean the variance is conditional on something this arc never isolated.  **HO 602 — THE PLATFORM READ IS IN, AND IT KILLS TWO HYPOTHESES.** *(Turso dashboard, org `csu-j3`, read 2026-08-05; no script, not reproducible from the repo.)* Plan **Developer** ($5.99/mo, **2.5B rows read/mo**, +$1/B overage). DB primary **`aws-us-west-2`** (`libsql://cbt-csu-j3.aws-us-west-2.turso.io`), single group `default`, **no replicas, no branches**; Vercel functions pinned **`pdx1`** (`vercel.json:2`) — us-west-2. **Store and compute are in the same AWS region, so cross-region latency and replica routing are both DEAD.** **The standing candidate is Developer-tier multi-tenancy** (Turso's own launch post describes the tier as a massive multi-tenant server) — the shape that leaves a median alone while fanning an upper tail, and consistent with the sub-60s page-residency story rather than replacing it. **There is no purchasable lever:** the plan comparison table carries quota, retention and governance rows and **no performance row at any tier**, so a Scaler upgrade is an **unfalsifiable experiment** and was rejected rather than run. **This loop is therefore now committed to closing by BOUNDING, not by explaining** — a co-located worst case with the margin stated as a number — which was always the second half of its own criterion; the *explained* half is no longer reachable with the levers available. **The blocker is discharged: do not re-file this as blocked on a platform read.** *Instrument:* unchanged (`scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts`, **worst - median per regime**).  **CLOSED — THE AUG 7 PLATFORM READ, AND IT CLOSED BY *EXPLAINING*, WHICH THIS ENTRY HAD GIVEN UP ON.** *(Turso Analytics + Vercel dashboards, read 2026-08-07; no script, not reproducible from the repo — same standing as the HO 602 read.)* **(1) THE DB TIER IS EXONERATED.** Turso's own **p50 / p95 / p99 run FLAT from Jul 18 → Aug 7** — the entire window in which the 2-15× spread was observed. A store whose percentiles do not move cannot be the source of a 2-15× fan on the caller's clock, so **Developer-tier multi-tenancy — the standing candidate this entry ADOPTED at HO 602 — is dead**, alongside the cross-region and replica hypotheses HO 602 had already killed. **(2) THE SPREAD WAS CLIENT-SIDE PATH, and the instrument was measuring itself.** Every run that produced the 2-15× figures fired **from the Windows box** — home route plus a fresh per-run TLS handshake to a US region — a cost path **prod does not have and never had**, and one that is **structurally invisible to the store's percentiles because it is spent before the query arrives**. *The numbers that opened this loop were measurements of the LINK, attributed to the QUERY.* **(3) The Jul 8-14 latency band predates tracking** — visible at the left edge, no instrumentation behind it, **unexplained and deliberately not chased**; recorded so its absence from this paragraph is not read as absence from the chart. **(4) TOPOLOGY, HEADER-VERIFIED rather than config-inferred:** `curl -sI …/api/version` returns `X-Vercel-Id: sfo1::pdx1::…`, so the **executing function region is `pdx1`** (`vercel.json:2`) against the Turso primary **`aws-us-west-2`** in the DB hostname — **HO 602's co-location claim STANDS and is now measured rather than inferred.** Fluid on, Standard CPU. **The near-miss is recorded because it is the lesson:** this session's dashboard read reported the function region as **`iad1` (Overridden)** — which would have put compute cross-country from its store and made HO 602's claim FALSE — and it was **caught by repo cross-check before it landed**; the Overridden badge marks the **INERT dashboard default**, config-as-code wins. ***A dashboard panel is a claim; `vercel.json` and a hostname are the record; a response header beats both.*** **(5) STANDING RULE:** do **not** reopen without new **PROD-SIDE** evidence — a co-located run, a runtime log, a response header. **A slow diagnostic run from the dev box is now a KNOWN NON-SIGNAL.** **AND THE SEARCH LOOP ABOVE DOES *NOT* CLOSE WITH IT, though this entry said the two close together or neither does.** That clause is honoured by naming what actually changed: `/lobbying?q=`'s criterion is **a co-located sparse run clearing the bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail**, and **no such run has been made** — what closes here is only that its residual is **no longer owned by an unexplained variance thread**, and that the re-measure it still needs must be **prod-side/co-located**, because a dev-box re-run cannot clear a bound it was never measuring. *Criterion, honestly scored:* the entry had committed to closing by **bounding**, judging the *explained* half unreachable with the levers available — **it closed by explaining anyway, from a lever it never considered: measuring the instrument's own path instead of the query's.**
- **`getBillLobbying`'s cost note is undated and predates the table's current cost (opened HO 598).** *Owner:* Code. **Not urgent, and filed rather than fixed because the SHAPE is sound.** `lib/queries.ts:3249` records the live below-threshold branch as *"measured 2.3s, 7.7s under the 10s cap"* — with **no corpus size and no date**, which is the exact SKILL authoring rule this arc added one HO earlier, catching one of its own. HO 598 measured that anything WALKING `lda_filings` cold is now **30-90s**, so an undated sub-3s figure on that table cannot be assumed current. **Why it is probably still fine:** this path never walks — it is an indexed `lda_activity_bills.bill_id` seek, then PK-`IN` seeks against `lda_filings` chunked at `HYDRATE_CHUNK=400`, and the `lda_bill_drill` blob covers every bill at **>= 150 distinct filings**, so the live branch is bounded at **<= 149 filings**. Bounded seeks, not a scan. It is also already `try/catch -> null`, so a breach hides the section rather than 500ing the bill page — which is the same silent-degradation shape HO 597 fixed elsewhere, and the reason this is worth a WATCH rather than a shrug. *Close:* re-measure the worst below-threshold bill co-located and restamp the comment WITH its corpus size and date, or promote if it breaches.

(Ruling: *"if no age cell in the filled table is ≥ 12 months, strike it; the two api.data.gov keys get one backlog calendar line instead, owner Corey, cadence annual."*) **HO 680 — start from the scripts, not from memory.** `CRON_SECRET` rotates via `scripts/diagnostic/rotate-cron-secret-680.ts` (tracked); the api.data.gov keys go in **by pipe from `.env`** (`grep '^NAME=' .env | cut -d= -f2- | tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9' | vercel env add NAME production --force --yes`) — **`tr -cd` not `tr -d '\r\n'`, because the latter leaves a trailing space and Vercel stores it.** **PREVIEW LEGS ARE DASHBOARD-SIDE, OWNER TYPES: the CLI cannot do them non-interactively without `--value`, which would put the secret in a command line.** And the read-back is `vercel env ls <env> --format json` → `updatedAt`, never the human-readable `created` column.

- **New (HO 690) · the `bxp` container-query threshold went stale exactly as its own entry predicted (P3).** *Owner:* Code. The dashboard-redesign entry's HO 623 note (e) states the rule — *"a container-query threshold is derived from the container it was tuned against, so widening the container obliges you to re-derive the threshold or it silently changes which viewports collapse"* — and then **HO 629 widened the container (`minmax(480px, 31%)` → `minmax(480px, 45%)`) without re-deriving it.** Measured at HO 690, off the DOM on a production build, with the panel's own `.bxp` width rather than the viewport: `/bills` **1140px @1440** and **2260px @2560**; `/` **598px @1440** and **1102px @2560**. All four are past `@container bxp (max-width: 520px)`, so **all four render WIDE** and the CSS comment's *"520 keeps 1440 stacked"* (`app/globals.css`, the `.bxp` block) is false. **The stacked branch is now reachable only at a ~430 viewport** (394px on `/bills`, 378px on `/`), and since `.dash-page` only becomes two-column at ≥1400 while 0.45 × 1400 already exceeds the threshold, there is **no desktop width at which `/` stacks** — the branch the 520 was chosen to preserve. *Why it is P3 and not P2:* wide is a legitimate render at 598px and nothing is broken on screen; what is broken is that a documented derivation no longer holds and the next person to tune this will read a false premise. *Close (must be able to fail):* either re-derive the threshold against the 45% column and state the boundary viewport it now sets, **or** record a keep reason — and in both cases correct the `.bxp` comment, checked by measuring the panel width at the stated boundary either side and showing the branch flips there. `scripts/diagnostic/chrome-shots-690.ts --section portrait` already prints panel width + `bxp-body` columns per capture, so the measurement is one run, not a build.
- **New (HO 690) · `/members` scrolls horizontally at 430 by 141px (P3, pre-existing, NOT introduced by 690).** *Owner:* Code. Measured on a production build while checking the HO 682 invariant across `/`, `/bills`, `/members`, `/electoral` at 1440 / 2560 / 430: every other cell reads **over 0**, and `/members` @430 reads **scrollWidth 571 vs clientWidth 430**. **Attributed, not guessed:** the elements past the right edge are `.mc-r-num` (the roster row's RATE / ENACT / BILLS numerals, ~52–64px each, one pair per row) and one `.pol-time-collapsed-hint` at 286px wide — **no nav element appears in the over-edge set**, which is what rules HO 690's negative `margin-inline` (12px, both edges) out as the cause. **It is invisible to every standing gate by construction:** `fit-finish.spec.ts` asserts the doc-scroll invariant at **1440 and 2560** only, the layout audit's **M0 is wide-viewport only** (0 of 26 routes at 2560), and the smoke crawl's overflow alarm rides the crawl's **1280** viewport — so a 430-only overflow is outside all three. This is the `<700` thread SKILL already carries as deliberately un-specced, now with a number and a named element rather than a general note. *Close:* either give the roster row a narrow-width treatment (the `.mc-*` two-pane stacks at 720 but the numeral columns do not), or state a keep reason and widen one gate to 430 so the figure stops being invisible.

## QUEUED

- **New (HO 690) · P3 — the wrapped-row 1px residue has a THIRD escape, measured and deliberately not applied.** *Owner:* Code, gated on **Corey**. The shipped N2 tab strip draws its hairline with `.pnav-item + .pnav-item { border-left: 1px solid var(--border-soft) }`, and a WRAPPED row's first tab is still an adjacent sibling — so it draws that border with nothing to its left and its label lands **1px** right of the row above (`/` at 1440, the only wrapping case). The CSS comment names two escapes and says both relocate the same pixel; **there is a third, and unlike those two it removes it**: `box-shadow: inset 1px 0 0 var(--border-soft)` paints the hairline inside the padding box and takes no layout space. **Measured at HO 690 on a production build via `scripts/diagnostic/nav-geometry-690.ts`, not predicted:** row-2 vs row-1 **1px → 0px**; masthead alignment **0px** and active-vs-inactive width **0px**, both unchanged; the group divider stays **21px** (it keeps its own border + margins, so the reset there becomes `box-shadow: none`). **Two costs, and the first is why this is Corey's and not Code's:** label-to-label falls **13px → 12px**, one under the figure `docs/design/mock-690-chrome.html` § 02 was ruled at (*"2. 6px"*, i.e. 6 + rule + 6) — so applying it silently would move a ruled number; and each tab being 1px narrower **moves the wrap point**, so `/` at 1440 fits nine items on row 1 instead of eight (captured either way: `docs/handoffs/690-artifacts/nav-home-1440-normal.png` vs `…/optionB/nav-home-1440-normal.png`, both local-only). **What it does NOT buy, so nobody re-discovers it as a win:** the stray hairline at the start of a wrapped row is present in BOTH — that artifact is the sibling relationship, not the paint method — so the whole trade is 13px-with-a-1px-offset versus 12px-aligned. *Close (must be able to fail):* **apply only under a re-ruling of the label-to-label figure** — a verdict quoted with provenance, the mock's § 02 caption amended to the new number — then verify with `nav-geometry-690.ts`, which must read 12px uniform, row-2 delta 0px, divider 21px and masthead 0px on `/` and `/bills` at 1440 and 2560; **or** record a keep reason and strike. Absent either, the border ships as ruled and this line stays open. **Corey was offered the letter at the HO 690 close and ruled NEITHER (2026-09-03) — file it, do not apply it** — which is the entry's own provenance and the reason its close criterion is a re-ruling rather than a build.

- **New (HO 684) · FILED — the rating-source short-label map now exists in FOUR places, and none of them is the source of truth.** *Owner:* Code, low. `rating_history.source` / `race_ratings.source` store **snake_case** (`cook` / `sabato` / `inside_elections`), and four separate sites turn that into something a human reads: `lib/enums.ts:214` `RACE_RATING_SOURCES` (full names — "Cook Political Report") · `components/RatingChip.tsx:19-28` `sourceLabel` (title case — "Inside Elections") · `components/RaceCard.tsx:180-184` the `raterPills` array (short caps — `COOK` / `SABATO` / `IE`, an inline literal, not a constant) · and now `components/RaceMovedIndicator.tsx` `SOURCE_SHORT` (the same short caps, for the MOVED chip). **HO 684 added the fourth deliberately and did not extract**, on the ruling that the HO was the badge referent and not a label-map refactor — this line is that decision made visible rather than left in a comment. *Two things to carry:* the four are **not redundant** (three genuinely different registers — full, title, short — for three surfaces), so the fix is a single keyed map with three fields, **not** one string; and there is no failing test today, so this is tidiness, not a defect. *Close:* one source-of-truth map consumed by all four, or a tombstone ruling the duplication acceptable.

- **New (HO 683) · FILED — the committees rollup is the largest total burner on the first trustworthy ledger.** *Owner:* Code. `getCommitteesIndex`'s two LEFT JOIN subqueries (per-committee `member_count` + 30-day `recent_count`) read **~247k rows per 6h** — 30,900 avg × 8 executions — the top line on the 2026-09-02 6h window and roughly 10% of the extrapolated ~2.5M/day. It is the **first legitimate optimization candidate under the HO 683 narrowing** (probes may be AIMED at attributed lines), and it is **filed, not chased**. Two constraints on whoever picks it up. **The figure is attribution, not sizing:** per the same narrowing, no Turso-window number is ground truth until `:427` reconciles, so a probe **re-measures on its own table** before proposing anything — and `EXPLAIN` prices the plan, not the scan, so that measurement is a timed, co-located, WORST-CASE reading rather than a median (SKILL, Query helpers). **And the surface is not `/committees`:** that route redirects to `/members`, where this query backs the LEFT RAIL on every render, so any change is a `/members` change and inherits its consumers. Any probe is its own HO.

- ~~**New (HO 682) · the document-overflow comparison in `smoke.spec.ts`'s PROD crawl — filed rather than built, because it is a policy call.** *Owner:* **Corey**, to want or strike. HO 682 put `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` in `fit-finish.spec.ts` (manual, local, 1440 + 2560, both tab states) and gave `layout-audit-606.ts` an **M0** gate on the pair it already collected. The third home was **declined at the HALT, not overlooked**: `smoke.spec.ts` runs unattended against prod on every `deployment_status` and daily, so the same one-line comparison there would make a **data-armed** defect **deploy-blocking** — a long committee title arriving overnight would red the next deploy of unrelated work, with no code change to blame and nothing a developer can fix except the CSS class this HO already fixed. That is a real thing to want (it is the only place the check runs without a human) and a real thing to refuse (it couples the deploy gate to the corpus), which is why it is yours. Two further inputs: smoke crawls at the `Desktop Chrome` **1280** viewport, so 1440/2560 means new contexts against the standing **74-document-request** WATCH; and it lands inside the `:50` `pageErr` WATCH context, where the crawl's failure signal is already carrying an open question. *Close:* wanted — add the comparison and say at which viewport(s); struck — tombstone this line, the fit-finish + M0 pair being the accepted coverage.~~ — **RULED AND BUILT AT HO 683: the blocking form is STRUCK, the alarm form SHIPPED.** The check now rides `smoke.spec.ts` at its existing **1280** viewport for **zero new prod requests**, **armed only on `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch`** — a `deployment_status` run does no reads, so a red deploy still means "your change broke it" — and a finding files a **GitHub issue**, which is a phone ping. **"1280 suffices" is now MEASURED, not argued:** HO 682's set did not include 1280, so the viewport-independence claim was an inference; HO 683 fired the armed path against the pre-682 build and read **`home`, scrollWidth 4598 vs clientWidth 1280, over 3318**. **Accepted limits, both in their measured form.** (1) **Cancellation is wider than `:448` describes** — concurrency is evaluated BEFORE the job `if`, so a `deployment_status` event the job goes on to SKIP (a Preview deploy) still cancels the in-flight run first. The window is therefore **any `deployment_status` event during the crawl's ~3 minutes**, not a production deploy burst; demonstrated live at HO 683 (dispatch `33673317612` cancelled by a skipped Preview run, re-dispatched clean as `33673524519`). The fix rides `:448`, not here. (2) A missed day: worst-case latency is the 24h schedule. — **HO 685: LIMIT (1) IS CLOSED (`88d776f`); limit (2) is the sole survivor.** The concurrency group is now keyed on the job `if`'s three discriminators, so a skipped run can never cancel a working crawl and a cancelled armed run is no longer a way for this alarm to miss a day. It cost one before it closed: the **2026-09-02 armed daily** (`33667308181`) was killed 2m46s in by a skipped Preview run, so that day filed nothing — the limit stated here, realized. **Two repairs to this line's own text, both recorded rather than silently fixed.** The cross-reference `:448` is wrong and was wrong **when written, not drifted** — checked against HO 683's own commit `960708b`, where `:448` was the `/amendments` blurb entry; the concurrency WATCH was never at that line. It is named here as **the `e2e-prod.yml` global concurrency group WATCH**, deliberately not by number, since a line number is exactly what just failed. And "cancelled 47s in" measures the gap between the two runs' creation timestamps; the job ran **89s**. Limit (2) is unchanged and unaddressed: a missed day still costs up to the 24h schedule interval.

Shape if wanted: extend the Kalshi discovery in `fetchKalshi` to select the **second** soonest-open event in the series (it already sorts them by date and takes `dated[0]`), and the Polymarket side to take the second `fed-decision-in-*` by end date. The HO 681 collision flag would then be the thing that proves the two horizons stay distinct, which is a good reason to build it on top of that HO rather than before it. *Close:* Corey rules want or strike.

The data says "Identical bill", not "companion" (opened HO 674)**~~ — **ANSWERED BY COREY 2026-08-19 AND BUILT AT HO 675 (`5caa4ec`).** The ruling is **(b), tightened**: promote `Identical bill` **only where the chamber differs**, match it **case-insensitively by containment and never by equality**, and treat the promoted element as **a bordered block of one to n** rather than "the companion". Same-chamber identicals stay in the list below — promoted nowhere, dropped nowhere (217 bills carry one). **Close criterion met, and it was the criterion that could fail:** the UI HO was writable and is written. **Three of this entry's own figures were wrong and are corrected here rather than left standing.** (i) The **69** multi-identical bills is an artefact of `= 'Identical bill'` — the very equality this ruling forbids; under the ruled predicate it is **91**. (ii) Counted by what the UI actually draws, **distinct cross-chamber targets**, only **25 of 3,060 bills (0.82%)** promote more than one, so the block is a single row **99.2%** of the time and the "no unique referent" worry is real but rare — the 47 you get counting ROWS is HO 674's "counts relationship entries, not bills" trap firing one layer up, which is twice in two HOs, so the rule is **count what the UI draws, not what the table holds**. (iii) The cross-chamber share is **3,107 of 3,478 rows (89.33%)** under the ruled predicate, not 3,083 of 3,454. **`Identical Bill (Became Law)` was decided too:** the 24 rows are matched, and the promoted line reads "· identical, became law" where they apply. *The original entry's text follows, preserved:* **OPEN QUESTION FOR COREY — what should HO 673's ruling 3 promote?** *Owner:* Corey, then the UI HO. Ruling 3 as approved says *the companion bill is promoted into its own bordered line above the rest of the related list*. **There is no "Companion" relationship type.** The corpus-wide value set, now measured across all 10,254 stored rows, is **six** values: `Related bill` 6,187 · **`Identical bill` 3,454** · `Procedurally related` 375 · `Public law contains the text` 114 · `Contained in public law` 100 · **`Identical Bill (Became Law)` 24**. The nearest thing to a companion is `Identical bill`, and the numbers that decide the ruling are these: it covers **3,237 bills**, **89.3% of its rows are cross-chamber** (3,083 of 3,454; 371 are same-chamber), and **69 bills carry MORE THAN ONE `Identical bill`** — so "promote *the* companion" has no unique referent on those. The choice, stated as the choice it is: **(a)** promote `Identical bill` as-is and accept that 10.7% of promotions are same-chamber and 69 bills promote an arbitrary one of several; **(b)** promote `Identical bill` **only where the chamber differs**, which is the strictest reading of "companion" and covers 3,083 rows, leaving same-chamber identicals in the ordinary list; **(c)** something else. **Whichever is chosen must also decide `Identical Bill (Became Law)`** — 24 rows, and note the **capital B**, so a `WHERE relationship_type = 'Identical bill'` silently excludes them (oddities). *Close:* the UI HO cannot be written until this is answered; it is not a Code decision and HO 674 deliberately did not make it.
