- ~~**Nothing refreshes the rosters — there is no roster sync at all, and the dataset starts decaying the day it lands (opened HO 675)**~~ — **SHIPPED AND CLOSED at HO 676 (`2779129`).** `/api/cron/bill-rosters` (`40 */3`, maxDuration 300, a 240s carve and a 120-bill cap) runs `refreshBillRosters` from `lib/bill-rosters-refresh.ts` against a new `bill_roster_state` watermark; `npm run refresh:bill-rosters` is the manual entry, read-only until `--write`. **All three legs are answered and TWO OF THE THREE WERE MIS-SPECIFIED BY THIS ENTRY — recorded here rather than quietly reinterpreted, because a close criterion that names the wrong quantity is the same defect as one that cannot become false.** **Leg 1 — named at close, and not chosen in advance: `119-hr-9641`.** It arrived rosterless *during* HO 676 (the corpus grew 17,728 → 17,729 between STEP 0 and the migration), carried `cosponsor_count` 6 against an empty roster, and the trigger selected it with nobody picking it out; it now reads **6 roster rows** (`ingested_at 2026-08-21T00:28:40.294Z`). **But its introduction is 2026-07-13, BEFORE the backfill** — what postdates the backfill is its INGESTION (`update_date 2026-08-20T18:50:39Z`, against the backfill's last write `2026-08-20T01:02:19.943Z`). This entry said *introduced after*; the class that actually matters is **ingested after**, because `/api/sync` reaches a bill weeks after it is introduced — `119-hres-1400` was introduced 2026-06-30 and ingested 08-19. The corrected criterion is the one the leg demonstrates. **Leg 2 — clean.** `revalidateTag("bill-rosters")` has exactly one caller, `app/api/cron/bill-rosters/route.ts:89`, guarded on `changedBills > 0`, and it was seen firing **both ways** against an untagged `/api/version` control: a tick with 40 changed → panel 96.9ms / control 4.1ms; a tick with 0 changed (120 bills stamped, 240 requests spent) → panel 7.7ms / control 4.3ms; the authorised tick with 4 changed → panel 47.6ms / control 5.4ms. **Leg 3 — answered by re-measuring, and by naming why the number barely moves.** This entry demanded the disagreement move off **1,910 of 13,929**; it reads **1,905 of 13,931**. The criterion was measuring the wrong quantity: that fraction is `:81`'s stale COLUMN, not roster freshness, and HO 676 is scoped out of repairing it — so it cannot move much by construction, and a version of this HO that *did* move it would have been out of scope. The instruments that answer the question the leg was reaching for: bills with `cosponsor_count > 0` and **zero roster rows, 5 → 0**, and the priority band **draining to 0** after every tick. Decomposed at close: roster-ahead **1,901** · count-ahead **4** · no-roster **0** · agree **12,026**, of 13,931 carrying a count. **The finding, which outlives the fix:** HO 674 STEP 0.4's `active roster <> cosponsor_count` selects 1,910 bills of which **1,906 are ones where the ROSTER is the fresher side** — it never clears, because the stale half is a column this HO may not correct. Adding DIRECTION took it to 10 + 73; the STEP 3 slice then caught direction alone re-firing forever on **5 bills whose comparand is stale ABOVE the truth** (four where a withdrawal dropped `pagination.count` while the stored column kept the old value, one where `$.relatedBills.count` reads 1 against an API returning 0), so MOVEMENT (`count <> count_at_check`) was added and falsified on the same rows, **5 → 0**. **A trigger compared against a value you are not allowed to correct must fire on that value MOVING, not on it disagreeing with you.** *Residual, stated so it is not read as complete:* **510 of 17,729** bills were re-checked in the shipping session; a full sweep cycle is **~18.5 days** at 8 ticks/day × 120 bills (~1,920 requests/day, 2 per bill — the refresh always fetches both rosters, because gating the related fetch on a possibly-stale count would reintroduce the blindness the sweep exists to backstop). The cron's first AUTONOMOUS fire on prod happens after this lands. *The original entry's text follows, preserved:* **Nothing refreshes the rosters — there is no roster sync at all, and the dataset starts decaying the day it lands (opened HO 675).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/backfill-bill-rosters.ts` is the **only writer to `bill_cosponsors` and `bill_related_bills`, and no cron calls it** — `vercel.json`'s cron list has no roster path, and SKILL records the same fact at the panel-route entry. Meanwhile `/api/sync` runs `0 */6 * * *` (**4×/day**) and adds bills. **Two consequences, and the second is the one that keeps growing:** a bill introduced from now on renders an empty Cosponsors row and no related-bills block **permanently**, and every existing roster is frozen at its backfill reading while cosponsors keep accruing — which is the same mechanism as the `cosponsor_count` entry below, now pointed at the table that was supposed to be the fix. **The decay is measured, not modelled, and it was already measurable hours after the backfill landed:** the backfill's last write is `2026-08-20T01:02:19.943Z`, and **5 bills carry `cosponsor_count > 0` with ZERO roster rows** (of 13,929 bills carrying a count, corpus 17,728). One of them is unambiguous — **`119-s-5354`, count 6, upserted `2026-08-20T03:28:43Z`**, i.e. after the backfill's last write, so a bill has already arrived with no roster. The other four (`119-s-5319` · `119-s-5311` · `119-s-5310` · `119-hres-1400`) are **not characterised** — backfill miss or the same class — and telling them apart is the sync HO's first question, not a claim made here. **The trigger does not need designing: HO 674's STEP 0.4 already worked it out** — **active roster size** (`sponsorship_withdrawn_date IS NULL`) **against `bills.cosponsor_count`**, which is a free integrity check because both are already stored. **The withdrawal trap is load-bearing on it:** `pagination.count` excludes withdrawals while the list includes them, so a comparison that does not filter to active disagrees on exactly the bills that have one (77 rows corpus-wide). Re-measured today under that filter: **1,910 bills disagree** (HO 674 read 1,915 — the figure moves, which is why the close criterion below re-measures rather than cites). **Three things already known that a build must not re-derive:** (i) the resume defect filed below is directly in this cron's path — coverage must be a **watermark or a per-bill stamp, never row presence**, or a scheduled refresh re-fetches every empty roster on every tick (~3,800 requests, ~16% of the job); (ii) the rate limit is **20,000 requests/hr** and the full cold backfill spent **24,429** over ~4h, so a trigger-keyed refresh is a small fraction of that — but **price it, do not inherit the number**; (iii) **`bill-rosters` is the tag this cron is supposed to flush** — nothing calls `revalidateTag("bill-rosters")` today, so panel entries live to their 3600s TTL and a refresh would be invisible for up to an hour. *Close (must be able to fail, and each leg can):* a bill whose introduction **postdates the backfill** — named at close time, not chosen in advance — reads a populated cosponsor roster from the local DB **without anyone running a script by hand**; `revalidateTag("bill-rosters")` has a caller; and the active-roster-vs-`cosponsor_count` disagreement is **re-measured after a full refresh cycle and stated as a fraction**, which must move off 1,910/13,929 or the refresh is not doing what it claims.
- ~~**`bills.cosponsor_count` is systematically stale — 13.8% of bills, and always low (opened HO 674)**~~ — **CLOSED at HO 677 (`061f96d`) by giving the column an OWNER, which is the question the entry was really asking.** Of its three acceptable outcomes it took the first: the count is **derived**, written by `lib/bill-rosters-refresh.ts` from the same `pagination.count` the HO 676 watermark caches, so the column and `bill_roster_state.active_count` cannot drift apart by construction. **`/api/sync` no longer writes it** — omitted from both the INSERT list and the `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET`, with the omission stated there (the HO 459 rule, which exists because an unexplained omission gets helpfully re-added). **The close criterion, re-measured as it demanded and stated as a fraction:** disagreement **1,905 of 13,931 → 0 of 13,931**, and NULL-despite-a-roster **108 → 0**. A one-shot reconcile shipped in the same commit because ownership alone cannot move a number when only 510 of 17,729 bills have been refresh-checked — 2,013 rows, pure DB, zero API calls: 1,901 corrected up, **4 corrected DOWN** (`119-hr-1238` 2→1, `119-s-1326` 8→7, `119-s-2904` 24→23, `119-s-3966` 21→20 — right, and a decrease, named in advance so it surprises nobody), and 108 NULL→value. **THIS ENTRY'S OWN MECHANISM WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART.** It says the column drifts because the sync cannot keep it correct; measured at HO 677, the sync's write was never *incorrect* — it writes the LIVE detail-endpoint count at upsert time, and **136 of 136 bills upserted within a day agreed with their roster, 501 of 501 within a week, while 0 of the 1,905 disagreeing bills had been upserted in that window** (oldest `update_date` 2025-02-28). The defect was **frequency, not correctness-at-write** — so the fix that mattered was not a better writer but a **single owner**, which is precisely what this entry lacked. **The NULL rule, ruled explicitly rather than left implicit:** write `active_count` when > 0, **NULL when 0**. The first reason is a measurement — **no bill in this corpus has ever stored `0`** (NULL 3,798 · `=0` 0 · `>0` 13,931), so NULL already IS the zero, and writing `0` would invent a third state and flip the panel's `cosponsor_count != null` gate true on **3,690 bills**, rendering an empty COSPONSORS row nobody asked for. NULL's meaning therefore changes from *the API omitted the key* to *the refresh saw zero active cosponsors*, with the ambiguity it used to carry now resolved in the DATA MODEL instead: `active_count = 0` beside a `checked_at` is a **verified** zero. Held under a real write — still 0 rows storing `0` afterwards. **What this does NOT claim, and the entry claims no more:** the column is now **bounded-stale, not correct.** Its freshness is each bill's last check, and at close **97.1% of state rows still carried the HO 676 SEED stamp** rather than a refresh check — so these are corrections to **2026-08-19/20 truth, not to now**, bounded going forward by the ~18.5-day sweep. Better than unbounded, which is what the column had. *Verified on screen, against HO 675's own captures as the before:* `119-hr-1628` 202/252 → **252/252**, `119-hr-8591` 10/11 → **11/11**, eight agreeing bills unmoved, four genuine-zero bills still hiding the row, and two of the 108 (`119-hr-8411`, `119-hres-1334`) now rendering the row **and their faces**, which they had hidden entirely. *The original entry's text follows, preserved:* **`bills.cosponsor_count` is systematically stale — 13.8% of bills, and always low (opened HO 674).** *Owner:* Code. Now that the roster exists the two can be compared corpus-wide, and they disagree on **1,915 of 13,922 bills (13.8%)**. The direction is the finding: **1,911 have a roster larger than the stored count and only 4 smaller**, with a summed net gap of **+4,887 cosponsors** the column does not know about. *(A 40-bill pre-backfill sample read 30%; the corpus reads 13.8%, so that sample over-estimated by ~2× and the corpus figure supersedes it.)* **Mechanism:** `cosponsor_count` is refreshed only when a bill is re-upserted (`lib/sync.ts:215`), and cosponsors accrue without reliably moving the bill's `updateDate`, so no upsert fires and the count silently drifts behind — every disagreeing bill sampled was last updated in May–June against an August reading. **The conclusion, and it is the point of the entry: once the rosters exist, the roster IS the better source, and `cosponsor_count` becomes a cache of it that nothing refreshes.** HO 674 deliberately did not touch the column (doubling the blast radius of an ingestion HO), and its own cross-check compares the **active** subset only — `pagination.count` excludes withdrawals while the roster stores them. **IT IS NOW ON SCREEN, which is a second and independent reason to take this entry (HO 675).** The expand panel renders the stale column as its cosponsor total and the roster as its party-group headers, so on **1,919 of 13,926 bills (13.78%)** the panel shows a total that does not equal the sum of the groups printed two lines below it — net **+4,880**, delta **median +1 · p75 +3 · p95 +8 · max +50**, with **56 bills off by more than 10**. Ruled visible at HO 675 rather than repaired there: sourcing the total from the roster is exactly the quiet fix that HO would have had to make silently, and the column is this entry's to close. **Captured, not asserted** — `119-hr-8591` (10 vs 11) and `119-hr-1628` (202 vs 252) are in the HO 675 artefacts, and the tail case reads as mildly stale rather than obviously broken because the total is a 12.5px sans line and the group counts are 9.5px mono annotations, so only a reader who does the arithmetic sees it. That is a reason to fix it, not a reason to relax. *Close (three acceptable outcomes, and a reason is one of them):* either the count is **derived** from `bill_cosponsors` (a view, a trigger, or a recompute in the sync), or it is **dropped** and its six readers repointed at the roster, or a **reason is recorded** for keeping a column known to drift — with, in every case, the disagreement rate re-measured afterwards and stated as a fraction. **AMENDED HO 676 — THE PREMISE NOW HOLDS, which is what makes this entry actionable rather than aspirational.** This entry's conclusion — *once the rosters exist, the roster IS the better source, and `cosponsor_count` becomes a cache of it that nothing refreshes* — was true about the roster and false about the refresh until HO 676 shipped one (`2779129`, backlog `:80` struck). A roster that nothing maintained was not a better source than a stale column; it was a differently-stale one. It is now maintained on a `40 */3` cron. **Two corrections to the figures above, both measured at HO 676:** the disagreement reads **1,905 of 13,931** (from 1,915 of 13,922 at HO 674 and 1,919 of 13,926 at HO 675 — it drifts, so treat every one of these as a reading and re-measure), and the **4 bills where the roster is SMALLER than the count are not noise** — they are the column stale ABOVE the truth after a cosponsor withdrew, which HO 676 had to build a trigger clause around because a refresh can lower the roster toward reality but can never raise a wrong count to meet it. That direction is a second drift mechanism this entry's *always low* framing does not cover, and whichever of the three outcomes closes this entry has to handle it. 
- **The roster backfill's resume cannot mark an empty roster as done (opened HO 674).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/backfill-bill-rosters.ts --resume` reads coverage as **row presence** (`SELECT DISTINCT bill_id FROM …`), so a bill with a genuinely empty roster is indistinguishable from one never processed. Measured on the STEP 2 slice: a resumed run over the same 50 bills skipped 45 and re-fetched **5 — exactly the empty-roster ones** — spending 5 requests against the 80 a cold run spends (that 80 is the control proving the counter moves). **At corpus scale that is a ~3,800-request tax on every resumed run, ~16% of the ~24,400-request job.** Consequence, stated plainly because it changes how the script should be used: **the full backfill should run to completion in one pass**, and a resume after a mid-run failure will re-fetch every empty roster it already knows about. Not fixed at HO 674 — the run completed, so the cost was never paid. *Close:* coverage becomes a watermark rather than a row count — a per-bill "rosters fetched at" stamp, or a sentinel row — such that a resumed run over an already-complete corpus spends **0** requests, demonstrated against a control run that spends non-zero.
- ~~**Cosponsor roster + related-bill identities — one ingestion domain, and rulings 2 and 3 of HO 673 are blocked on it (opened HO 673)**~~ — **SHIPPED AND CLOSED at HO 674.** Both tables exist and are populated: **`bill_cosponsors` 168,610 rows across 14,033 bills** (77 withdrawn, 90,746 original) and **`bill_related_bills` 10,254 rows across 6,768 bills** (6,934 distinct related ids). Backfill spent **24,429 API requests** against a STEP 0 estimate of 24,505 — **0.3% off** — over ~4h wall clock, with 2 transient errors both recovered on an explicit retry. **Close criterion met, and it was written so it could fail:** `119-hr-452` reads **300 cosponsor rows, 299 active and 1 withdrawn** (`C001129`, sponsored 2025-02-06, withdrawn 2025-02-11) from the local DB with no network call, and `119-hr-7567` reads **92 relationship rows across 91 distinct related bills** the same way. Integrity at full scale: **cosponsor rows resolve to `members.bioguide_id` at 100.00%** (168,610/168,610 — the party-split UI will resolve every row), related rows resolve to `bills.id` at **99.20%**, and the 82 that do not are their own filed entry below rather than an accepted loss. The original entry's text follows, preserved: **Cosponsor roster + related-bill identities — one ingestion domain, and rulings 2 and 3 of HO 673 are blocked on it (opened HO 673).** *Owner:* Code. Both are the **same shape of gap**, which is why this is one entry and not two: Congress.gov's bill *detail* endpoint returns each as a `{count, url}` stub and never a list, so the corpus has counts and no identities. **Cosponsors:** `bills.cosponsor_count` (INTEGER, `scripts/migrate.ts:1389`) populated from `$.cosponsors.count`; `lib/sync.ts:42` types the API as `cosponsors?: { count?: number }`; `scripts/backfill-cosponsors.ts` is pure SQL over `raw_json` with no API call; **none of the 50 `CREATE TABLE`s is a roster** (control: the `amendments` table grep returns 1, so the instrument discriminates). **Related bills:** zero `relatedBills`/`related_bill` references in tracked code — every "companion" hit is the unrelated *news-row* companion pill — and measured on prod, `$.relatedBills` is present on **6,773 of 17,722 bills**, always `{count, url}`, never a list (control: `$.cosponsors.count` present on 13,922, so the JSON path instrument works). The two sub-resources are `…/bill/{congress}/{type}/{number}/cosponsors` and `…/relatedbills`. **The party split additionally needs a party per cosponsor**, which resolves through the existing `members` join at no new column — but there are no rows to resolve. **What this blocks:** HO 673's ruling 2 (six cosponsor faces, party-split, unused shares redistributed, names on hover) and ruling 3 (related bills format R2, companion promoted into its own bordered line). Their ruling record is `docs/design/mock-673-sponsor-cosponsor.html` and `docs/design/mock-673-related-bills.html`, both committed at HO 673 and both **self-declaring their data as stand-ins**. Nothing was part-built: shipping the markup or CSS ahead of a consumer is the `.pdc*` class HO 668 spent a handoff excising, and HO 664 settled that a comment is not a caller. **Price it before building it** — this is a per-bill sub-resource fetch across 17,722 bills with its own rate-limit, cost and cadence questions, and it should get its own STEP 0 pricing rather than being inherited from this entry's framing. *Close (must be able to fail):* a **named** bill's cosponsor roster and related-bill identities are readable from the local DB **without a network call** — checked by querying that bill by id and printing both lists, which today returns nothing and must return rows.
- **Nine `if (await X.count())` guards in the e2e suite are latent green-on-deletion tests (opened HO 673).** *Owner:* Code. HO 673 deleted `.v2f-sc-card` and found the test **named for it** — "sponsor hover card renders inside the expand panel" — guarding its only assertion with `if (await card.count())`. With the card gone the count is 0, the assertion never runs, and the test **passes while annotating itself "not a finding."** That instance is fixed (replaced, and proven able to fail: red on pre-change markup, green after). **The pattern is not.** `grep -rn "if (await .*\.count())" e2e/` returns **11** hits, 2 of them the ones HO 673 replaced, leaving **9** (control: 48 total `.count()` occurrences in `e2e/`, so the guard grep discriminates rather than matching everything). Of the 9, **at least 4 wrap an `expect.soft` directly** — `fit-finish.spec.ts:449` (Acted chip rebases `?disposition=acted`), `:473` (agency facet rebases `?agency=`), `:495` (issue bar rebases `?issue=`), `:550` (trade member link points at a member hub) — so if any of those elements is renamed, removed, or broken, the assertion silently does not run and the suite stays green. The remainder (`:479`, `:504`, `:508`, `:633`, `:671`) guard setup clicks rather than assertions, which is the milder form of the same shape. **This is § Gates' subject in its purest form** — the guard makes "feature deleted" and "feature working" produce the same output — and it is the reason that section's reopening criterion exists. *Close (must be able to fail):* every remaining guard either (a) becomes an unconditional assertion, or (b) keeps its guard **and** gains a companion assertion that the guarded thing exists, so absence fails; demonstrated by deleting one guarded element in a scratch build and showing the suite go red where it previously stayed green. A close that reports "reviewed all 9" without that demonstration satisfies neither half.
- **Two `SponsorPhoto` implementations with different image provenance, and a comment asserting there is only one (opened HO 673).** *Owner:* Code. `components/SponsorPhoto.tsx:43` **constructs** `https://bioguide.congress.gov/bioguide/photo/{X}/{BIOGUIDE}.jpg` from the bioguide id; the local `SponsorPhoto` at `components/BillExpandPanel.tsx:152` renders the **stored** `members.depiction_url`, which is a `https://www.congress.gov/img/member/…_200.jpg` URL — **different host, different path, different failure surface**, and each carries its own `onError`→initials fallback. Consumers split accordingly: `AbsenceWatchCards.tsx` and `SponsorExpandedPanel.tsx` take the shared one, `BillExpandPanel` its own. **HO 673 edited the BillExpandPanel copy** (portrait always-on) and deliberately did not unify them — switching the panel's image source is a behaviour change beyond that HO's ruling, and it would swap a stored URL for a constructed one on seven routes at once. The comment at `SponsorPhoto.tsx:25-28` claimed the whole point of its `height` prop was that *"there stays exactly ONE implementation of the bioguide URL and of the `onError->initials` fallback"*; that was false when written, and **HO 673 corrected it in place** rather than only filing it, because a comment asserting an invariant that does not hold is worse than no comment — the next reader reasons from it. *Close (must be able to fail):* one implementation of the URL derivation and one of the fallback, with **both** hosts still reachable — i.e. the surviving implementation renders the same image for a member whose stored `depiction_url` and constructed bioguide URL differ, verified on a named member rather than asserted; and a grep for the retired implementation returns 0 render usages against a non-zero control.
- **`docs/design/` has the `scripts/diagnostic/` defect HO 672 just closed, at directory scale — nearly half the design artefacts are untracked, and three of them are cited by tracked files (opened HO 672 at the close; ruled its own HO — do NOT build it inside 673).** *Owner:* Code. **Measured, not estimated:** `docs/design/` holds **16 tracked · 15 untracked · 0 ignored**. There is no disposable-artefact location at all, so *every* mock that was not deliberately committed is simply loose in the working tree, and the split is close to half. **Three untracked mocks are cited by TRACKED files**, which is the same fresh-clone breakage HO 672 closed for probes: `components/PolarizationOverTime.tsx:14` (`// Visuals from docs/design/ideology-polarization-over-time.html`) — **a shipped source file pointing at a path absent from a fresh clone**, and the strongest instance because it is not a doc; `docs/roadmap.md:655` cites `mock-657-races-merge.html` for the HO 657 variant-B ruling, so the roadmap records a decision whose evidence does not ship; and `docs/handoffs/425-…md` cites `ideology-cut1-members-insitu.html`. **The precedent is already on the record and was handled ad hoc:** at HO 670, `landing.html` and `welcome-formats-mock.html` were committed mid-HO (`b284a42`) *precisely because* SKILL and the roadmap referenced them by path — the fix was applied to two files under time pressure instead of to the directory, which is why it is back. **A nuance that widens the surface rather than narrowing it:** `docs/handoffs/` is ignored in **both** the repo `.gitignore` and `~/.gitignore_global`, yet handoffs 425 and 428 are **tracked** — an ignore rule added later does not untrack what is already tracked, so "ignored" does not imply "cannot cite" for anything predating the rule. Any fix that assumes the ignore boundary is also the citation boundary will be wrong on exactly these files. **Evidence for the disposable class being real** (i.e. this is not just "commit everything"): `floor-votes-section(1).html` is untracked beside a tracked `floor-votes-section.html` — a browser-download duplicate, the paradigm case of an artefact that is throwaway *at the moment it is written*. *The fix pattern to transplant, and it is only half-transplantable:* `.gitignore` already carries the shape at `scripts/diagnostic/scratch/*` + `!…/README.md` — **design artefacts default to TRACKED, with one explicit ignored location for what is known-disposable when written, and the README negated back in so the directory and its rule ship with the repo.** **But the diagnostic fix's second half has no counterpart here and the HO must not assume one:** there, `tsconfig` *also* excludes the scratch directory, so `tsc` is a real enforcement that nothing depended-upon can live in it. HTML mocks are compiled by nothing, so **"cannot be cited by accident" currently has no instrument** — inventing or declining that instrument (a citation grep over tracked files in the doc sweep; a CI step; or a reasoned "prose citations are the only risk and review catches them") is the actual open design question, not a detail. *Close (HO 672 form, both halves, and state them so they CAN fail — cf. `docs/method.md` § Gates and the HO 662 fresh-clone count check that could not):* **(a)** a mock cited by any tracked file resolves in a **fresh clone** — checked by cloning to a temp dir and resolving every `docs/design/…` path appearing in tracked files, which fails loudly today on at least the three above; and **(b)** a genuinely throwaway mock **cannot be cited by accident** — checked by naming the instrument that would catch a tracked file referencing the disposable location, and demonstrating it catching a deliberately planted citation. A close that only reports "15 files committed" satisfies neither half. **HO 689 — FOURTH ad-hoc instance, FLAGGED NOT ABSORBED, and the entry's own figures have drifted.** HO 689 commits one mock (`mock-689-week-sentences.html`, a ruling record marking the ruled placement and voice — the HO 673 precedent) and was asked whether this entry came due. **It does not: its prescription is larger than a rider and it says so itself** — *ruled its own HO*, a two-part close where part (b) is an **open design question** (HTML is compiled by nothing, so the `tsconfig`-exclusion half of the HO 672 fix has no counterpart and the "cannot be cited by accident" instrument does not exist yet). Committing one more file ad hoc is precisely the pattern this entry records as *why it is back*, so it is counted here rather than repeated silently. **Re-measured 2026-09-03 at HEAD: `docs/design/` holds 18 tracked · 14 untracked · 0 ignored** — against the **16 · 15 · 0** this entry records, so the split moved while the entry stood still (the measured-number-goes-stale class, on the entry that exists to fix a directory). Of the 14, **13 pre-date this session**; the 14th is HO 689's mock, now tracked. The three tracked-file citations named above are **unchanged and still broken in a fresh clone**. **HO 690 — FIFTH ad-hoc instance, counted not absorbed, same reasoning as the fourth.** HO 690 commits `mock-690-chrome.html` (a ruling record: four sizes and three nav variants drawn live, the ruled option tagged in each section, the pad knob defaulting to the ruled 6px — the HO 673 / 689 precedent). The entry still does not come due: its prescription is a directory-scale fix whose part (b) is an **open design question**, and committing one more file ad hoc is the pattern this entry exists to record. **Re-measured 2026-09-03 at HEAD, BEFORE this HO's own mock is committed: `docs/design/` holds 19 tracked · 14 untracked · 0 ignored, 33 on disk** — against HO 689's **18 · 14 · 0** and the entry's original **16 · 15 · 0**. **The two readings reconcile and the reconciliation is the point:** 689 measured before committing its own mock, so its 18/14 became 19/13 the moment it did, and `mock-690-chrome.html` then took untracked back to 14. Committing this HO's mock makes it **20 · 13 · 0** — the tracked count rising one per handoff that rules against a mock, which is the ad-hoc pattern expressed as a number. The three tracked-file citations remain **unchanged and still broken in a fresh clone**; nothing in this HO touched them.
- **The summarize write pair is not atomic, and its failure path skips the flush it needed (opened HO 671 — a pre-existing exposure this HO neither creates nor widens; ruled its own HO on the review).** *Owner:* Code. In the advance branch of `lib/summarize-runner.ts`, `UPDATE bills` (`:315`) and `INSERT INTO stage_transitions` (`:341`) are two separate `db.execute` calls with **nothing wrapping them in a transaction**, and both sit **outside** `processBill`'s `try/catch` (`try` `:234`, `catch` `:255`). So if `:341` throws, `:315` has already committed, `stats.ok++` (`:401`) never runs, the worker's `await processBill(...)` (`:426`) rejects, `Promise.all` rejects, `runSummarize` throws, and **`revalidateTag("bills")` is never reached** — a summary written and never flushed, stale until the 3600s TTL. **HO 671's guard does not change this**: today the throw skips the flush by escaping the route, and after the guard it would skip it by escaping before `ok` is incremented — same outcome, same window, one line earlier. It is recorded here because the alternative was letting a live guarantee the code does not make ("no surface can serve a written-but-unflushed summary") land in the docs — the class of error the HO 670 SKILL guard caught. **The same shape exists in `lib/sync.ts`** (`upsertBill`'s upsert at `:238` then the conditional transition insert at `:272`) with the opposite consequence, and that asymmetry is what kept `/api/sync`'s flush unconditional at HO 671: there the throw is *caught*, so the tick continues and the unconditional flush still fires. *Close:* any one of three, and which one is the HO's call — (a) the pair becomes atomic (a batch/transaction, so a failed transition insert rolls the summary write back), (b) the flush moves somewhere a partial write can still reach it (a `finally`, or a wrote-anything flag set before the second write), or (c) a measurement shows the throw path cannot occur in practice — for which the instrument is `cron_runs` error rows for `/api/cron/summarize` carrying a `stage_transitions` failure, against the 0 such rows in the last 300 ticks. **Its own HO — do not fold it into a feature handoff.**

- ~~**`/api/cron/summarize` flushes the `bills` tag UNCONDITIONALLY every 10 minutes, and 98.7% of those flushes wrote nothing (opened HO 670 — a SITE-WIDE finding the `/welcome` measurement merely surfaced; ruled its own HO on the review).**~~ — **CLOSED HO 671 (`3edbb3d`), guard `if (stats.ok > 0) revalidateTag("bills")`.** The close criterion is answered by measurement, all four readings taken against a real server with the HO 670 rows tap, each able to fail: **A** warm visit **0** (the tap and cache behaving) · **B** idle tick (`summarized: 0`) then a visit **0** — *the guard took* · **C** a real WRITING tick (`summarized: 1`, 1,800 prompt / 122 output tokens, so a genuine Gemini call and a genuine row write) then a visit **89,090**, with the visit after it back to **0** — *the flush still fires on a write and settles* · **D** direct `POST /api/revalidate?tag=bills` then a visit **89,090** — the positive control proving B's zero is a reading, not silence. C and D agreeing to the row is what makes the pair mean something. **The guard shape is `ok > 0`, chosen on column visibility rather than on counts:** the deciding cell (`ok = 0, failed > 0`) was empty across 300 ticks, and the failure path writes only `summarize_failed_at` / `summarize_attempts`, which **0 of the 37 tagged queries read**. **`/api/sync` KEPT its unconditional flush**, ruled at STEP 0 on a discriminator rather than by analogy: its upsert sits **inside** the loop's `try/catch`, so a throw after the row commits is caught and leaves `upserted` at 0 with the write landed — invisible in the return value — where summarize's writes sit **outside** its `catch`, so a partial write cannot reach the flush at all. 4 flushes/day is cheap insurance; 144 was not. **Producing C required one authorised prod write** (Corey, HO 671 review): `119-hr-3370`'s `summary` nulled, full row captured first, the bill picked to be invisible on every `/welcome` surface — introduced stage, last action 2026-02-11, `stage_observed_at` NULL, not among the 12 oldest by `latest_action_date` (the stalls panel's exact slice), 0 news mentions, no cluster. It healed on the same tick: corpus back to **17,716/17,716**, **0** `stage_transitions` rows appended (the predicted `noop`), `text_length` and `stage` identical; `topics` came back **`["government_operations","housing"]`** where it had been **`["other"]`** — the regenerated classification is different, and arguably better, but it is a real content change and is recorded rather than smoothed over. **Cron-lock side effect, named because it is a local action with a prod consequence:** `claimCronLock` is shared state, so the local tick took the slot lock prod's `*/10` would otherwise have claimed and prod's tick no-op'd for that window — the HO 432 design working as intended, harmless, and on the record.

  **The residue is the new steady state, and it is the number the entry existed to produce.** Flushes fall from ~**148/day** (144 unconditional + 4 sync) to ~**6/day** (~2 writing summarize ticks — 4 in 300 ticks over 49.8h — plus sync's 4), a **~25× reduction**, and it lands on every consumer of all **37** `bills`-tagged entries, not just the page that found it. `/welcome` alone goes from ~13.2M rows/day if continuously visited to ~**0.53M**. Nothing was traded for it: no query changed, no panel was dropped, no schedule moved.

  Original entry, kept verbatim below because the close is only legible against what it closed: *Owner:* Code. `app/api/cron/summarize/route.ts:88` calls `revalidateTag("bills")` immediately after `runSummarize`, outside any condition, on a `*/10` schedule — **~144 flushes/day**, plus `/api/sync`'s 4 (`0 */6`), so ~**148**. It invalidates the tag for **every** `bills`-tagged consumer on **every** route, not just the page that found it: **37 cache entries in `lib/queries.ts` carry the tag** (27 `["bills"]` alone, plus 10 in combination with `news-breaking` / `members` / `votes` / `committees`).

  **The write-free share is measured, not inferred** (`scripts/diagnostic/summarize-flush-670.ts`, over `cron_runs`): **300 consecutive ticks**, 2026-08-16T13:40Z → 2026-08-18T15:30Z — **300 reached the flush, 4 summarized ≥1 bill, 296 summarized ZERO (98.7%)**, 0 lock-skips, 0 unreadable payloads. That ratio is the expected consequence of HO 406 working: the queue is drained, inflow is ~30 bills/day, and a `*/10` tick clears 100–120, so the overwhelming majority of ticks correctly find nothing to do — **and flush anyway**. The HO 669 envelope trap was avoided rather than dodged: the raw row is printed (`{ok, elapsedMs, payload:{summarized,…}}`, the real fields one level down) and the control holds — `wrote` reads **4**, not 0, so a zero is a genuine idle tick and not a misread field.

  **Cost, from the `/welcome` measurement in the entry below:** the first visit after a `bills` flush costs **89,090** rows, matching the sum of that page's eight `bills`-tagged entries (**88,987**) to within 103 — the sum-match is what identifies the mechanism rather than merely observing a number. **The coupling that decides the fix: ~94% of `/welcome`'s per-render cost is `bills`-tagged, so the lever on the absolute number is the FLUSH CADENCE, not the dataset count.** Gating the flush on a write would remove **98.7%** of the flushes and cut the page's steady-state bill by roughly an order of magnitude **without touching a single query or dropping a panel**; trimming datasets would not — dropping a whole panel's three datasets saves far less than one avoided flush, and every other `bills`-tagged route gets the same relief for free.

  **The shape of the fix is one line and the precedent is already in the file:** `stats.ok` is in scope at the call site, and the lock-overlap path (`route.ts`, "another drain in flight") **already returns before the flush** — so a tick that does nothing not flushing is the existing behaviour for one case, not a new principle. Also note `revalidateTag("bills")` in `/api/sync` is likewise unconditional, and `runSync` reports `upserted`/`seen`; same question, same file family, decide both in that HO. **Adjacent, comment-only:** the route's own header still says "Schedule lives in vercel.json (13:00 UTC daily…)" while `vercel.json` reads `*/10 * * * *` — stale since the schedule changed; it rides that HO's touch of the file. *Close:* the flush fires only when the tick wrote, **measured** — a tick with `summarized: 0` followed by a page visit costs **0** rows, while a tick that wrote is followed by the full ~89k. *Instrument fails honestly:* if the zero-write tick's next visit still costs ~89k, the guard did not take, and if the writing tick's visit costs 0 the guard is over-tight and the surface is serving stale summaries — both readable from the same two measurements. **Its own HO — do not build it inside a feature handoff.**

- ~~**`/welcome`'s absolute read cost is governed by the `bills` TAG, not by any TTL (opened HO 670; reframed twice by measurement; **the cadence half CLOSED at HO 671**).** *Owner:* Code. **HO 671 UPDATE — the cadence this entry was built on is gone:** the summarize flush is now guarded (`if (stats.ok > 0)`), so flushes fall from ~**148/day** to ~**6/day** and this page's steady-state bill falls with them — ~13.2M rows/day if continuously visited becomes ~**0.53M**. The per-flush figure is unchanged and still measured at **89,090** (readings C and D of HO 671 STEP 3, agreeing to the row), because the guard changes *how often* the tag is invalidated and never *what* it invalidates. What remains open here is only the **prod confirmation** below; the local mechanism is now measured twice over. The third path also stays worth knowing: `app/api/revalidate/route.ts:50` **defaults to `"bills"` when the `tag` param is absent**, which makes an unparameterised manual call the single most expensive request the app can serve. **Headline: ~89,090 rows on the first visit after each `bills` flush, against the old page's ~54,665 — a 1.63× steady-state multiple, up to ~148 times a day.** The 94,240 per-render figure is the **cold-start** cost and is labelled as such; warm visits cost **0 rows** (all 14 reads are `unstable_cache`-wrapped, and `force-dynamic` disables the full-route cache, not the Data Cache). **The review's framing — a per-TTL ceiling of ~69.7k rows/hour, 1.27× — is superseded: it modelled the TTL as the governing clock, and the TTL is a backstop the tag almost never reaches.** Measured, not modelled (`scripts/diagnostic/rows-tap-670.cjs`, a `NODE_OPTIONS` tap on a real server): warm visit **0** → `POST /api/revalidate?tag=bills` → next visit **89,090** → next visit **0** again; `tag=news-breaking` costs **10,185** the same way. The 89,090 matches the sum of the page's eight `bills`-tagged entries (88,987) to within 103 rows, which is what identifies the mechanism rather than merely observing a number.~~ — **CLOSED HO 672: the prod confirmation landed, and the criterion it was written against turned out to bundle two questions.** *"Flat across idle ticks, with steps only at writing ticks"* mixes a **mechanism** claim with a **cadence** claim, and a latency window was only ever the right instrument for the first. **Mechanism — closed.** Does the guard take on Vercel's Data Cache, or does that surface behave differently from `next start` (HO 312)? Measured 2026-08-19 across a chosen window (`scripts/diagnostic/flush-window-672.ts`, `/welcome` every 20s, `/api/version` sampled in the same cycle as an untagged control): baseline **p25 333 · p50 353 · p75 380 ms**; **sensitivity PASS** at the six-cron 00:00Z slot (peak **2251 ms, 6.4×**); **one clean positive** — the `00:10:20` tick wrote (`summarized=21`), the guard fired, and the next sample read **1304 ms (3.7×)** before returning to 391/350/368; **one clean negative** — the `00:20:20` tick was idle, and the following five samples read 334/348/345/330/307, **entirely inside the baseline IQR**. The control channel held **91–115 ms through the 2251 ms cycle**, so the excursions are server-side regeneration and not network. **Cadence — not a latency question, and already answered better elsewhere:** how often a tick writes is a `cron_runs` claim, measured over 8 days at **3.3 writing ticks/day, mean, range 0–5** — which supersedes the ~2/day this arc had been carrying (HO 671 sampled a 49.8h stretch containing 08-16, which wrote nothing all day, and 08-17, which wrote once; **a point estimate reported without its range invites exactly that correction**). So the guarded cadence is ~**7.3 flushes/day mean, range 4–9**, against ~148 — the conclusion unchanged, the point estimate corrected. A second window at 06:10Z was **declined**: it would buy more negatives for the half the tick log already answers. **THE EXPERIMENT IS THIN AND THE REASON IS SELF-INFLICTED:** `TaskStop` reported success twice without killing the detached children, so three samplers ran concurrently and the load changes at **00:40:01** and **00:50:02** landed on two of the four planned negatives, which were discarded as unattributable (oddities). **One positive, one negative, one sensitivity reading.** **The variance cuts both ways and this hour ran HOT, not cold** — two writing ticks in one hour (`00:01:15` and `00:10:20`, both `summarized=21`) against a 3.3/day mean. **One unplanned corroboration, in a single two-minute stretch:** the idle `00:31:19` summarize tick produced nothing (321 ms), and the news cron flushing `news-breaking` fifteen seconds later produced **977 ms** — the discriminator working in both directions, on two different tags, inside two minutes.

  **The amplification is worse than the review's estimate and the reason is a second cron.** `/api/sync` fires `revalidateTag("bills")` 4×/day (`0 */6`) — but **`/api/cron/summarize` fires it too, UNCONDITIONALLY, on a `*/10` schedule** (`app/api/cron/summarize/route.ts:88`), so the ceiling is ~**148 flushes/day**, not 4. **Yes, the 86400s entries carry it:** `getMembersRanked` is `revalidate 86400, tags ["members","bills","votes"]` and `getFeedBills` is `86400, ["bills","news-breaking"]`, so both are flushed by a tag ~148× more often than their own timer would ever expire — `getMembersRanked` alone is 20,995 × up to 148 ≈ **3.1M rows/day** rather than the ~21k/day its TTL implies. The other two 86400s entries are NOT amplified: `getMostCompetitiveRaces` (`race-ratings`) is flushed weekly (`0 11 * * 3`) and `getLatestMarketTicks` (`markets`) ~24×/weekday by the markets crons at 3,004 each (~72k/day).

  **The STEP 0 table conflicts with this and the STEP 0 table is WRONG — the blank cache column was mine, and an empty cell read as "uncached".** The `86400 · tags` reading is right, and it is right three independent ways: (a) the source, read directly — `lib/queries.ts:6537` `["getMembersRanked"]`, `:6545` `{ revalidate: 86400, tags: ["members","bills","votes"] }`; (b) the warm measurement — visits 2, 4 and 6 each cost **0** rows, which is impossible for an uncached read; (c) the flush measurement — invalidating `bills` makes exactly those entries reappear in the cost, which identifies *which* entries the tag governs instead of just showing that something is cached.

  **The lever is the flush cadence, not the dataset count — stated here because it is the decision this entry exists to inform.** ~94% of the page's per-render cost is `bills`-tagged, so making the summarize flush conditional on a write (filed separately above; 98.7% of its flushes wrote nothing) would cut this page's steady-state bill by roughly an order of magnitude **without dropping a panel or touching a query**, while trimming datasets — the intuitive move — would not: the whole Patterns panel is 4,477 rows against 89,090 per flush. **What actually bounds the bill is `min(visits, flushes)`**, since a flush costs nothing until someone arrives: a continuously-visited page is ~**13.2M rows/day** (148 × 89,090; the old page would be ~8.1M), one visit an hour is ~2.1M/day, and an unvisited hour is free. For scale, HO 582 measured the markets-tape burn at ~17.6M/day per open tab against an ~88M/day plateau, so this is the same order as the item that arc was opened for. *Close:* confirm the shape **on prod against a real cron-driven flush** — a summarize tick followed by a `/welcome` visit, read off the Turso rows-read slope rather than a local tap — **or** record why the local measurement suffices (it uses the same `revalidateTag` primitive the crons call, via the app's own `/api/revalidate` route). *Instrument:* if the mechanism holds, prod shows a step of ~89k rows per visited flush window and **nothing** in unvisited ones; a flat slope across visited windows falsifies it and means the Data Cache behaves differently under Vercel than under `next start`, which would itself be the finding.

- ~~**`docs/method.md` needs a look-at-it gate for render-touching work — its own small HO, not a ride-along (opened HO 670, ruled by Corey on the HO 670 review).** *Owner:* Code. **The evidence is HO 670 itself, and it is the strongest kind: the gates were all green and the page was wrong three times.** `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `/welcome` 200, both stylesheets 200, the retired tokens at 0 with a positive control at 11, the dropped module selectors at 0 in the built CSS, and **all nine panel datasets present in the served HTML with every row matched** — and underneath that clean sheet sat a ~300px dead zone below every panel, an empty right third of the ODDS strip, and a reduced-motion render whose top fade permanently half-erased the first row's title. Each was found by **looking at a screenshot**, and none is reachable by any string assertion: a string is in the markup whether or not it is inside the visible box, and a mask that erases text changes no DOM. **This generalises past `/welcome`.** HO 664–669 ran a tally of 13 instrument failures against 0 code failures, which trained the harness to trust greps and 200s; HO 670 inverts it — sound instruments, wrong code — so the lesson "control your instruments" is necessary and **not sufficient**. *Close:* `docs/method.md` carries a visual-inspection step in its gate list for any HO that touches render output, and the step **names what looking means concretely** rather than saying "eyeball it": the viewport set to capture (HO 670 used 1920/1440/1100/430), a **`prefers-reduced-motion` pass** as a separate capture because it is a different layout and not a dimmer one, and the screenshots retained as artifacts. **Method changes get their own HO** — do not write this inside a feature handoff. *Instrument for the close:* the step exists in `method.md` **and** the next render-touching HO's report cites it by name; if the step is written and the following UI HO's paste has no capture in it, the step is decoration and the entry reopens.~~ — **CLOSED HO 672: written as `docs/method.md` § Gates, and WIDENED past render output at the HO 671 close.** The general form is **a gate you cannot fail is not a gate** — before running a check, say what it reads if the work was never done; if that is the same as success, it is not the check you think it is. It carries **six** instances, and **three are about reading an instrument wrongly rather than about the thing under test**, which is the pattern the entry exists to name: HO 670's green gates over three wrong layouts (*a visual check with no capture is not a check*); HO 671's `| tail -3` showing stack frames instead of the message (*never `tail` a command whose failure mode is a stack trace*); HO 671's same one-liner being a **write** whose failure mode was "unclear whether it landed" (*a write instrument that does not read back is not an instrument*); HO 672's `git check-ignore -v` **exiting 0 on a negated pattern**, so "not ignored" reads as "ignored" — authoritative instrument `git add --dry-run`; HO 672's **`TaskStop` reporting success without killing the detached children**, the first here where a tool lied about an *action* rather than a state; and HO 672's **`--word-diff=color` written to a file**, an export carrying **0 markers in 38,468 bytes** because the markers are ANSI escapes — *an empty word-diff and a diff with no changes are the same bytes*. All three HO 672 instances are in oddities. **The last two arrived AFTER this entry's close note was first drafted, one of them inside the delivery step of the commit that closes it** — which is the entry's own subject continuing to happen while it was being written, and is left on the record rather than smoothed into a round number. The concrete requirements this entry asked for are all in it: the named viewport set, the **separate `prefers-reduced-motion` capture** because it is a different layout and not a dimmer one, captures retained in the paste, plus what an instrument's zero means with a control that can produce a non-zero, read-back on every write, and no truncation from the end of trace-shaped output. **Two requirements go beyond what this entry asked for.** *A gate binds to the state at the paste, not to the state when it ran* — every other instance is a check run and misread, whereas HO 670's tree went red **after** its final gate because a probe was written afterwards, so the report was true when made and false when read; cheap gates are re-run immediately before the paste. And the append-only **deletion gate carries its reproduction**: HO 639 filed `grep -c '^-[^-]'` as blind on reasoning, and this HO caught it red-handed **on the commit that closes this very entry** — striking a backlog line edits a line beginning `- **`, so the removed side reads `-- **…` and `^-[^-]` excludes it by construction; measured, the grep returned **0** while `--numstat` reported **2**. A clean grep and a genuinely additive commit are the same output. **Its reopening criterion survives the widening verbatim** — if the next render-touching HO's paste carries no capture, the step is decoration and this reopens.

- **Global `IN BETA` tag + live clock on `HeaderBar`, every route (opened HO 670 — ruled by Corey 2026-08-17, deliberately not taken there).** *Owner:* Code. Corey wants both on the dashboard and on every page. `HeaderBar` renders on every route, so per the HO 507 shared-component rule this is its own change behind its own route gates, and HO 670 was scoped to one page. **Nothing is blocked meanwhile:** `/welcome` has no masthead, so its tag rides its own top rail (`app/welcome/landing.module.css` `.beta`) and its clock is a page-local island. **The reduced-motion guard is the reusable half and is not optional:** the tag pulses `opacity 1 → .32 → 1` over 2.4s and `@media (prefers-reduced-motion:reduce)` sets `animation:none` — a persistent pulse is a real problem for photosensitive and vestibular users, and the media query costs one line. **The clock must be MOUNTED, not re-implemented:** `components/WelcomeClock.tsx` (HO 670) already reuses the shared `useZoneCycle` (HO 183), so the masthead's copy is a second render site for that component, not a second implementation — the thing HO 183 exists to prevent. *Close:* land both behind route gates, carrying the `prefers-reduced-motion` guard the `/welcome` tag already has, **or** record why the masthead keeps neither.

That is the same test HO 669 applied to the bare branch it stripped — which makes these its residue, found by applying the standard to the neighbours. `MarketsTape`'s `showMeta` defaults to **`true`** (`components/MarketsTape.tsx:27` post-strip) and **neither live mount passes the prop** — `DashboardV2Header.tsx:146` and `:147–155` pass `symbols`/`pairs`/`kind`/`scroll`/`reverse`/`label` and nothing else — so the client's false-arms at `components/MarketsTapeClient.tsx:884` and `:958` (plus the `:638` hooks-must-not-be-conditional comment that exists to serve them) cannot be reached from anything that renders. Unlike HO 664's `BillRowList` filing, **nothing this HO did made that true**: the last `showMeta={false}` caller was the top tape of a two-tape shape that predates the B2 rework; the prop and its default are exactly as HO 669 found them, and were left exactly as found. **Deliberately NOT chased in HO 669**, per the HO 507 shared-component rule: `MarketsTapeClient` is the live client island on `/` and runs past 1,000 lines, so touching it is its own change behind its own gates — and the same two live tapes are the only surfaces to gate on.

This surfaced out of HO 669's `:2000` SKILL cut-back and is the CSS shadow of the branch that HO 669 removed from the component: the static tape died as a *mount* at HO 608–610 and as a *code branch* at HO 669, and this is the third place it persists.

  **The dead-value caveat, stated up front so nobody reaches for the wrong instrument. What is dead here is VALUES, not SELECTORS, and it is exactly two declarations** — measured, not inferred: (1) `.markets-tape-item`'s `padding: 0 10px` (`app/globals.css:6663`), overridden to `0 11px` by `:6678–6680`; (2) the `width: 28px` on `.markets-tape-meta::before` (`:6956`) and `.markets-tape-label::after` (`:7006`), overridden to `72px` by `:6970–6972`. **Everything else in those base rules is fully live on the scrolling tape** — `display:inline-flex`, `gap:6px`, `white-space:nowrap`, `position:relative`, and both fades' `content`/`position`/`gradient`/`pointer-events` — because the unscoped rules still match a `--scroll` tape for every property the scoped ones do not override. **And `.markets-tape--scroll .markets-tape-item::after` (`:6691`, the HO 363 pipe) has NO unscoped counterpart at all** — one occurrence in the file — so it is purely additive, fully effective on both live mounts, and contributes **no** dead value; it does not belong in this set despite sitting in the same scoped cluster. **Consequence: the HO 668 `.pdc*` excision instrument does NOT transfer.** No selector loses its last consumer, so a built-CSS "selector reads 0" gate would read **green here and prove nothing** — the exact shape of a close criterion whose instrument cannot fail, which this file's own header warns against. Any close needs a per-declaration argument, not a per-selector census.

  **Recorded because it decides how much weight the comment carries: `app/globals.css:6674–6675` already states why the scoping stays, and its stated reason is API reachability, not a live surface** — "*its one caller, the pre-swap classic dashboard, was removed at HO 608 — the scoping stays because `scroll` is still an opt-in prop on MarketsTape*". That is the **weaker** of the two reasons it could have given: it does not claim anything renders unscoped, only that the component's signature still permits it. HO 669 verified that clause is still true and cut its `:2000` SKILL edit back to the verified half on the strength of it. **A keep reason that rests on an opt-in prop is exactly as durable as the prop** — if `scroll` ever becomes required or is removed, this comment's justification goes with it, and the values above become unreachable by construction rather than merely uncalled.

  *Close:* **both instances together, or each with its own recorded reason, and the two have different shapes so do not assume one disposition covers them.** For `showMeta`: strip the branch behind gates on the two live tapes, or record a keep reason. For `scroll`: decide whether the static shape is still wanted as an API affordance — if yes, the `:6674–6675` comment is the keep reason and should say so in those terms rather than by pointing at a caller that has been gone since HO 608; if no, `scroll` stops being a prop, the `--scroll` scoping collapses into the base rules, and the two dead declarations fold away with it. **Do not open this as a CSS-deletion chore** — per the caveat above, there is no dead selector to excise.

- **SKILL describes the HO 244 masthead readout as FIVE stats; the live dashboard renders TWO — comment-only, rides the next touch of the `/` entry (opened HO 667).** *Owner:* Code, cheap. `SKILL.md:1937` (the `HomeHeader` bullet) spells the readout as `` `n bills tracked · n in committee · n on the floor · n other chamber · n enacted` ``, and `:1579` (`getCorpusStats`) and `:1952` (the font-size table) both point at the same description. **Measured against a local prod render at `ea4fa31` (2026-08-15): `class="home-stat-readout"` emits `17,203 tracked · 98 enacted`** — two stats, and not even the same wording (`tracked`, not `bills tracked`; the literal string `bills tracked` returns **0** hits in the rendered HTML).
