It was stale before this HO — **news split out to `/api/cron/news` at HO 117** — and HO 667 made it stale a second way by **retiring the lead step from `/api/sync` entirely**, so the sentence now names two things that are each independently untrue of that route. **Deliberately not fixed at HO 667:** correcting news-cron prose is a different entry's scope, and widening a retirement diff into a neighbouring topic is how a scoped change stops being reviewable (the HO 660 `CRON_ROUTES` reasoning, applied to a doc). **Filed rather than flagged in chat on purpose** — HO 667 struck the fourth never-struck ledger item in four sessions (the primaries display-orphan sweep, done at HO 603 and never recorded), and a chat-only flag is exactly that class's feeder. *Close:* both passages restated on whatever change next opens the news-cron sections. *Instrument:* `grep -n "summarize + lead generation" .claude/skills/cbt/SKILL.md` reads empty when it's done.

- **`isSettled` freezes a contest the instant it is SCORED, so a winner that arrives late can never land — the parent of the 11 (opened HO 641, measured HO 640).** *Owner:* Code. `lib/primaries-sync.ts:551-565`: a row is settled ⇔ `primary_date < today` **AND** some `primary_candidates` row carries a non-NULL `vote_pct`. **There is no winner term.** The guard runs at all three sync write paths (`:646` `scrapeSenateSpecialState`, `:905` `syncSenateCandidates`, `:1311` `syncHouseDistricts`) **before the incoming roster is built**, and `status` and `vote_pct` are written in the **same INSERT** (`:685` / `:948` / `:1380`). So a contest scraped in the window *after* Ballotpedia posts results but *before* it marks a winner takes shares + `running` and **becomes settled in that same instant** — every later tick refuses it. It is a race against Ballotpedia's call. **516 of 718 completed contests are settled, and most of those froze CORRECTLY**, having been marked before they settled: the damage is the 11 that froze mid-write, so **the mechanism is the defect, not the count**. The arithmetic closes exactly — 506 with-winner + 11 = 517 against 516 with-shares, the one-row difference being **`senate-MI-2026-R`** (1 candidate, winner marked, no share recorded — uncontested). **TWO dispositions, independent of each other.** **(1) STOP NEW LOSSES — the guard fix, and it is NOT a one-liner.** Adding a winner term to `isSettled` un-freezes every contest that legitimately has no winner — uncontested seats like `senate-ME-2026-R`, where Ballotpedia marks no votebox winner because there is no contest — and those would re-scrape forever. A correct fix needs a **bounded re-check** (eligible for N days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless) or an attempt counter, and **N has to be measured against how long Ballotpedia actually takes to call.** Probe, then build, under its own number. **(2) RECOVER THE EXISTING 11 — the script already ships; do not build one.** `isSettled` is module-private and guards only the three sync paths: **`npm run backfill:primary-results`** bypasses it entirely (`scripts/backfill-primary-results.ts` UPDATEs `vote_pct` AND `status`, no settled check — its only date clause *targets* past contests), as does `reingest:primary-slate`. **Gated on one measurement: has Ballotpedia marked those voteboxes since?** One live fetch against one contest answers it — a live fetch, therefore not a docs-HO action. **Framing, corrected in review:** the freeze is **unreachable by any automatic path, NOT irreversible**. Calling it permanent would justify building a repair that already exists. What is true is the reporting: the cron reports success every tick while never touching these rows — the same-as-success shape (HO 503/506/637) on the ingestion side. **HO 656 — DISPOSITION (2) EXECUTED AND PROVEN ON REAL DATA; DISPOSITION (1) NOW HAS A RECOMMENDED SHAPE AND AN HONEST NON-MEASUREMENT.** The gate was one live fetch of `house-NJ-09-2026-R`: **Pino now parses `isWinner=true` at 51.3%**, so Ballotpedia had marked it since — the freeze is **unreachable, not irreversible**, exactly as the corrected framing said. `npm run backfill:primary-results` then ran off-tick (**398 seats, 370 ok / 28 empty-or-failed, 1,926 rows updated, `vote_pct` 1,663 → 2,077 of 2,527**) and took the frozen set **11 → 6**: **7 recovered** (CA-04/14/16/43, NJ-09, WA-07, WA-09), **4 residual** (WA-05, WA-08, ME-D, ME-R). **The residuals were live-checked, not inferred:** all three pages parse `ok` with **zero winners marked**, so the residue is **upstream-unmarked, NOT a parse failure** — a distinction that changes the disposition, since a parse failure would be our bug. **THE POPULATION IS A MOVING SET, NOT A FIXED 11**, and that is the durable correction: two rows entered the class *during the run* (`house-AZ-03-2026-R`, 1 candidate/uncontested; `house-VA-10-2026-R`, 2 candidates, Aug 4) because writing shares ahead of the call is what settles a row. So the mechanism is **live and continuously recoverable**, and re-running the backfill is maintenance, not a one-off repair. **N CANNOT BE MEASURED FROM OUR DATA — say it plainly rather than fitting one:** we know only that Ballotpedia *had* marked the recovered 7 by the run date, never *when*, so those are upper bounds only; the live residuals give the other end at **9 days (WA) and 65 days (ME) still unmarked**. **RECOMMENDED SHAPE: a bounded re-check window, N = 30 days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless** — chosen **generously, not fitted**; cost is N wasted fetches on an uncontested seat, and at 30 the recent contests stay eligible while ME settles. The attempt-counter alternative needs a column, hence a migration, hence out of scope by construction. **Corpus figures re-derived: completed contests 768** (entry: 718), **with-shares 517 → 658** across the run. **The build is QUEUED below under its own number; `isSettled` was NOT edited here.** **HO 661 — DISPOSITION (1) IS NOW BUILT, and it corrected this entry's own cost model on the way.** The predicate is **past-dated AND (`status='winner'` OR older than `today − 30d`)**; the `vote_pct` term is gone, so the guard no longer freezes the scored-but-uncalled rows this entry was opened about, and the naive-winner-term failure mode it warned of (uncontested seats re-scraping forever) is bounded by the window rather than by a share. **The "N wasted fetches" framing in this entry is FALSE:** `isSettled` is a **write-freeze evaluated after the fetch**, and the cursor selects units with no settled term, so the window spends no fetches at all — it spends N days of rewrite-eligibility, which is the healing. **Disposition (2) is unchanged and still load-bearing:** `backfill:primary-results` / `reingest:primary-slate` remain the recovery past N, and re-running them stays **maintenance, not repair**, because **the population was always a moving set** — a row joins the class the instant it takes shares ahead of its call, which is exactly what the window now gives 30 days to resolve. Live at ship: 4 rows unfrozen, 2 windows running (AZ-03-R to 08-20; the three Aug-4 rows to 09-03).
- ~~**QUEUED — build the bounded re-check that stops NEW freezes (recommended HO 656, parent above).** *Owner:* Code. **Shape: a contest is re-check-eligible for N days past `primary_date`, then settles regardless**, replacing today's settle-the-instant-it-is-scored. **N = 30, chosen GENEROUSLY rather than fitted, and the reason is that N is NOT MEASURABLE from our data** — recovery timing gives upper bounds only (we know Ballotpedia *had* marked, never *when*), and the live residuals sit unmarked at 9 days (WA) and 65 (ME). At 30 the recent contests stay eligible while the structurally-unmarkable ones (uncontested, RCV) settle instead of re-fetching forever — which is the failure the naive winner-term fix has. **Cost is N wasted fetches per uncontested seat; say that in the build rather than pretending the parameter is free.** **The attempt-counter alternative is DISQUALIFIED, not rejected on merit** — it needs a column, so it is a migration. *Close:* the window ships and a contest scored-then-called inside N lands its winner without a manual backfill. **Until then `backfill:primary-results` is the recovery path and re-running it is MAINTENANCE, not repair** — the frozen population moves (HO 656 watched two rows join mid-run).~~ — **SHIPPED HO 661** (`c0d2376`, prod-verified on `2d188f2`). `isSettled` now settles on **decided (`status='winner'`) OR expired (past `today − SETTLE_WINDOW_DAYS`, N = 30)**; the `vote_pct` term is gone. **The cost line above is WRONG and the build corrected it rather than restating it:** `isSettled` is a **WRITE-freeze, not a fetch-skip** — all three call sites evaluate after the page is fetched and parsed, and the cursor's unit selection carries no settled term, so **there are no wasted fetches to spend**; N buys N days of *rewrite-eligibility* on decided-but-uncalled rows, which is the healing itself (oddities: a cost stated in units the architecture does not spend). **Figures, 2026-08-14 against 876 rows: settled 658 → 732 (+74), predicted == observed, arithmetic reconciles.** Unfreezes **4** (`house-VA-10-2026-R`, `WA-05-open`, `WA-08-open`, `AZ-03-R`; **WI-06 resolved NO** — shareless); stays-settled-via-expiry **2** (ME-D/ME-R); called-but-shareless **0** — **`senate-MI-2026-R` was FALSIFIED** as a member, it carries a winner *and* a share; newly-settles-via-expiry **78** (3 with a roster: the GA/LA runoffs; 75 never had a page). *Close criterion met on real data:* the guard was exercised in both directions in one district — `house-AZ-03-2026-D` (called) refused, `house-AZ-03-2026-R` (scored, uncalled) took the write, content byte-identical because Ballotpedia still has not marked it, which is the correct answer from an unchanged source. See roadmap "Also (HO 661)".
- **~~QUEUED — the challenger harvest has no schedule; it runs when a human remembers (scoped HO 656, not wired).~~ — WIRED AT HO 660** (`23fa9fa` refactor, `5b08bdd` feature). **Both halves of the close criterion are now true: slot (b) CHOSEN on the record at HO 659, WIRED here** as `/api/cron/race-challengers` daily at **`30 12 * * *`** — thirty minutes after the 12:00 UTC primaries tick that writes this harvest's entire input, so it harvests what that tick just settled; a 00:00-tick winner waits at most 12.5h, the stated price of one daily fire. Table went 16 crons → 17 (functions 15 → 16); `CRON_ROUTES` gained the route at **26h**, mirrored from the existing daily entries. **No cron-lock, as a decision:** the primaries tick is deadline-bounded at 50s and done by ~12:01, and nothing else writes `race_candidates` on a schedule. The harvest moved to `lib/harvest-challengers.ts` (one implementation, two callers — the npm run survives as the manual path), SQL byte-identical, **equivalence gated on the HO 659 instrument**: checksum `127aeac…` unchanged, one fresh stamp across 206 rows, curated 11 still NULL. **Prod verification at `5b08bdd`:** 401 with no header AND with a wrong secret (guard fired, not assumed), then 200 in 643ms — `cleared 206 · inserted 206 · rows 206 · races 147 · of 187 rated`; one `success` `cron_runs` row; `/api/health` lists the route healthy. ~~**WATCH — the first SCHEDULED fire is pending and is not claimed (HO 504 precedent: a manual POST proves the route, never the schedule).** Confirm the ~12:30 UTC `cron_runs` row for `/api/cron/race-challengers` on the next calendar day.~~ — **CONFIRMED HO 662 (2026-08-14).** `/api/health` reads the route's `lastRunAt` **`2026-08-14T12:30:49.466Z`**, `lastStatus` **`success`**, `healthy` true, against schedule `30 12 * * *` — the row this WATCH asked for, landing at the scheduled minute rather than by hand, which is what the HO 504 precedent required. *(Instrument caveat, so a later reader does not over-read it: `/api/health` reports only the LATEST `cron_runs` row per route. It confirms this fire; it enumerates no history and cannot show a subsequent miss until one is the latest.)* **That row is also what ARMS the M3 re-measure trigger below** — the restated entry keys on the first *scheduled* fire, because the detector calibrates against the maintained regime and a hand-fired route is still a harvest that runs when a human remembers. **The trigger is therefore live as of 2026-08-14, and no memory carries it across the migration — it is stated here and nowhere else.** *Original shape, retained for the record —* *Owner:* Corey/Code. HO 656 measured the cost of that: the harvest sat at its FIRST-RUN figure (82 rows / 53 seats) until run by hand, and one run moved **94 seats** into having a challenger. **Two shapes priced. (a) Chain it after the daily primaries sync** — its input is exactly what that sync writes, so freshness is ideal; **but `/api/cron/primaries` is deadline-bounded at 50s inside a 60s ceiling and walks a per-unit cursor**, so added work trades cursor progress for harvest freshness, and the cursor is the thing that makes a killed tick resumable. **(b) Its own daily cron slot.** **RECOMMENDED: (b)** — the harvest is pure DB-to-DB (no Ballotpedia fetches), it is idempotent under its sentinel, and it does not need to ride the sync's transaction, so it buys freshness without spending the cursor's budget. Read cost is small against the HO 602 picture (the top consumers are the `bills_agg`/participation aggregates, not this). *Close:* a slot is chosen and wired under its own number, or the manual cadence is accepted **on the record** with the 94-seat staleness figure attached. **Nothing was wired at HO 656.** **RULED AT HO 659 — slot (b), its own daily cron slot, is CHOSEN on the record; the wiring is HO 660.** The recommendation above is adopted unchanged and for its stated reasons (pure DB-to-DB, idempotent under the sentinel, no claim on the primaries cursor's 50s budget). **This entry stays OPEN**: its close criterion is *chosen AND wired*, and only the first half is done — a ruling with no cron line is still a harvest that runs when a human remembers.
- **~~QUEUED — `race_candidates` has no `updated_at`, so a harvest's effect can only be bracketed, never dated (scoped HO 656, migration deferred).~~ — SHIPPED HO 659** (`00b07eb`). `updated_at TEXT` added by `ensureColumn` in `main()` (never the CREATE array — the HO 597 `activity_count` rule), with both writers stamping: the harvest's `INSERT OR IGNORE` and `seed-races`'s upsert, whose `DO UPDATE` sets it too. **Writers were re-derived, not trusted: exactly two**; every other reference in `scripts/`+`lib/` is a SELECT. **Semantics pinned — reach, not last content change** (an idempotent rewrite stamps), one `runStamp` per invocation so run identification is a GROUP BY, and **NULL = pre-instrumentation, never backfilled**. **Both falsification legs ran.** Post-migration non-NULL = **0** (the migration stamps nothing, proven). Post-harvest: non-NULL **206 = the sentinel row count**, **one distinct stamp** across 206 rows / 147 seats, and the sentinel content checksum **byte-identical** to the pre-migration capture (`127aeac…`, 206 rows) with zero row-count drift. Negative leg: the **11 curated rows are still NULL**, count unchanged — which proves the migration didn't stamp AND that the harvest's skip-curated guard still holds with the new column in the statement. Instrument: `scripts/diagnostic/race-candidates-stamp-659.ts`. *Original shape, retained for the record —* *Owner:* Code. The table records `source_url` (the `harvest:primary_winner` sentinel) but nothing temporal, so “when did this seat gain its challenger” is answerable only by diffing counts around a known run — **which is exactly the bracketing pain HO 642 hit** and why HO 656 had to capture a before-state by hand rather than reading one. **It is a migration and therefore out of scope for any HO that has not budgeted one** (the same rule that disqualified the attempt-counter above). *Close:* `updated_at TEXT` lands via `ensureColumn` with the harvest and the seed writing it, after which the sentinel rows carry their own provenance and a staleness check is one query instead of a run-diff.

- **A name market that resolves to no active roster member is a stale-roster detector — already computed, already discarded (opened HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `favoredMember`'s name-market branch does `roster.find(m => nameKey(m.name) === nameKey(fav.favoriteLabel)) ?? null`: when a market names a **candidate** and no roster row matches, the function has already computed *"this market names someone who is not on this roster"* and **throws it away as a null**, falling back to a party label. On **2026-07-25** that condition went true for `S-ME-2026` on **both** feeds at once — Kalshi (65%) and Polymarket (69%) both naming **Troy Jackson** while the card's roster read Mills and Platner — and stayed true on **every render for roughly two and a half weeks**, on a card whose `last_verified` read a green 2026-07-03. **Scope it precisely: the signal is the NAME-market branch failing to match** (`!favoriteIsParty && favoriteLabel` present, and no roster hit) — **NOT** `favoredMember` returning null generally. An unresolvable **party** market is a normal, first-class state (bare-party is ~65% of matched markets), so gating on the general null would make the detector fire constantly and be muted within a week. Beats `races.last_verified` as an instrument on both axes: it is **derived from an independent feed** rather than curator-set, and it **reads differently on a stale roster** instead of carrying the same-as-success shape (HO 503/506/637).

- **11 contests carry recorded `vote_pct` but no `status='winner'` row — ONE class, not two; the two-class split is FALSIFIED (opened HO 638, rewritten HO 641 on HO 640's measurement).** *Owner:* Code. Surfaced as the third independent bar on the S-ME defect: `backfill:race-challengers` derives general-election challengers from primary **winners**, so a contest with shares but no winner row is inert to it. **HO 639 asserted two classes with opposite fixes — top-two/jungle where a single `status='winner'` row is a category error and the harvest's model is wrong, and ranked-choice as a genuine ingestion gap. HO 640 falsified the first half by measurement.** Across **completed** contest-party `open` contests: **CA 52 total / 48 with winners / 48 carrying exactly TWO winner rows; WA 10 / 3 / 3; and ZERO contests anywhere carry exactly one.** Fifty-one top-two contests are marked correctly with two advancers each — the schema, the ingester and HO 207's two-★ rule all handle top-two properly. The 8 are **not** a model error; **they failed the same way ME and NJ did**, so the 11 are one class: scored, unmarked, and frozen. **The shapes are descriptive, not causal** — 8 contest-party `open` (CA-04/14/16/43, WA-05/07/08/09) · **2** ME ranked-choice (`senate-ME-2026-D` and `senate-ME-2026-R` are separate `primaries` rows; HO 639 counted them as one, which is exactly where its phantom "2 uncharacterized" residue came from) · **1** `house-NJ-09-2026-R`, an ordinary partisan primary — two candidates, Pino 51.5%, a derivable winner, no top-two or RCV excuse. A **fourth shape rides inside ME**: `senate-ME-2026-R` has **one candidate, Collins at 100.0%** — uncontested, so there is no votebox contest to mark, which is precisely why the naive guard fix is wrong (see the freeze parent). **Product impact is 5, not 11:** only NJ-09, WA-05, WA-08, ME-D and ME-R sit on rated seats the harvest reaches; the other 6 are unrated and it never reaches them regardless of status. Measured on the harvest's **real** join — `state` + `chamber` + `CAST(district AS INTEGER)`, gated by `EXISTS(race_ratings for the cycle)` — **not** `primaries.race_id`, which is what HO 640's own first pass used before re-measuring 11 → 5 (oddities). **This line is now a SYMPTOM. Its parent is the `isSettled` freeze, above.** **HO 656 — 7 OF THE 11 RECOVERED, 4 RESIDUAL, AND THE COUNT IS NO LONGER THE POINT.** Recovered: CA-04, CA-14, CA-16, CA-43, NJ-09, WA-07, WA-09. Residual: **WA-05, WA-08** (Aug 4, top-two, 12 and 6 candidates) and **ME-D, ME-R** (Jun 9) — every one of them **live-verified as unmarked UPSTREAM** (`status=ok`, `winners=0`), so none is a parse gap. `senate-ME-2026-R` (Collins, uncontested, 100.0%) remains the expected-and-correct residual this entry already carved out. **The line is superseded as a COUNT** — HO 656 observed two fresh members join the class mid-run, so “11” was a snapshot of a moving set, not a fixed backlog. Track the mechanism on the freeze parent; this symptom stays only for its shape analysis (top-two two-winner marking is CORRECT, RCV and uncontested are structurally unmarkable). **HO 661 — the class SELF-HEALS within N going forward:** a contest that takes shares before its call now stays write-eligible for 30 days, so a since-arrived winner lands on the next tick that reaches it instead of never. The figures in this entry remain dated snapshots of a moving set, and the structurally-unmarkable shapes (RCV, uncontested) still never gain a winner — they now settle by expiry rather than by scoring. **HO 686 — the 4 residual are now 2, and the self-heal claim above is CONFIRMED on the pair that could use it.** `WA-05` and `WA-08` **recovered**: both now carry **two** winner rows (WA-05 Baumgartner 47.4% R / Conroy 18.9% D; WA-08 Schrier 53.8% D / Meline 15.9% R), stamped **2026-08-25**, i.e. **10 days before** their 2026-09-04 floors — a since-arrived call landing inside the 30-day window, which is exactly the mechanism HO 661 bought and the first time it has been observed working end-to-end. **`ME-D` and `ME-R` remain and are now long expired** (2026-06-09, settled by expiry since early July, ~86 days past floor at this reading) — unchanged in kind, still the RCV and uncontested shapes this entry already carved out as structurally unmarkable, so **nothing is owed on them and the expiry is the designed outcome, not a residue to chase**. Read via `scripts/diagnostic/settle-predicate-661.ts` class 2, which lists exactly `house-AZ-03-2026-R` + the two ME rows.

- **There is no ingestion path for a convention nominee — S-ME was hand-seeded and the next one will be too (opened HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. Every automatic route into `race_candidates` is primary-derived (`backfill:race-challengers` harvests `primary_candidates` winners), and a **ballot-vacancy / convention replacement never appears in a primary result** — Jackson was nominated at a 2026-07-25 party convention *after* the June 9 primary had already resolved to a different candidate who then withdrew. So the only way a `nominee` row exists today is a hand edit to `races-seed.json`, and the defect it fixes is invisible until someone eyeballs the card. **Size the demand before building:** how often a 2026 general-election candidate arrives by convention rather than primary is unmeasured. **Note the coupling to the vocabulary decision (HO 638):** `nominee` is a true claim whether or not anything ever writes it automatically, so this line can close as *"permanently hand-curated"* without disturbing the status value — what it must not do is quietly become an argument for collapsing `nominee` back into `won_primary`.

- ~~**Eight class-(C) counting readers: disposition (4) SURVIVED falsification and is HELD on MATURATION — ruled per reader, not a blanket answer (opened HO 636, measured + ruled HO 637).** *Owner:* Corey (a label ruling, see the close criterion), then Code. **HO 636 recorded (4) as "priced and PROVEN VIABLE" off an 85.0%-vs-60.0% week-binning figure. That figure measured binning accuracy for rows ALREADY IN THE SET and never measured SET MEMBERSHIP** — moving the window changes which rows are in the set at all. HO 637 measured membership, and it **survives**: over the current week and three prior (cohort **1,556** stamped rows, 2026-08-11), the rows (4) admits that the shipped clock does not are overwhelmingly **late notice, not staleness** — 0/57/144/157 per window observed *after* the window closed against 3/2/6/12 observed before it opened — and the share of set (2) stale beyond the p90 observation lag (16.01d) is **0.6% · 0.0% · 0.9% · 5.1%**. Corpus-wide, only **52 of 1,556** rows (3.3%) have any action postdating their observed advance, **28** by more than a week. **That rate is a FLOOR, not a steady state:** both sets require the stamp, which begins 2026-05-11, so the staleness ceiling is 92.1 days and the rate can only grow as the stamped population ages. **WHAT HOLDS IT IS MATURATION, which the arc had not looked for.** (4) still requires `stage_observed_at IS NOT NULL`, so a week's count **keeps growing as late observations arrive** (57 of w1's eventual 106 landed after w1 closed) — the current window is always missing its unobserved tail while the comparison window has matured, so **the WoW delta is biased negative by construction**. Settling curve, pooled over seven closed windows: **f(0)=32.7% · f(1)=43.3% · f(3)=63.2% · f(7)=79.7% · f(14)=92.1% · f(21)=95.0%**. Bias floor = `f(N+7) − f(N)`: **N=0 → 47.0pp** (a flat week renders −47% WoW) · N=3 → 23.5pp · N=7 → 12.4pp · N=14 → 3.0pp · N=21 → −0.7pp. Live: w0 155 vs w1 106 is a naive **+46.2%**, but at equal maturity (both m=0) it is 155 vs **49**, **+216%** — understated ~4.7x. The shipped clock has **no maturation term** (window and stamp are the same event, f(0)=100% by construction); that is the property (4) gives up. **THREE WAYS OUT WERE PRICED AND ALL THREE REFUSED — inherit the refusals, do not re-derive them.** (a) **Settling lag N=14** (3.0pp bias, 92.1% in hand) or **N=21** (−0.7pp): **REFUSED by owner ruling** — it buys a small residual on a strip then labelled for a week that ended a fortnight ago, and the dashboard's claim is that it answers what is happening NOW; a 14-day-stale live signal is a different product, not a tuned one. **This is not a parameter to re-tune.** (b) **(4d) disclose-don't-delay** — keep the window current, mark the week still settling: **REFUSED ON THE MEASUREMENT.** Completeness at close ranges **3.9%–70.3%, an 18.0x spread, median 34.2%**, so one caveat cannot describe the number (w4 would have rendered **3** against an eventual **77**); the cause is that observation is **batched, not continuous** — over 56 days, 45 carry any observation and 11 carry none, per-observing-day median 12 against a max of 137 — so the close-day reading tracks sync cadence, not Congress. Its "settled prior-week delta" is not settled either: at any week's close the two halves sit at f(7) and f(14) by construction (−12.4pp floor), and simulated over w0–w5 the shown delta **sign-flips against the settled truth twice** (−4% shown vs +2% true; −15% vs +89%). (c) **HOLD.** **PER-READER RULINGS.** **Group A — four readers, move together or not at all** (`getStageChangesCount` · `getStageChanges` · `getWeeklyBandTransitionLadder` · `getWeeklyBandPriorWeek`): **APPROVED IN PRINCIPLE, HELD on maturity.** HO 636's "`getStageChanges` is separable" was **wrong** — feed and count are co-rendered on three surfaces (`app/changes/page.tsx:57`, `app/welcome/page.tsx:138`, `components/ActivityTicker.tsx:56`) and **arithmetically coupled** via `ActivityTicker.tsx:63`'s `remaining = counts.total - bills.length`. When it moves: hint → `idx_bills_latest_action` in the **same edit**, the forced-hint justification comment at **all four sites** (`queries.ts:6793`, `queries.ts:6835`, `dashboard-lead.ts:51-57`, `report-generation.ts:669-676`), and `cold-start-audit-332.ts:205,207`'s pinned copies reconciled in the same edit. **Group B — `gatherLeadData`'s two reads: DECLINED** (recorded as declined, not left open — the lead is orphaned since HO 244; see the orphaned-lead-path entry below). **Group C — the "two reads" are NOT a pair:** `report-generation.ts:676` is a named-calendar-week count (already `date(...) BETWEEN ? AND ?`), while `:720`'s `MIN(date(stage_observed_at))` is `stageTrackingStart`, a statement **about the observation clock** that must never move; moving `:676` alone would also leave that guard insufficient (**118 cohort rows, 7.6%, actions spanning 2025-01-23..2026-05-07** would count as transitions in weeks that predate tracking). **`:676` STAYS anyway, on maturation not the guard** — the report generates Monday 09:30 UTC for a week that closed ~9.5h earlier (maturity ≈0.4d), so (4) would **freeze ~33–43% of the week's movements into `reports.content_md` and never revisit them**, where a live reader self-corrects. **The split stands as analysis; the move does not happen.** *Close:* an owner ruling on the strip's LABEL (a "7 days ending N days ago" strip is a different product — that decision, not a bias threshold, is what unblocks Group A), **or** a change that makes observation continuous rather than batched, which is what would lift f(0). *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/stage-count-settling-637.ts` (settling curve + (4d) simulation) and `scripts/diagnostic/stage-count-anchor-637.ts` (the membership sets); both read-only, one bounded cohort read each. **Do not reopen (4) on the 85%/60% figure alone — it does not speak to membership or maturity.**~~ — **CLOSED HO 654** (`12ee9c9`), and it closes at **ZERO readers still held, not seven.** The handoff assumed one reader would be dispositioned and seven left queued; reading the entry says otherwise. **Group A's four close together** under **disposition (4c)** — decline to re-source, disclose, stay on the observation clock — because *move together or not at all* governs PREDICATE moves and HO 654 moves no predicate, so disposing one of a coupled four and leaving three had no basis. **Group B's two were already DECLINED at HO 637** (“recorded as declined, not left open”). **Group C's two were already ruled to stay** (`report-generation.ts:676` on maturation, `:720` as `stageTrackingStart` which must never move). 4 + 2 + 2 = 8, all dispositioned. - ~~**14 UNTRACKED files in `scripts/diagnostic/` sit inside `tsconfig.json`'s `include`, so local `typecheck` can red-light on files a fresh clone never sees — local is STRICTER than CI, and that direction is the whole point (opened HO 669).** *Owner:* Code. `tsconfig.json` includes `scripts/**/*.ts` with `strict` + `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, and `exclude` is `["node_modules"]` only. `scripts/diagnostic/` currently holds **129 tracked** files and **14 untracked** ones — the design mocks' sibling habit, and `docs/handoffs/` is gitignored the same way (see the handoffs entry). **Demonstrated live at HO 669, which is the evidence:** the WATCH probe `scripts/diagnostic/sync-lead-watch-669.ts` was written, never staged, and **failed `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck` with two `TS2532` errors** under `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` — a gate on the HO 669 chore went red for a file that is not in the commit, not on the branch, and invisible to CI. **State the DIRECTION, because it decides how much this matters:** the risk here is a **false RED locally**, not a false green in CI. CI clones the repo, so it typechecks only tracked files and cannot be fooled by an untracked probe; the failure mode is local noise that looks like the change under test breaking, and the cost is misattributed debugging time on a chore whose own gates are supposed to be clean. **The tracked half of this class is already recorded and is the opposite hazard** — SKILL `:2332` (HO 485) covers a probe that is *committed* and breaks the Vercel build. That inverts (c) from "require probes to typecheck" into "finish the convention already in force." **Option (b) is refused with the file list as evidence** — excluding the directory blinds **126 tracked** files including every standing instrument SKILL names (`cold-start-audit-332`, `delete-rebuild-erosion-566`, `participation-falsify-595`, `hydration-clock-harness-590`, `layout-audit-606`, 11 more), which is the SKILL `:2332` objection made concrete. **The DIRECTION this entry insisted on stating is now measurable:** CI checks out a fresh clone, so it typechecked **185** `scripts/` files while this box typechecked **214** — that gap *was* the bug. Criterion met **per class**: **invisible → destined-for-CI** for the tracked location, **impossible** for `scratch/`. Reproduction ran both, byte-identical `TS2532`: tracked → `BUILD EXIT=1` + `TYPECHECK EXIT=2` naming the file; scratch → both exit 0 **while the identical defect sat on disk**, which is what proves the exclusion rather than the file's absence is doing the work. **Second finding, closed for free:** six untracked probes were cited as the instrument behind a recorded finding while existing on one machine — SKILL named `welcome-read-budget-670.ts` as the source of the 94,240-row `/welcome` price and a fresh clone could not run it. **One thing this entry could not see about itself:** the exposure fired three times (HO 669/670/671) and **was never visible between firings**, which is why three sessions each read it as a one-off.

Firing count now **3** (HO 669's WATCH probe, HO 667's 479 diagnostic coupling, this), all local false-REDs on clean work rather than false-greens in CI. *Close criterion EXTENDED:* whatever lands must make an untracked probe's type error either **impossible or visible** — not merely tolerable. A fix that only silences it (widening `exclude` so probes are never checked) trades a false RED for a real blind spot, since a probe that is later committed then breaks the Vercel build (SKILL `:2332`, HO 485) — so the two halves of the seam have to be closed together.

If it stays large, the finding is the harvest, not a missing detector.

  > **Restated (HO 659).** The harvest-currency parent closed at HO 656 and the harvest has run since (2026-08-13), so the 642-era figures this entry carried — 88/186 exhibits; 129 zero-roster, 62 derivable, 67 not — are **superseded**: post-run zero-roster is 36, bucketed and summing (28 future primary · 2 incumbent-only · 2 `isSettled` freeze · 3 empty-roster · 1 too-recent). **Trigger:** after HO 660's first *scheduled* fire, re-measure M3 — against a cron-maintained harvest, not a decaying one-off. **THE TRIGGER FIRED 2026-08-14 (confirmed HO 662: `lastRunAt` `2026-08-14T12:30:49.466Z`, `success`, on the `30 12 * * *` schedule), so the re-measure is DUE, not pending.** **Read the number through the buckets first:** the 656 classes are expected residue, not defect; split the exhibits by bucket before calling the shape. **Build the detector only if the remainder beyond the buckets holds the ME shape (a handful).** If it stays large past that discount, the finding is the data, not a missing detector. Calibrating against a stale harvest bakes staleness into the baseline (642 R3) — which is why the trigger waits for the maintained regime.

  > **RE-MEASURED HO 663 (2026-08-14, HEAD `f83f439`) — NO-GO on the detector, and the reason is that the ME-shape remainder is EMPTY, not merely small.** Measured against the maintained regime the trigger required: newest sentinel stamp **`2026-08-14T12:30:50.466Z`**, one distinct stamp across **206 rows / 147 seats**, reconciled to the `cron_runs` row `started_at` `2026-08-14T12:30:49.466Z` `success` 2003ms — the `30 12 * * *` scheduled fire, read from `cron_runs` directly rather than inherited. **Rated set 187 · zero-roster 36 · M3 exhibits 8** (down from 88 of 186 at HO 642 — the figure this entry was opened to prevent building against). **The 8 bucket and SUM, no unnamed remainder:** **1** future primary (`S-AK-2026`, votes 2026-08-18, 16-candidate open field, no winner — a name market ahead of a primary is normal) · **2** winners exist but all are the incumbent, correctly excluded (`NC-07-2026` Rouzer, `S-SD-2026` Rounds — the 656 instances, unchanged) · **1** past-dated, fully scored, uncalled upstream (`WA-05-2026`, voted 2026-08-04, 12/12 scored, 0 winners) · **3** past-dated with an EMPTY roster (`ME-02-2026`, `NY-02-2026`, `NY-22-2026` — `cands=0` on both party rows; HO 564 territory) · **1** too recent to be called (`WI-06-2026`, voted 2026-08-11, three days before the run) · **0** ME-shape remainder. **1+2+1+3+1+0 = 8.** *Promote-when* is therefore **not met and cannot be met by this population**: the entry's own rule builds only if the remainder beyond the buckets holds the ME shape, and the remainder is zero — every exhibit is ordinary pipeline state, so a detector calibrated here would fire exclusively on contests that have not voted, incumbents who won their own primaries, and rosters awaiting an upstream call. **This entry stays OPEN as a WATCH, not closed:** the population is a moving set (28 of the 36 zero-roster seats are future-dated, 21 of them on 2026-08-18 — 18 FL House seats plus `S-AK`/`S-FL`/`S-WY`), so an ME-shape remainder can appear as those resolve. **(a) RE-ARM PREDICTS NO ZERO READ, and nothing above should be read as forecasting the exhibit count decaying to nothing.** Buckets 2 and 4 are **standing populations, not transients**. An incumbent who wins their own primary stays correctly excluded by the harvest for the remainder of the cycle, so **bucket 2 persists by construction**; the past-dated empty rosters are tracked in **the HO 586 M5 entry above** (77 of 560 past-dated house rows carrying 0 candidates — `ME-02-2026` is named among its 8 both-party-empty district holes, and `NY-02-2026`/`NY-22-2026` are the same population), the **HO 564 class**, and that debt is **not drained by this re-measure**, so **bucket 4 persists until it is paid**. Bucket 1 does not vanish either — it *converts*, into a filled roster where the contest rosters and resolves, or into bucket 4 / bucket 5 where it does not. **The re-arm question is therefore the SHAPE of the remainder, never the size of the total:** re-run `scripts/diagnostic/roster-coverage-642.ts`, re-bucket, and **read bucket 6 alone**. *Re-arm date:* after the September primaries close — last future-dated zero-roster contest is `S-DE-2026`, 2026-09-15. **(b) A gated detector build INHERITS the bucket-2 spec decision, and the rule is ROSTER-STATE-INDEPENDENT — an empty roster is NOT the trigger condition.** Before alarming, the detector must resolve the market's favorite label against **incumbent + challengers** — the set `resolveKalshiFavoriteParty` takes (`lib/race-colors.ts`), i.e. the seat's full roster — and **never** against the `race_candidates`-derived roster that `favoredMember` (`lib/race-matchup.ts`) resolves within. **The incumbent lives on the RACE ROW** (`races.incumbent_bioguide_id` → `members`), **not in `race_candidates`**, because the harvest excludes an incumbent-winner by design (`pc.bioguide_id <> r.incumbent_bioguide_id`) — so any resolution keyed on `race_candidates` alone cannot see the very person the market named, **at any roster size**. `NC-07-2026` (Rouzer) and `S-SD-2026` (Rounds) are the worked cases and both happen to carry an empty roster, but the carve-out is not conditioned on that: a seat with a fully populated challenger roster whose market names the incumbent is the identical false positive. A detector without it reproduces HO 642's false-positive baseline on a smaller corpus, which is the exact failure this entry was opened to prevent. **(c) Bucket 3 is WINDOW-OPEN, not a freeze** — the HO 656 label *"the `isSettled` freeze class"* no longer describes it and should not be carried forward. Under HO 661's predicate `WA-05-2026` is past-dated with no winner and inside the 30-day window (floor 2026-07-15), so it is **write-ELIGIBLE through 2026-09-03** and will take the call when Ballotpedia marks one; only on **2026-09-03** does it expire into the frozen state. That date is the later of the two HO 661 settle-window WATCH dates — read that WATCH and this bucket as the same row.

It was deliberately NOT removed there: dead-CSS removal is an orphan sweep and orphan sweeps do not ride inside a behaviour commit. **(HO 650) MEMBER 1 OF 3 IS DONE, and this entry deliberately stays OPEN.** `DashboardTopicTreemap` was deleted at `ded820a`. The handoff proposed striking this line as subsumed; that would have been wrong, because the other two members are untouched — **`V2FeedList`'s non-v2 renderer arm** (an intra-file dead branch, invisible to a file-level reachability check by construction) and the **`.home-grid:has(.breaking-row:hover)` CSS rule**, still filed as a CLAIM rather than a verified orphan and still owing its positive-control check.~~ — **CLOSED HO 664 (`33a4e78`), all three members landed.** Members 2+3 in one chore: the three wrapper else-arms + their `variant` plumbing stripped, `components/TopStallsList.tsx` deleted (zero callers post-strip), and the `.home-grid:has(.breaking-row:hover) .home-col-right` rule excised. Member 1 was HO 650 `ded820a`. **MECHANISM CORRECTED WHILE EXECUTING:** the “non-v2 fallback renderer arm” is **not in `V2FeedList`** — that component has no fallback. It was the else-arm of each of the **three server wrappers** that choose the renderer (`ActivityTicker`, `TopStalls`, `NewThisWeek`); the half of the claim that held is that `app/page.tsx` is their sole caller and always passed `variant="v2"`. **The CLAIM filed at HO 642 is now VERIFIED:** `.home-grid` and `.home-col-right` read **0** against a `.breaking-row` positive control reading **7**, with `.home-quadrant.home-breaking-panel` KEPT as the worked negative control (7 and 1, both live). **CSS scope was WIDER than this entry and the handoff both expected — the “Tailwind utilities only, no globals.css excision” expectation was FALSE.** Deleting `TopStallsList` orphaned two further blocks, ~69 lines: `.top-stalls-row*` (HO 126 row + HO 164 accordion rules) and `.bill-chip*` (the HO 125-derived chamber-tinted pill), plus the adjacent `.home-col-stack`, which sat in the same HO 178 block as `.home-grid` with 0 consumers and rode the same excision. `.row-chevron`'s base rule was KEPT (still live on `BillRow`); only its `.top-stalls-row` descendants died. **TOMBSTONE — `.bill-chip`:** written as a reusable primitive “available for any future surface that wants a chamber-tinted bill-id pill” and never gained a second consumer in ~540 handoffs. Excised at HO 664 on the zero-renderers criterion — a comment is not a caller — with the reasoning recorded at the site; resurrect from git history if a surface ever wants the pill. The chamber-tint concept survives in `--rail-house` / `--rail-senate` via `BillIdRail`. **Gates:** typecheck + build green; built-CSS 0 for every removed selector with all four controls >0; localhost 200 with the feed renderer and all three tabs intact.
- ~~**Absence-band payload — slice the prefetched card down to what renders (opened 2026-08-07, HO 630).** *Owner:* Code. **Filed off HO 630's no-LIMIT correction**, which is the whole reason this exists: reconciling the cost probe against the shipped helpers showed `getSponsorRecentBills` has **no LIMIT** (the 7-row cap is render-only in `SponsorExpandedPanel`) and `getSponsorTopTopics` counts in JS over the full raw row set rather than aggregating in SQL. So every absent member's prefetch carries that member's ENTIRE sponsored-bill set into the RSC flight payload to render seven rows. **Two moves, and the first is the safe one:** slice `expansion.recentBills` to the render cap at the BAND's assembly site — **consumer-side, shared helper untouched**, because `/members` is the other consumer and its own cap is `Infinity` on committees and 7 on bills; changing `getMemberCardExpansion` would reach both (the HO 507 shared-component rule). Then consider an SQL-side count for `getSponsorTopTopics`. **Trigger: band membership grows past a handful** — at today's 2 members the whole prefetch is 236 rows/regeneration and the payload is not the problem it becomes at ten. *Close:* the slice lands, or the trigger is re-priced against a measured payload delta.~~ — **SHIPPED HO 631** (`fa87cdf`), **and the saving is not where this entry expected to find it.** The slice landed exactly as filed — consumer-side at the band's assembly site, `getMemberCardExpansion` untouched, `/members` unreached — but the **served document does not move**, and that is structural rather than a failed slice: the card is a SERVER component, so only its RENDERED output serializes, and HO 631 commit 1 had already capped the render at 10. Verified **by id** (`119-s-476`, `119-hr-5868` absent from the pre-slice document), because a byte total can coincide. The saving lands on the value `getAbsenceWatch` **caches**: **16 rows, 15,481 bytes, 44% of the bills payload**, realised on the next regeneration rather than at promotion (the Data Cache survives a deploy). **This entry's own trigger clause was wrong, and in the direction that would have deferred the work**: it read “not the problem it becomes at ten” at today's 2 members, and the handoff carried a matching “12→10 and 9→9” expectation taken from the mock's **illustrative** Wilson dataset. Live, Wilson carries **24** bills — so the two-member band already sheds 44%, and the policy the owner extended off it is that **illustrative mock data never sets a numeric expectation for a build**. **The second move is NOT done and is named rather than dropped:** the SQL-side count for `getSponsorTopTopics` (still counted in JS over the full raw row set) was filed here as a “then consider” and was not taken — re-open it if a topic-count cost ever surfaces in the ledger. Corrected rationale: roadmap, HO 631 block. **Trigger re-priced HO 645 — see the WATCH below; the clause about "ten members" fired at TWO.**
- ~~**`getPrimaryForRace` → prefer the soonest future contest (HO 560 §3.1).** The member-hub primary chip reconstructs `senate-{ST}-2026-{party}` literally, so it can't see `-special-` and keeps showing SC's June 9.~~ — **SHIPPED HO 576** (`e3cf396`), **but not as a soonest-future preference.** STEP 0 (M3a) **proved that query wrong** — with no `district` restriction it leaked per-district house rows and would have "fixed" 127 nulls with arbitrary district dates — and surfaced that the caller is **chamber-blind** (`district=null` for House *and* Senate members), so "prefer the soonest future" would hand a House member a Senate-only special that isn't theirs. Shipped instead as a query over structured columns with a **`memberChamber` argument** and the rule *a Senate special is never a valid proxy for a House member's primary in any state* (**no `p.chamber=?` in the WHERE**, so no House chip emptied). - ~~**Member-hub Press tab has no out-link (HO 509–510)**~~ — **SHIPPED HO 512 (`af3ab90`).** `/news` gained a `?member=` mode and the Press tab its `View press coverage →` out-link. **The close-path's mechanism claim was WRONG, not just imprecise** — recorded so a reader who finds the old wording elsewhere knows which is right: `getNewsFeed` does **not** join `observation_entities`; it drives `news_mentions m INNER JOIN bills b`, so a person-filter bolted on there would have returned only the member's coverage that *also* matched a bill — a quietly short subset, not an error. The fix **branched to `getMemberNews`** (the exact query the Press tab previews) with **zero `lib/queries.ts` changes**. **Precedence:** when `member` parses it owns the view (other params ignored + not carried; ✕ → bare `/news`; `getMember` null → raw-id label + empty state, not a 404; parse-fail → unscoped feed). **Filter-bar asymmetry:** member mode renders the member pill only (observation rows have no source/topic/window/signal dims); bill mode keeps its chips (still `news_mentions`). **Cap asymmetry:** tab previews `getMemberNews(…, 8)`, out-link serves `NEWS_MEMBER_CAP = 50` — the out-link lands on *more* rows than the tab, so **no count in the label**. See roadmap "Also (HO 512)" + oddities. Docs: HO 513.

- ~~**The `bills_agg` sponsor aggregate — the largest single read consumer on the database, untouched (HO 602).**~~ — **RESOLVED BY PROBE, HO 624** (`scripts/diagnostic/bills-agg-cost-624.ts`, on main at `55529d2`). **The 110M / 26% attribution was the PRE-595 statement, not `bills_agg`.** `participationAggCte()` full-scanned `member_votes` *inside the same query text* as `bills_agg`, and the per-query ledger attributes by statement text — so when HO 595 materialized that scan away in July, the dead cost stayed on a trailing 30-day leaderboard read 2026-08-05. **Measured current cost (2026-08-07, 17,463-bill corpus): 20,752 rows per call** for the full `getMembersRanked` statement — component sum 18,617 (`bills_agg` 17,463 + `part_agg` 554 + `members` 553 + `palestine_scorecard` 47), the ~2.1k remainder being ≈4 index seeks per member row across the three LEFT JOINs. That is **16.7× under the ledger's ~347k**, and the coincidence test is decisive: **`|347k − member_votes 365,996| = 18,996`** against **`|347k − measured| = 326,248`**. Plans re-verified covering on both sorts (`SEARCH bills USING COVERING INDEX idx_bills_sponsor_agg`); 14.1ms / 6.2ms, **one sample each**, laptop→us-west-2. **NOT the largest consumer; NOT worth a materialization** — the sketch would add ~1,092 written rows to save a 17k covering scan that reads in single-digit ms. **The congress lever is dead:** all 17,463 sponsored bills are congress 119, so a `congress = 119` clause excludes **zero** rows. *(`includeCeremonial` is not a lever either — both CTE variants read 17,463; the predicate filters rows RETURNED inside the covering scan, not rows read.)* **The probe's own instrument note is worth keeping:** `@libsql/client` v0.14.0 does not surface per-statement `rows_read`, so the probe reads Turso's hrana `/v2/pipeline` directly and *measures* what the ledger meters instead of deriving it — and a **bare `COUNT(*)` under-reports by 227–416×** (366,496 rows → `rows_read: 1617`) because it is answered from B-tree interior pages, so nothing may price a scan by bare count.

  **Variant-surface inventory** — the ledger groups by statement text, so these are **THREE separate lines**; confirm which one any future dashboard read is showing before attributing it: `getMembersRanked` (`queries.ts:6283`, key = filters × sort × page × pageSize — 72 combinations before state/q/page, × 56 states) · `getCommitteeRoster` (`queries.ts:6427`, per committee — 236 × 3 sorts × 2 = 1,416) · `searchMembers` (`queries.ts:7583`, per query string at a 600s TTL, **unbounded by construction**).

  **THE REUSABLE LESSON — date every per-query ledger read against the fixes that landed inside its window.** A killed statement stays on the leaderboard for the length of the window: a 30-day view read 2026-08-05 still contained weeks of executions from a query shape that stopped existing in July, ranked by a cost nothing had paid since. This is the HO 420/577 invalidation class at ledger scale, and it is the measured-number rule one level up — a ledger line is a measurement of `f(query, corpus, window)` being read as a property of the query.

  ~~**STILL OPEN, found in passing (the residual this entry carries forward):** `searchMembers`' lean `bills_agg` copy (`queries.ts:7583`) carries **no `INDEXED BY`** and plans onto `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` as a **non-covering MULTI-INDEX OR** — the HO 277/329 misplan class, live on a request path. Measured **35,129 rows / 594.6ms, 2.01× the hinted plan** (the row-fetch doubling a non-covering OR predicts). Cheap fix — add the hint, mirroring `billsAggCte` — **not done at HO 624, which was read-only.** *Owner:* Code.~~ — **DONE at HO 626 `e9d2aad` (2026-08-07), struck at HO 664.** The hint shipped a week after this line was written, mirroring `billsAggCte`: `FROM bills INDEXED BY idx_bills_sponsor_agg` now sits inside `searchMembers` (`lib/queries.ts` ~:7851) under a comment header naming HO 626 and the HO 624 measurement. Gates were run then and recorded in the roadmap **“Also (HO 624–627)”** block: the plan flipped to `SEARCH bills USING COVERING INDEX idx_bills_sponsor_agg`; full-statement `rows_read` fell across all five probe terms (`smith` 35,129→18,580 · `texas` 35,228→18,679 · `a` 36,594→20,045 · `johnson` 35,126→18,577 · `california` 35,268→18,719), with the hinted CTE standalone at **17,463 — exactly `billsAggCte`'s figure**; equivalence was proven **row-identical on all five**. **The line was never struck at 626** — the third instance of the never-struck class in two sessions (HO 663 struck two). Struck here as HO 664 session-open reconciliation, verified against the tree at `cb52368` (`e9d2aad` confirmed an ancestor of `main`). No code was touched: `lib/queries.ts` was out of scope for HO 664, which only records this.
- **Absence Watch — a front-page strip naming members who have missed a vote threshold (HO 588 STEP 0; filed HO 603).** *Owner:* Code. **State: SHIPPED at HO 622** (`abd8768` data layer · `12b3e57` band), after the HO 621 probe redux re-ran it against a corpus four months newer than the one that scoped it. **THE FORK RESOLVED TO NEITHER ARM — it went to the STREAK, and the ruling is three sentences.** A cumulative trigger makes a **present-tense claim off a career statistic**, and the 621 corpus priced exactly that: **6 sticky false positives to 1 blind false negative** — six House members above 15% cumulative had missed 0–2 of the last 30 roll calls and five of them voted on the final pre-recess roll call, while Biggs (26 of 30 missed, 9.0% cumulative) was invisible to it. **The copy is dated, never "now"** — *no vote cast since {date}* survives a recess, and the House had not voted in 15 days on the day this shipped, so a present-tense phrasing would have been false about that entire chamber the moment it recessed. **The division of labour is acute-vs-chronic**: this band is *acute* (gone), the HO 527 participation dot-plot and the HO 535 MISSED sort are *chronic* (spotty), so a 26-of-30-with-streak-0 member is correctly absent here and correctly present there — do not later "fix" one surface into the other. **WATCH — S = 30 is launch-conservative.** Revisit after a live cycle. A long-empty strip is C4 working, not a broken threshold (the empty state renders nothing at all, by design); the signal that S sits above the corpus is a strip that **never fires across a full ACTIVE session**, which is a different observation from "it is empty today during a recess." **Live at ship (2026-08-07): exactly two rows — McConnell (51 consecutive, no vote cast since Jun 11, 14.1% of 119th) and Wilson (34, since Jul 16, 29.3%)**, the streaks being the true figures past the probe's 30-roll window cap. Cost as shipped: **1,064 rows returned / ~516ms cold**, one regen a day on the `votes` tag. *Superseded below — the fork text is kept because it is the reasoning the ruling overturned:* **THE FORK WAS UNRESOLVED AND WAS RECORDED HERE AS UNRESOLVED:** **R2** = cumulative missed *rate* vs **R3** = a recent window. R2 was preferred in the design conversation as **sticky** (a member cannot fall off the strip by having one good week) and as **near-free**, because it reused `getParticipationStrip` at a marginal read of ~0. **THE COST HALF OF THAT ARGUMENT HAS EXPIRED — this is the reason not to resume from the design conversation.** HO 594/595 rewrote the participation path onto the materialized `member_participation` table, and HO 602 measured that the **pre-materialization `getParticipationStrip` had been 22% of the entire account read budget** (91.3M rows / 30d). *"Reuses `getParticipationStrip`, marginal read ~0"* was a true statement about a function that no longer exists in that form. **Re-derive the cost of BOTH arms against the current materialized path before choosing.** R3's standing merit is unchanged and is what cumulative is blind to: **a member who stopped showing up last month is invisible inside a long denominator.** **Population + delegate carve, copied from `getParticipationStrip` so the strip and the HO 527 dotplot cannot disagree:** `is_current = 1`; `HAVING COUNT(*) >= PARTICIPATION_FLOOR` (50); delegates carved by the territorial predicate `chamber = 'house' AND state IN ('DC','AS','GU','MP','PR','VI')` — **population-correctness, not outlier-trimming** (HO 527: the real high-miss tail is a *voting* member, Wilson at 29%). *Scope:* **re-read `scripts/diagnostic/absence-watch-588.ts` AND ITS OUTPUT before scoping. Do not resume from the design conversation that produced the fork — it predates the materialization.**
- **Dashboard redesign — the two-region declutter (HO 590 mock arc; filed HO 603).** *Owner:* Design + Code. **State: SHIPPED across HO 608–610 (P3).**  Originally: iterated through **revision J** in conversation. **THE MOCK FILE DOES NOT EXIST** — `mock-590-dashboard-declutter.html` is absent from `docs/design/` entirely (not merely untracked; audited HO 603 against both the worktree and `git ls-files`). `docs/design/dashboard-2col.html` is **NOT** it — that is an HO 254 artifact (`f0cec04`, 2026-06-17) with none of this arc's markers. **So this entry IS the artifact; there is no pointer to follow.** Decisions, each of which was *corrected during* the arc and will be re-litigated if not written down: **(1) Two regions** — a full-height bills/movers column on the **RIGHT**, spanning **from the very top, level with the masthead**, NOT starting below the nav; everything else stacks in the left region (masthead, market/odds tapes, nav, absence watch, hearings, week strip, breaking news, distributions). **(2) The tapes stay HORIZONTAL** — they are not converted to a vertical rail. **(3) Iteration on the existing dashboard — NO parallel v3 route**; sequenced handoffs against the live page (the HO 311 `/dashboard-v2` swap is the precedent for why a parallel route accrues a sunset loop). **(4) Row-level packing:** elements pack **left** via flex, because far-right-anchored elements were stretching rows and opening horizontal voids. **(5) Bills-column readability:** no boxed bill IDs, no chip borders, no sponsor underlines; a quiet **two-line** format; colour reserved **EXCLUSIVELY** for stage status (ENACTED green / FLOOR amber / CMTE dim); date-grouped sections **TODAY / YESTERDAY / EARLIER THIS WEEK**. **(6) CUT IN THE ARC — do not re-add:** the **Polarization band** (slow-moving data not worth its space), the **five-column week grid** for hearings (collapsed to a single-day schedule + a week ribbon), and **masthead counts that duplicated the stage-distribution chart**. **SHIPPED ACROSS HO 608–610 (P3).** All six decisions landed against the committed `docs/design/dashboard-layout-target.html`. **Three of them shipped as REFINEMENTS of the decision above, not violations of it, and each is recorded so it is not re-litigated as drift:** **(a) the Polarization band** was CUT as a band (decision 6) but its numbers survive **in miniature** as three chips in the week strip (`.weekly-band-polchip`, HO 608 §5) — the committed mock shows them, and the mock is the later artifact, so "CUT — do not re-add" applies to the full-width band and NOT to the chips; **(b) date-grouped TODAY / YESTERDAY / EARLIER THIS WEEK** (decision 5) was **superseded by the mock**, which has no date groups in the feed — those strings belong to the hearings day bar — and the rows carry a per-row relative age on line 2 instead (HO 609 §0); **(c) the masthead** packs fully left (decision 4) **deviating from the mock**, whose own `.mast` pins SIGN IN right with a `.sp{flex:1}` spacer — that spacer is exactly the C1 pattern, and SKILL owns the conventions, so the mock loses this one (HO 610). The **nav one-row-at-≥1200px target is RETRACTED**, and its shape is the reusable part: **a fixed-width target set before the element moved into a narrower container measures the old container.** The nav moved into `.dash-left` at HO 608, the feed column takes ~432px of the width the target was computed against, and no amount of nav tuning could meet a number derived from the old geometry. If the nav's wrapping is worth fixing it needs a new target measured against `.dash-left`, not the retracted one. **TWO MORE MOCK DEVIATIONS, recorded at HO 623 so the mock's numbers stop being the record where the app has moved past them.** **(d) The feed column is `minmax(480px, 31%)`, not the mock's 400/27%** — an owner ruling; measured, the column reads **779px at 2560 and 480px at its 1440 floor** (from 678 / 400), both under the ~850px C8 ceiling past which a row of text buys nothing from more width. **(e) The `bxp` container query is `max-width: 520px`, not 440** — and the reason is the reusable part, because *the handoff's stated reason for it did not survive measurement*: at 1440 the panel's inner width is **420px**, already below the old 440, so 1440 would have stayed stacked either way. The boundary that actually moves is **1680**, where the inner width is **446px** — under 440 the widened column would have flipped it to wide mode at a cramped 446px. **A container-query threshold is derived from the container it was tuned against, so widening the container obliges you to re-derive the threshold or it silently changes which viewports collapse** — that is the transferable rule, not the two numbers. `/bills` was checked rather than assumed (its panel is 1140px at 1440, far past either threshold), which is the point of a container query over a route branch.
- **Layout audit — the C1–C8 conventions and their M1–M6 instrument (HO 591 origin; filed HO 603; C2 SHIPPED HO 604; audit BUILT AND RUN HO 606).** *Owner:* Code. **State: BUILT AND RUN.** **This entry now reads M1–M6, not M1–M7, and the count change is deliberate: M7 was the C2 blast-radius static measurement, and HO 604 discharged it PRE-BUILD** — its numbers live in the globals.css --fs comment block and arc-md §2, so the audit implements six. `scripts/diagnostic/layout-audit-606.ts` (`e794194`, corrected `94f6543`) is on main and its numbers are in the HO 606 pasted output. C2 shipped at HO 604 (identity `4185325`, ladder `fd069b9`, rider `5b9c34c`). **Still unbuilt: the C1/C3/C4/C7 remediation — P3 onward.** **Read `docs/design/layout-conventions-arc.md` §3 first — it holds the full spec; this entry holds only what must survive independently.** **THREE OF THE EIGHT CONVENTIONS HAVE NO AUDIT INSTRUMENT, BY DESIGN — a decision, not a gap:** C5 and C6 are **review rules** (enforced by a reader, not a script) and **C8 is C1 seen from the other end** (a too-wide row presents as a C1 gap). So the audit prices four conventions, not eight, and phases are done on M1–M4 reaching zero while C5/C6 ride the eyeball pass. **Scoping findings that cost a session to establish and must not be re-derived:** test against **`next start` (a local production build), NOT `next dev`**; **`/` is gated behind a `ct_seen` cookie** (`app/page.tsx`) that must be set context-wide or the crawl measures `/welcome` and reports a clean dashboard it never visited; **M6 needs no browser at all** and is static analysis; and **900px is a BAD single test width** because it is the breakpoint at which the dashboard grid collapses — bracket it (1024/900/720) rather than measuring only at it. **VERDICT CONDITION — and HO 606 is now the worked example, not HO 604: the detector must be proven to fire on a known defect before any zero is trusted.** Pointed at `/`, the first build of M1 read a **10px** interior gap on the breaking row whose headline visibly ends ~1,100px from its timestamp, and M2 read zero; the leg **refused the crawl**, which is the whole reason it runs first. **WHAT A RE-RUN MUST KNOW:** (a) **exclude or dedupe the three redirect aliases** — `/races` and `/primaries` 308 → `/electoral`, `/members/pass-rate` 307 → `/members?sort=passrate` — the script's route list **still contains them** (`layout-audit-606.ts` ~L95–98) and removing them is a **code change deferred to the next audit run**, not a doc sweep; uncorrected they inflate site-wide M1 from **1,329 to 1,902**, and **27 distinct pages is the real denominator**, not 30. (b) **M1b counts INSTANCES, NOT PROBLEMS** — `/members`' 565 is one row component rendered 565 times, so **remediation is priced per component**, and a phase plan that reads the raw count as 565 units of work is reading it wrong. (c) **Raw counts inflate ~1.4×** because one visual gap is counted once per ancestor wrapper; dedupe to the innermost element of an ancestor chain carrying the same gap. (d) **The per-route counts are the cross-run currency; selector paths are human pointers only** — remediation renames classes, so a cross-run comparison keyed on class names goes blind exactly where the fix landed (the HO 604 keyed-diff oddity). **Where the numbers live:** the pasted HO 606 output, and `docs/handoffs/606-artifacts/audit-2560.json` — local-only, repo-ignored, **regenerable by re-running**, so do not treat it as a historical record. **PHASE PLAN off the table:** P3 is `/` against the committed `dashboard-layout-target.html` (+ the `dashboard-classic` removal, now cheap per the corrected M6); **P4 opens `lobbying` then `members`** — `lobbying` because 78 of its 88 are **M1a** repeated-row anchors, the canonical C1 defect and the cleanest test of whether the fix generalises, and `members` because its 565 M1b wide singletons plus the worst narrow-width surface on the site (724/225/1300 overflowing elements at 1024/900/720) make it the C1/C8 case;   **THE MAINTENANCE LOOP — four steps, and they are the price of touching layout.** (1) **Run the fixture legs first**; v4 reads **18 of 18 exact** and a zero is not trusted before they are green. (2) **Label the era on every number**, and never present an instrument note as a remediation claim. (3) **Re-check BOTH identities after any change** — site M1 against the residue register, site M1x against the exemption registry — because they are what "done" means here and a drift in either is a missing line, not a rounding. (4) **Any new `data-viz-row` marking ships with an APPLIED curve and both units** (`M1x <candidates> / <rows>`), and the site total is restated.

  **A KNOWN CAVEAT THE LOOP CARRIES, and it is deliberately NOT another instrument revision.** M4's emitter has **three** false-positive kinds, and v4 closes two of them. It catches (a) short labels in repeated rows — that is **M4f**, split off and counted separately — and (b) short *populated* text generally, which HO 617 named when `/member-detail`'s 2,312px turned out to be 27 populated elements rather than a reserved panel (**M4 counts SHORT-TEXT elements, not empty ones**). The third kind survives v4 by construction: **populated non-repeated chrome**. `/members` reads **M4-true 2 / 84px**, and that 84px is the nav strip and the search box — real content, but not inside a repeated-sibling row, so the furniture clause cannot reach it. **Read a small M4-true figure as "check what it is", not as reserved space.** Widening the furniture clause to cover non-repeated chrome would need its own falsification leg and its own fixture pair; it is not worth an instrument revision for 84px, and the caveat is cheaper than the rev.

  **MAINTENANCE ITEMS, FILED WITH THEIR PIXELS AND NOT FIXED** (found by the terminal crawl; none is a defect the arc owed, and each is small enough that a future HO can take it or leave it). **(a) `/`'s document-overflow chain at 720, +19px** — `home-shell` / `home-main` / `dash-left`, three ancestors carrying the same 19px, which is what most of `/`'s residual bucket-(b) 8 at 720 is. **(b) `/`'s races box at 720, 44px of overflow.** Both are narrow-width only; 1024 and 900 read bucket (b) 0. **(c) `/members`' M4-true 84px**, per the caveat above — a nav strip and a search box, not reserved space. None of the three is on the residue register, because the register is M1 and these are M4/M5.
- **THE `data-viz-row` EXEMPTION REGISTRY (opened HO 610, extended HO 615 / 616 / 617 / 619, made standing HO 620).** *Owner:* Code, standing. **This entry exists so the exemption cannot grow silently — an uncounted exemption is how an audit stops measuring the thing it was built for.** **THE CREEP GUARD, verbatim, and it governs every future marking:** *the attribute marks rows containing a bar or shared-axis encoding, never plain text tables, and every new marking ships with its curve.*

  **THE KIND ANNOTATION (added HO 620, from the HO 617 axis pair) — record what the position ENCODES, because that is what decides the verdict.** Clause 1 asks for "a bar or shared-axis encoding", which reads as *bars only* and is too narrow: **a positional encoding qualifies with no bar in it at all.** The worked example is `.ideology-axis` (6) — a fixed −1..+1 DW-NOMINATE domain with positioned markers, where the **distance between two party medians IS the finding** and narrowing the track costs the encoding linearly. **The discriminator is MAGNITUDE vs SEQUENCE, and HO 617 got opposite verdicts out of the same applied method on the same page:** `.ideology-axis` encodes magnitude and was **marked**; `.v2f-bar`, the stage bar, encodes **sequence** — position says *which step*, not *how much* — so compressing costs the reader nothing, a cap reaches it (live 2486 → 379px worst · 1000 → 132 · 640 → 72), and it was **capped with no attribute spent**, scoped to `.bdp` per SKILL's HO 507 shared-rule guard. **File every marking with its kind (magnitude / shared-axis / sequence), and a `sequence` reading is a presumption AGAINST the attribute.** **The metabox outcome, recorded because it is the other direction:** `/bill/[id]`'s three topic chips were argued at HO 619 as exempt-by-context (a metabox *field* is not a repeated row, and C3's noise target is per-row multiplication) — **that argument was true and was not what the rule asks.** The rule asks what the chip *does*; they are labels, and HO 620 (`25493bd`) made them plain. **A context argument is not a kind argument, and only a kind argument reaches this registry.**

  **THE REGISTER — seven marked elements, each with the measurement that justified it:**
  **(1) `StageFunnel`'s `ul` (HO 610).** The dashboard stage funnel. A bar row is label · bar · value-on-a-shared-axis, so the space between a short bar and its number is the encoding. **M1x 13 / 6 visual rows** on `/` (and the same 13 / 6 on `/?stage=committee`, the same component) — the six stage rows plus their `li` wrappers plus the `ul`. Filed in both units retroactively at HO 616, when the caveat below was written.
  **(2) `FirmsLeaderboard`'s table (HO 615, `8a05ad4`) — CURVE CORRECTED HO 616; the exemption stands.** Void: between the firm NAME and **the bar column's fixed origin at x=2107**, 476–699px, median 600, on **25 of 25** rows. That origin is what makes the bars comparable at all.
    **THE HO 615 CURVE WAS MODELLED AND ITS KEY CLAIM WAS FALSE.** It read *"cap 260px clips 2 names and leaves 25 of 25 over threshold; cap 300px clips 0 and leaves 25 over — a cap cannot reach it."* It was computed from track arithmetic — subtract each label's own excess over the cap — which reports *no change* for any cap wider than the longest label. That misses the actual effect: capping a `1fr` label track **moves the fixed 150px bar track left**. **Applied and re-measured (HO 616):** cap 180px → **worst 114px, 0 of 25 over, 10 of 25 names clipped** · cap 220 → 154px, 3 over, 5 clipped · cap 260 → 194px, 7 over, 2 clipped · cap 300 → 234px, 15 over, 0 clipped. **A cap CAN reach it.**
    **The exemption is kept on the honest numbers, not the old ones.** The reachable cap is 180px, and it clips **10 of 25 firm names — 40%**. Against this arc's knees (`/members` 16 of 537 = 3%, `/trades` 4 of 1,209 = 0.33%) that is not an acceptable knee, so clause 2 is satisfied by the corrected curve rather than by the wrong one. Clause 1 was never in question: it is a bar chart on a shared origin.
  **(3) `TopicCrosswalk`'s table (HO 615, `8a05ad4`) — CONFIRMED HO 616 by the applied method.** Void: between **the bar's painted end and its right-aligned value**, 37px on the widest topic to 648px on the narrowest, median 522 — a short bar with a long run to its number IS the small number.
    Re-measured by applying each cap in the page rather than modelling it, the verdict is **unchanged and stronger**: live worst 648px / 22 of 23 over · cap 180 → **worst 873px** · 220 → 835 · 260 → 796 · 300 → 758 · 400 → 662. **Every cap makes it WORSE**, because the bar sits in a fractional `2fr` track: width taken off the label is handed straight to the bar, and the fill is `widthPct%` of whatever that track becomes. The modelled curve happened to reach the right answer here; the applied one explains it.
  **(4) `/amendments` — both `RankRow` tables (HO 616, `780da6d`). M1x 94 / 30 visual rows.** Most-amended bills and Top amenders, 15 rows each. Applied curve: live worst 652px, 27 of 30 over · cap 200 → **917px**, 26 over, 13 clipped · cap 320 → 828px · cap 400 → 753px, 27 over, 10 clipped. Same fractional-`2fr` mechanism as (3): every cap is worse. Marked on each `div.border` table.
  **(5) `/nominations` — the agency `RankRow` table (HO 616, `780da6d`). M1x 65 / 15 visual rows.** Applied curve: live worst 1,347px, 15 of 15 over · cap 200 → **2,071px**, 14 over, 8 clipped · cap 280 → 1,998px · cap 400 → 1,888px, 15 over, 0 clipped. Every cap is worse. Marked on the `div.border` table.
    Both share `components/RankRow.tsx`, extracted from two near-identical page-local copies in the same commit. **Neither table has a column header today** — the table-not-rows clause below still decided the attribute's placement (on the table, not the `ul`), so a header added later inherits the exemption instead of being remediated off its own axis.
  **(6) `MemberIdeology`'s `.ideology-axis` (HO 617, `cd8c3d7`). M1x 4 / 2 visual rows.** Kind: **magnitude**, and the first marking with **no bar in it** — a fixed −1..+1 DW-NOMINATE domain with positioned markers, end labels marking the domain's ends, and the party-median spread as the finding. Applied curve (axis width → worst gap / scale / party-median spread): 2490 → 2299px / 1195px per unit / 1878px · 1400 → 1209 / 672 / 1055 · 1000 → 809 / 480 / 753 · 700 → 509 / 336 / 527 · 500 → 309 / 240 / 376. **Narrowing the track is the only lever and it costs the encoding linearly** — reaching the 120px threshold needs a ~420px axis, a **6× loss of resolution** on the one distance the rail exists to show. The 4 candidates are the ends row and the track; the container and the rail are wrappers. Consumers checked: `MemberIdeology` only — `PolarizationBand` is a different class family, so no other route moves.
  **(7) `PatternBars` on `/patterns` (HO 619, `d56608e`). M1x 18 / 6 visual rows** (5 bar rows + 1 header). Kind: **magnitude on a shared origin** — bar **length** is the bill count and bar **colour** is % past committee, in a shared `2fr` track, which is what makes five patterns comparable at a glance. Applied curve, read in the page: the void is the header `Pattern` → `Bills`, spanning the empty cell over the bar column, **781px live and monotonically WORSE at every cap tried** — 600 → 746 · 500 → 846 · 420 → 926 · 340 → 1,006 · 260 → 1,086 · 180 → 1,166 — while clipping **0 of 5** labels, because the bar sits in a **fractional** track that absorbs whatever the label column gives up. **That is the `TopicCrosswalk` shape, not the `FirmsLeaderboard` one: no cap reaches it.** Placement by the table-not-rows clause, because this one **does** have a column header; the bar rows themselves are never over threshold (14px). The arc's last new exemption.
  **(8) `TopicDistributionList`'s `ul` on `/` (HO 627, `3d0d6d1`). M1x 17 / 8 visual rows.** Kind: **magnitude on a shared origin** — the DISTRIBUTION panel un-tabbed (BY STAGE over BY TOPIC, co-visible per the committed mock), and BY TOPIC ships as a ranked bar list whose bars all start at the same x and are scaled to one max. Applied curve, read in the page — void = bar-end → value: **2560 8–395px, median 353** · **1440 8–155px, median 140**, against the `StageFunnel` directly above it in the same panel at **8–488 / median 454** and **8–166 / median 155**. **No cap reaches it, and this is the STRONGEST form of the exemption rather than the `FirmsLeaderboard` kind: the bar is scaled to the track, so the void is PROPORTIONAL TO THE VALUE ENCODED** — a topic at 1/8 of the max *must* leave ~7/8 of its track empty, and capping shrinks track and bar together, preserving the proportion exactly while making the small topics unreadable. The sibling `StageFunnel` marking (1) is the calibration: same shape, same panel, slightly larger magnitude. Placement is the `ul` and not a table, because this list has **no column header** (the `.dist-sub` "BY TOPIC" is a section label above the list, not a header over its columns) — the table-not-rows clause does not bite. Leg B refused the crawl until the curve existed (`HOME_M1X_EXPECTED` 13 → 30), which is the guard working as designed.
  **THE ATTRIBUTE GOES ON THE TABLE, NOT THE ROW LIST** (2) and (3) both — a column header's geometry is not independently choosable, it has to sit over the columns it labels, and both headers were over threshold (742px, 1051px) for exactly that reason. Exempting the rows and remediating the header would pull the labels off their own axis.
  **THE UNIT CAVEAT, and record BOTH numbers for every future marking: M1x IS A CANDIDATE COUNT, NOT A ROW COUNT.** `/lobbying` reads **M1x 125** for what is **50 visual rows** (25 firm rows + 1 header + 23 topic buttons + 1 header); the other **75 are wrappers and tracks** that pass a width-based candidate gate — a 1255px bar track and a firm-name span stretched by its `1fr` column are both ≥600px wide.
