  **FILED AS ADOPTED-UNVIEWED, and that is part of the record, not a footnote.** The eight paired renders under `docs/handoffs/634-artifacts/shots/` were **never opened**. The ISSUES-column loss is the thing this HALTed for and it was ruled **on the written recommendation, not by eye**. A later reader must know which kind of decision this was: the shots stay on disk as the **unexercised record**.
  **(i) THE HEADER LINE, RESTATED ON THE FOUR-TERM FORM — WITH ITS PRECONDITION IN THE LINE, NOT A FOOTNOTE.** The header-axis gap is **`gap = track_A − ink_A + column_gap + inset_B`**, where `inset_B` is the next cell's ink offset inside its own track (0 when left-aligned, `track_B − ink_B` when right-aligned). **VALID ONLY WHERE `inset_A = 0`** — the label's own ink flush to its own track's left edge; where it is not, the outgoing gap is not four-term and the form OVER-PREDICTS. Live counterexamples, measured: `.mc-r-num` on `/members` (`inset_A` 21 / 20) and AMOUNT on `/trades` (`inset_A` 139) — the form gives **52 where the surface reads 32**. All three header-axis entries close **EXACT, delta 0**, measured at `1e6435a`, fixture legs 18/18 green first: `/members` **209** (240 − 43 + 12 + 0) and **230** (320 − 122 + 12 + 21) · `/trades` **196** (80 − 35 + 12 + 139) · `/lobbying` **215** (340 − 137 + 12 + 0). **`/trades` is the exhibit for why this is not cosmetic: its `inset_B` is 139px and the old three-term form predicts 57 — off by more than the 120px threshold itself.** **Two of the four terms are TEXT METRICS** (`ink_A`, and `ink_B` inside `inset_B`), which corrects HO 633's "cannot be moved by type at all" — see the append-only correction below.
  **(ii) A NEW LINE, `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row` — AND IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT A PX CONSTANT. This is the amendment to the admission standard.** The existing standard (px, class, price, arithmetic restated) assumes a **fixed px** figure, and the fixed-px form is exactly what makes the closing identity checkable arithmetic. **This class cannot have one**, so it is admitted as **class + bound + the statistic behind the bound**: *class* `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row`, data filing rows, NOT header-axis · *bound* **`/lobbying` M1 varies 1–3 per crawl** (header always 1, plus 0–2 data rows) · *statistic* **7 of 81 data rows over threshold pooled across seven filing sets, magnitudes 121–179px**. **The 1–3 bound is measured twice, independently:** the HO 633/634 HALT crawl read per-set M1 3/2/2/3/1/2/1 and the HO 634 close crawl read 1/3/2/2/1/2/1 — same band, different days. *Mechanism:* **`gap ≈ 340 − rendered_ink + 12`**, with the 340 cap fitted at HO 614 to a then-current **minimum** ink of 239; the corpus has since produced registrant→client strings shorter than anything in that sample, and every pixel below 239 goes into the gap. **No commit, no regression — the data crossed under a constant.**
  **(iii) THE PRICE, STATED SO REOPENING IS A PRICED DECISION AND NOT A REDISCOVERY.** `(c′)` — the rc track sized per row and capped at today's 340, `fit-content(340px)` — was **measured, not reasoned**, both modes read in the same page instance against the same live state. It **buys**: over 5 → 1, header gap 215 → 12, and **zero vertical cost** (feed +0px, page +0px, wrapped 63 → 63 — a row wraps iff its natural ink exceeds its track, and that condition is identical under both modes). It **costs**: **ISSUES stops being a column — x-origin spread 0 → 167px at 2560 and 0 → 201px at 1440**, worst at the narrower width because the bound is `340 − min_ink` and min ink shrinks with the type ladder. The header's retirement to 12 is **not a second win**: it retires **because the column it labelled no longer exists**, the same event counted once. And the result is a **partial** column, which is why it was declined by recommendation: **78% of rows (63 of 81) wrap at the bound and hold the old x while 22% (18 of 81) pull left to their own text** — neither a column nor an honest pack. **Residual under `(c′)` is 1 row, and it does not close the class:** that row is the wrap-at-bound case, and its population is **63 rows whose widest rendered line runs 231–338px (spread 107) against the 232px line that decides over-threshold**, on ~24px of margin. **Reopening `(c′)` means re-taking that trade, not rediscovering it.**
  **(iv) SITE M1 ARITHMETIC RESTATED — CITED, NOT SUBTRACTED.** **9 (`/members`) + 2 (`/vote`) + 3 (`/lobbying`) + 1 (`/trades`) = 15.** The `/lobbying` term is **3 = 1 header row + 2 data rows**, read at the crawl this entry cites. **NOTE THE TWO COUNTS THAT ARE EASY TO CONFLATE, because the ruling instruction itself did:** `/lobbying` now carries **2 register LINES** but contributes **3 ROWS** to M1. M1 counts rows. *(The ruling as typed itemised "2 (`/lobbying`)" against a stated total of 15; 9+2+2+1 is 14, and 15 requires the header's row. Written as 3 to match both the stated total and the "15 if they attribute" already on the record — flagged rather than silently picked.)* **The "13 with up to 2 unexplained — 15 if they attribute" line is RETIRED: they attributed, and nothing unexplained remains.** **The `/lobbying` term is itself a sample, per (ii)** — 15 is the figure at the cited crawl under a 1–3 bound, not a constant, and that is the price of admitting a varying class rather than refusing to price it.
  **(v) THE ADMISSION STANDARD GAINS A CLAUSE: A REGISTER LINE PRICES THE ROW'S *WORST* GAP, NOT THE ROW.** Measured, not asserted: `/members` carries **4 over-threshold gaps across 2 header rows** (209 and 230 on each of the two panes) and the register carries **2**. Without this clause an entry reads as "this row's gap", which is false the moment a row has two, and the next reader who re-measures finds a number the register does not carry and mistakes it for drift.
  **(vi) APPEND-ONLY CORRECTION TO HO 633's STATED REASON — HO 633's BLOCK IS NOT EDITED.** HO 633 predicted the `/members` header pair would HOLD at 230 while the knee rows crossed under, on the reasoning that the header-axis class "**cannot be moved by type at all**". **The prediction was right and the stated reason is over-strong:** two of the four terms are text metrics, so the class *can* be moved by type. It held because those two metrics are **short fixed labels rather than corpus data** and the tracks either side are **authored constants**. Prediction and reason are graded separately; only the reason is corrected. Also settled and **not to be chased**: the pair now reads **230 / 231 / 230** across three crawls, all four terms fixed — sub-pixel rounding, not a move.
  **(vii) BOTH ATTRIBUTION WINDOWS ARE RETIRED, AND ARE RECORDED AS RETIRED.** The owner's candidate window (**HO 597–598**) and the reviewer's bound (**after HO 620**) both assume a commit. **Neither contains the change, because the change is not in the code.** Verified by **byte identity**, which is the evidence that actually settles it: the `.lob-filing-row` block in `app/globals.css` hashes to **md5 `bc49313d103615a3f976173dd7791770` at `62c25e4` AND at `1e6435a`** (HEAD at this sweep) — the same bytes, not merely no commit found. Corroborating: `git log 62c25e4..HEAD -- app/lobbying components/FilingRow.tsx` returns **ZERO** commits, and the governing grid landed at **HO 614 `13203eb`** and has not been touched since. **AND THE HANDOFF'S OWN STEP-0 FIGURE IS WRONG, CORRECTED HERE:** HO 634 §Step 0 stated that this log "returns four commits and none of them is lobbying work: three absence-band commits and one `searchMembers` index hint, **all in `lib/queries.ts`**", and instructed the reader to *verify, not re-derive*. **The correct reading is ZERO** — and the claim contradicts itself, since a log filtered to `app/lobbying components/FilingRow.tsx` cannot return commits whose changes are all in `lib/queries.ts`. **A commit-absence argument is the weaker instrument anyway: hash the bytes, don't count the commits.** A count answers "did anyone touch these paths"; a hash answers "are these the same bytes", which is the question actually being asked. The WATCH asked for the two readings to be reconciled rather than assumed, and **"neither applies" is a reconciliation only because it is written down as one.**

- **The padding half of C2 — row padding and fixed-size glyph strips stepping at the type breakpoints (deferred from HO 604, named not dropped).** *Owner:* Code. HO 604 shipped **type only**, deliberately: once the ladder is live you can see which paddings actually need it instead of guessing. **The 2560 eyeball produced its concrete worklist, measured against the pre-arc build at the same width** — each is a fixed-width container that did not scale with the type it holds, not a type bug: (a) **`/lobbying`** — the BY-TOPIC section blurb reflows 1→2 lines (the *only* leaf text element on any crawled route to gain a line), and the TOP FIRMS top-issue label clips harder (20/25 → 23/25) because its column is pinned at `minmax(0,120px)`; (b) **`/`** — the hearings filter bar reflows onto a second row; (c) **`/welcome`** — a feed title goes 1/7 → 2/7 clipped. At **1440 there is no change at all** (0 new wrap, 0 new clipping, 0 computed-size differences vs pre-arc), so this is strictly a wide-viewport item. **The non-scaling set that drives it:** ~68 one-off CSS sizes (8 / 8.5 / 9.5 / 10.5 / 11.5 / 12.5 / 15–38) and **34 SVG `fontSize` presentation attributes**, which are user-unit attributes and **cannot take a CSS var at all** — so a 20px heading sits over 15px body at 2400 instead of over 12px body and the hierarchy compresses at wide widths. That consequence is **recorded in the `globals.css` ladder comment as bounded and real — do not re-file it as a bug.** **Measured nav baseline (HO 606):** `nav.primary-nav.home-header-nav` wraps **1→2 lines at 1024 and 1→3 at 900/720 on every route crawled** — the single most consistent narrow-width finding in the audit. **Nav compaction is P3 scope** (the arc moved it there from the old P1); this is its baseline. *Gate:* run after the audit (HO 606) so padding is fixed against measured rows rather than guessed ones.

That residue splits **27 class 2** (the number IS the evidence for the claim it sits in — a historical incident, a measured justification for a design decision — priced as: stays, carries a date, no other action), **18 class 3**, which is the only class that is work, **and 1 instrument artifact — line 2435's `+93.5`**, which is the handoff number HO 93.5 written with a `+` prefix instead of `HO `, so the sub-number filter (which only looks for `HO ` / `handoff ` immediately before a token) did not catch it. **It did NOT turn out to have a home on hand-reading** — the class-1 test never saw it, because the TOKENIZER emitted something that is not a measurement at all; 27 + 18 + 1 = 46 and the first draft of this entry reported 45, having dropped the artifact from the arithmetic silently. Class 3 splits again, and the halves want different handling: **8 are layout constants** (`1715` 56fr/1199px · `1719` 264px · `1720` 430px · `1725` 384/650/210px · `1735` 110/70px · `2026` 126px/0.85 · `2030` 1000/360px · `2338` 430px) whose home is the CSS; **10 are corpus or behaviour figures** (`1050`+`1131` 240s carves · `1124` ~2,700 · `1135` ~468 rows · **`1550` ~16,289 — the clearest single case, a raw corpus size with no home** · `1580` 235 rows · `1581` ~12% · `1587` 60 rows · `1588` ~97 rows · `1589` ~99%). **NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED** — the finding is which figures are structurally exposed, not which have gone stale; re-measuring turns a bounded classification into an unbounded audit. **THE INSTRUMENT CAUGHT ITS OWN DEFECT CLASS TWICE WHILE BEING BUILT, and that is evidence about the class rather than something to bury.** Both bugs were exactly the too-loose-pattern failure this probe exists to detect, occurring inside the probe: (1) the first reachability graph counted `import type` edges, which are erased at compile time, and reported the known orphan `DashboardTopicTreemap` as live — the direction that reads as a clean bill of health; (2) this M2 extractor matched handoff sub-numbers (`HO 112.2`, `HO 154.1`) as measurements and manufactured **13** false residue lines before the filter was added. **One survives and is named rather than quietly carried: line 2435 above.** A too-loose pattern is not an occasional slip in this codebase — it is the default failure of every identifier- or figure-matching check written here, and a probe designed against it still shipped two. *Close:* each class-3 line gets a home, a date, or a deletion.
- **SKILL documents a deleted ROUTE and a deleted COMPONENT as live, and the probe cannot see either (found HO 650, out of its scope).** *Owner:* Code. While removing the four dead names, more sites turned out to name things that no longer exist. **L1715 is the `/dashboard-classic` entry, a route whose `app/dashboard-classic/page.tsx` is gone** (removed in the HO 608–610 arc). **`DistributionsTabs` — a component with no file in the tree — is named 4x in SKILL**, at L1715, L1716, L2044, **and L2043**. **L2043 IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, AND IT CHANGES WHAT THIS FINDING MEANS.** That is the line HO 650 CORRECTED (`123e4c3`), and `DistributionsTabs` sits **immediately after** the clause that was rewritten — *“…rendering nowhere), `DistributionsTabs` (**HO 244**, STAGE | TOPIC tab state…”*. So the defect survived **inside a sentence under active repair, in the same commit, read by someone who had just verified two other component names on that exact line** (`DashboardTopicTreemap` and `StateFlag`, both removed from it). **The class is therefore not confined to lines nobody has touched recently — it is invisible to a careful human read, not merely to the probe**, which is a far stronger argument for measuring it than “two stale lines exist” would have been. A name in a list of live components carries no signal that its file is gone: nothing renders differently, nothing fails to compile, and the eye reads it as one more familiar identifier. **AND IT IS NOT A MARKDOWN-ONLY CLASS — `app/globals.css:1563`** carries an HO 244 comment describing the treemap's fixed-height pane and *“only the active pane is mounted (DistributionsTabs)”*: a stale comment about two deleted things, in a file type a docs-oriented check would never open. The third-direction instrument must not assume markdown. **NOT WIDENED AT HO 650, deliberately and on the owner's ruling.** The full documented-and-nonexistent set is UNMEASURED, and editing SKILL against a hand-assembled list is precisely how the L1711-vs-L2044 count contradiction this HO just resolved came to exist. **Enumerate first, in its own HO**, then fix from the enumeration. **THE INSTRUMENT LIMIT IS THE DURABLE PART.** `skill-coverage-649.ts` measures in two directions — *exists and undocumented* (M1) and *documented and unreachable* (the M1 inverse added at HO 649) — and **both start from a file that EXISTS.** A passage describing something already deleted from disk is invisible to both, by construction: there is no file to look up. That is a third direction, *documented-and-nonexistent*, and it is the one that hides the oldest drift, because a file that is gone stops generating any signal at all. Cheap to add: extract the backticked identifiers SKILL claims are live, and check each against the filesystem. *Close:* the third direction is measured, and L1715/L1716 are reconciled against what actually renders.
- **The documented-and-nonexistent set is now MEASURED: 94 phantom mentions over 50 names (HO 651 probe, M1/M2).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/diagnostic/skill-phantoms-651.ts` on `0ee0570`, answering the HO 650 entry directly above. **M1:** 392 backticked identifiers SKILL names → 127 excluded by a `components/<N>.tsx` file, 67 by an export somewhere in source, leaving **198 candidates with no file and no export**. **M2, per MENTION rather than per name** (a name is legitimately RECORD at one line and PHANTOM at another): 344 mentions → **203 NOT-A-COMPONENT** (vocabulary, 135 names), **47 RECORD** (correctly states the thing was deleted/replaced, 27 names), **94 PHANTOM — presented as live — over 50 names.** **20 of the 50 are corroborated by git history**, on evidence owing nothing to any heuristic in the probe (`PHANTOM_GITCHECK=1` finds a recorded `components/*.tsx` deletion): `BillsIntroTimeline` `ColorKeyStrip` `DashboardLead` `DashboardTopicTreemap` `DistributionsTabs` `DualMarketsTape` `EnactedBanner` `HomeHeader` `MediaAttentionCell` `MemberAffiliations` `OnTheHillBand` `PrimariesMap` `PrimaryDistrictCard` `PrimaryDistrictModal` `PrimaryMapCard` `PrimaryRow` `RacesMap` `ShareBar` `SponsorRow` `TopicDistribution`. **THAT CORE RESTS ON THREE CONVERGENT CHECKS, ARRIVED AT BY DIFFERENT METHODS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS CITABLE WITHOUT RE-RUNNING ANYTHING.** (i) the probe's own exclusion — no `components/<N>.tsx`, no export anywhere under `app/ components/ lib/ scripts/`; (ii) git history — a recorded `components/*.tsx` deletion, which owes nothing to the marker regex, the ownership scope or the vocabulary rules; and (iii) **an independent check by the reviewer against the tree** (no file, no export under `app/ components/ lib/`), done separately from the probe and confirming **18 of the 20**. Re-running that third method over all twenty returns the same answer for every one, so the core is **20/20 under two hand-checkable criteria plus the historical record**. A later reader can cite these twenty from the entry; the other thirty need the re-run. **The other 30 are NOT all real, and the list is deliberately unpruned so that is visible** — the residual is dictionary-word UI labels (`Watching`, `Voted`, `Solid`, `Safe`, `Republican`, `Dem`, `Bills`, `Legislation`) and symbols that lived inside another file rather than owning one (`StageKey`, `TopicKey`, `PrimaryExpandProvider`, `SectionShell`). A list already tidied to look sensible cannot be checked, so the rules that fired are emitted per entry instead. *Close:* every PHANTOM mention is repaired or ruled correct. **(HO 652) `### Pages` IS DISCHARGED, and the counts above are now historical — they remain true of `0ee0570` and are NOT restated here.** A re-run measures **77 PHANTOM mentions, down from 94**, with the `### Pages` section going **47 → 30**. The section was repaired as a unit, every clause grounded in the route's own `page.tsx`, one bullet deleted (`/dashboard-classic`, whose removal IS recorded — `1d9c339`, HO 608, and the roadmap names it twice, so no loop closed unrecorded). **The 30 survivors are NAMED RESIDUE, not unmeasured drift, and 25 of them are ONE classifier limitation worth knowing before 653 touches `### Server / client split`:** a single deletion marker serving a LIST of names — *“`A` / `B` / `C` are retired”* — lands in only the LAST name's ownership scope, so the earlier names read PHANTOM while the sentence is a correct record. The canonical-list section 653 inherits is built almost entirely of such lists, so expect its raw PHANTOM count to overstate its real drift by a similar margin, and price the repair from the mentions rather than the count. The remainder: a paragraph-level historical frame the per-mention scope cannot see (`/primaries`), a locally-defined component the probe's own limits section predicts (`ActivityCaption`), and vocabulary. **And the probe's C1 control expired mid-repair, by a route its own C4 did not predict:** it was anchored to SKILL **line 2043**, and deleting one line 300+ lines above shifted the file so the control silently tested a different mention. **A line-number anchor is invalidated by ANY edit above it, not just by a repair of its subject** — which matters directly to 653, since any `### Server / client split` edit shifts every line below it. C1 is now an inline fixture and no longer expires that way. **(HO 653) `### Server / client split` IS DISCHARGED, by DELETION rather than repair.** A re-run measures **73**, down from 77, with the section going **11 → 10** — reconciled, not asserted: 3 lost at L1711, 3 lost with the deleted name list, 2 gained in the demoted changelog, all other 63 unchanged. **The hardcoded snapshot and its name list are gone**; what remains is one unambiguous refresh command plus the residue a grep cannot produce (the 2 hook files; that one file can carry two client exports — `BillExpandPanel` also exports `BillStageBar`; `MobileNavDrawer` dormant; and the 13 server components that LOOK interactive, each verified present and directive-free). The changelog survives DEMOTED, as history of how the set changed rather than a claim about membership. **THE EVIDENCE THAT THE SNAPSHOT COULD NOT BE MAINTAINED IS NOT THAT IT WAS STALE — IT WAS SELF-CONTRADICTORY.** Measured at `f47b807`: `.tsx`-only **67**, `.ts`+`.tsx` **69**, recursive **69**, none under `svg/`; so the line's own “69 matches incl. the 2 hooks → ~67 components” was arithmetically RIGHT. What was wrong is that the same line closed with “the live count is 55 grep matches = ~53 islands, as of HO 328” — **HO 650 corrected the headline and left the tail, so the `~53`-vs-`~67` disagreement never ended, it moved inside one line.** A figure that reads as authoritative while contradicting itself four hundred words later is a stronger argument for deletion than mere staleness, and it is why the refresh instruction had to become an exact invocation: *“grep under `components/`”* admits readings that differ by exactly the two hooks, so a `.tsx`-only reading followed by the documented hook-subtraction double-subtracts. **A NOTE FOR WHOEVER PRICES THE REMAINING SECTIONS:** the 10 survivors are the list-marker artifact, exactly as predicted before the edit — a demoted changelog is built of *“`A` / `B` / `C` were deleted”* sentences and the marker lands only in the last name's scope. Two of them are names this HO ADDED while demoting. **The count going down by 4 while a section was gutted is the correct reading, not a disappointing one**, and any section repaired by demotion-to-history will behave the same way. **Scope 653 FROM THE ENUMERATION, by re-running the probe — not from this line.** Editing SKILL against a hand-assembled list is exactly how the `~53`-vs-`~67` contradiction HO 650 resolved came to exist, and this list is long enough that a hand transcription of it would repeat that.
- ~~**`fit-finish.spec.ts:143` pins a class the v2 feed row stopped rendering (found in passing, HO 654).** *Owner:* Code. The test asserts `a.bill-id-chip` inside `.v2f-group` and it **fails today**; the class is absent from the render entirely (0 occurrences against 30 `.v2f-group`), because `V2FeedList` renders `className="v2f-id"` — its own header records the swap, *“bordered `BillIdChip` → plain amber id text”*. **PRE-EXISTING and NOT caused by HO 654**, established by control rather than asserted: the pre-edit and post-edit renders are identical on both counts. **The failure mode is the one that matters here** — a committed test that cannot pass is indistinguishable, in a red run, from a test catching a real regression, and it trains the next reader to skim past a red `fit-finish`. *Close:* the assertion targets what the row actually renders, or the test is retired with its reason. **Do not “fix” it by re-adding the chip** — the row markup is the deliberate HO 609/613 compaction and the fixture is what drifted.~~ — **CLOSED HO 655** (`c201985`), and BOTH halves of the HO 654 entry were wrong. **THE FRAMING FIRST: there was no red run to skim past.** The entry said a committed test that cannot pass *“trains the next reader to skim past a red `fit-finish`”* — but **nothing in CI executes this spec**: `e2e-prod.yml` runs `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` by **explicit path** (no `e2e/` glob, deliberately) and `ci.yml` invokes no Playwright at all, both read off the workflow files at HO 655 §0. **That claim came from the reviewer's memory of CI coverage rather than from the workflow file**, which is the reasoning-over-reports class arriving in the entry that names it. **The mechanism RELOCATED rather than evaporating:** `npm run test:e2e` is `playwright test` with **no path**, so the default glob picks up BOTH specs — anyone running the documented manual command got a failure in `fit-finish` unrelated to whatever they were checking. The audience is a person at a terminal, not a workflow. **THE STATED CLOSE WAS ALSO WRONG, and that is what needed a handoff rather than a selector swap.** *“The assertion targets what the row actually renders”* produces a **still-failing test**: `.v2f-id` exists and is a real `<a href="/bill/…">`, so repointing satisfies `:143` and **fails four lines later at the navigation assertion**, where it reads as an app REGRESSION rather than as fixture rot and the next person spends the budget on the app. The behaviour was deliberately reversed by HO 609/613/627. **Measured on the running app** (not taken from the handoff or the component comment): **plain click → url unchanged, `.v2f-group.open` 1, tabs 1** · **ctrl → open 0, tabs 1→2, popup `/bill/119-s-3266`** · **middle → open 0, tabs 2→3, same popup**. The test now pins all three legs (real link · plain expands · modified opens a tab and does NOT also expand — the mirrored defect `rowLinkClick`'s `stopPropagation` guards, which had **no coverage at all** before). **Both new legs proven able to fire on scratch perturbations of HEAD, reverted:** strip `onClick` → **RED `:169`**; strip `stopPropagation` → **RED `:194`**. One perturbation would have proven only leg 2 and left the newly-added leg unfalsified. **`BillIdChip` is NOT deleted** — live at `components/BillIdChip.tsx:33` + `app/globals.css:4340`; HO 654's “0 occurrences” was scoped to `/` and correct there. **ROT RULING: (a) ACCEPT MANUAL ROT, plus this note.** **(b) a static selector check was declined on a MEASURED null, not a predicted one** — the HO 655 census (70 selectors, 41 distinct class tokens) returns **0 with no producer on the very suite that was carrying the defect**, so a static gate would have shipped **green over a broken test**. That is stronger than the handoff's prediction that it *“would not have caught this one”*, and it is the part that stops (b) being re-proposed: the class here is **selector exists, behaviour moved**, and no static check reaches it. **(c) a cadence tied to an existing event** is a convention nobody enforces, which is how it rotted. **AND THE OPTION NOBODY COSTED, recorded so it is DECLINED rather than omitted: running `fit-finish` unattended against LOCALHOST.** Running the test is the **only** thing that reaches the real class. The prod blocker is BotID, but that is a **prod-domain** reason and does not apply here; the localhost blocker is **different and unpriced** — the spec needs feed rows, so it needs `TURSO_*` credentials, and **`ci.yml` is secretless by deliberate decision at HO 488**. **NOT RECOMMENDED and unpriced:** taking it means reopening a property that was established on purpose, and nobody has costed the fixture/seeding work that would avoid doing so. Do not treat it as a bright idea; treat it as a trade someone must argue for.
- **WATCH — `WeeklyBandMetricCard.prior` went OPTIONAL at HO 654, so a metric that FORGETS it renders no delta instead of failing to compile.** *Owner:* Code. Before 654 the prop was required and omission was a **type error**; now a fifth metric card added without `prior`/`priorDate` silently renders a metric with no week-over-week delta, and **“absence is the claim” is indistinguishable from “someone omitted it” to everything except a human reading the comment.** The prop's meaning moved **out of the type and into a comment**, which is the only place it is now written down. **That is the HO 635 shape arriving inside the fix for HO 635's consequences** — a field whose semantics the type system cannot enforce (`stage_observed_at` was a timestamp column that could not say which clock it was on; `prior?: number` is a prop whose absence cannot say whether it was reasoned about). **It STAYS as-is:** the blast radius is **4 call sites in one file** (`components/WeeklyBand.tsx:189/233/269/292`), all four visible in one screen, so the cost of the looser type is bounded and observable. **The eventual fix shape, named here so the next person adding a metric card doesn't re-derive it: an explicit opt-out prop** — e.g. a required discriminated `delta` (`{prior, priorDate}` | `{none: "no-correct-delta"}`) or a required `noDelta` flag — **so that OMISSION is a compile error again and DECLINING a delta is a positive statement in the type.** *Close:* a fifth metric card lands, or the opt-out prop ships. Do not close by re-requiring `prior`: that would force TRANSITIONS OBSERVED to pass a number it has no correct value for, which is the thing HO 654 declined.
- **A tight string-presence filter is unsound for this class, and the instrument can re-enter the same trap (HO 651).** *Owner:* Code. **165 of the 198 M1 candidates are PAIRED-DRIFT** — the name occurs as a token somewhere in source, so a string-presence check would CLEAR it, while it has no file and no export. The motivating case is the whole argument: **`DistributionsTabs`'s only occurrence anywhere is `app/globals.css:1563`, inside a comment describing the deleted treemap's pane** — one stale artifact vouching for another, and two pieces of drift read as one live component. Hence the probe's rule: **existence means a DEFINITION, never a mention**, with presence reported as a column rather than used as an exclusion. **The same shape re-entered through the instrument itself and was caught by measurement, not by reading:** this probe NAMES components in its own comments and `scripts/` is inside the scanned tree, so before self-exclusion it vouched for a phantom it was enumerating (`DashboardBubbleChart`, vouched solely by `skill-phantoms-651.ts`). **An instrument that discusses its subjects in its own source will vouch for them if its own tree is in scope** — worth carrying to the next probe that scans repo text.**STATE IT AS ONE MECHANISM WITH TWO INSTANCES, NOT AS A PROBE QUIRK:** *an artifact that merely MENTIONS a name can clear a string-presence check for it, and that artifact may be stale prose OR the checking instrument's own source.* Instance one is `app/globals.css:1563` — a comment about a deleted pane vouching for a deleted component. Instance two is `scripts/diagnostic/skill-phantoms-651.ts` naming the very things it hunts, with `scripts/` inside its own scanned tree. **Any future check of this shape inherits both**, and the second is the easier to miss because the instrument looks like the one trustworthy thing in the room. **OWED TO ODDITIES** — flagged here rather than written there, because a probe does not get to add a standing rule; whoever acts on this list should land it. **AND A SECOND WAY THE INSTRUMENT READ AS SUCCESS WHILE MEASURING NOTHING, from the same arc.** HO 651 §4 states the deletion marker sits **four characters** from the L2043 phantom. It does not: **the real distance is ~162 characters**, and the “four” was measured off an ELIDED QUOTE inside the handoff — its own `…` hiding ~145 characters of clause — rather than off SKILL. The consequence was concrete: the control was first built with a ±120-char naive window, which cannot reach a marker at 162, so **it passed under both the correct classifier and the broken one and discriminated nothing.** An inert control that passes is this repo's most-repeated defect, and this is the reasoning-over-reports class arriving *from the reviewer, inside the handoff for the probe built against exactly that*. The fix was to make the control assert the MECHANISM — PHANTOM *and* the adjacent-marker trap demonstrably present — so it now fails under `PHANTOM_NAIVE=1`. **Measure a distance in the artifact, never in a quotation of it.**  *Watch:* `app/globals.css:1563` is the known vouching artifact and is untouched; it is named in the HO 650 entry above as part of that repair, not here. **THIRD INSTANCE, AND THE OWED-TO-ODDITIES DEBT IS NOW PAID (HO 655).** The mechanism is filed at the END of `docs/oddities.md` as *a matching check whose pattern cannot fail for some inputs reports those inputs as passing* — recorded here rather than as a second entry because it is **one mechanism, and splitting it across two entries is exactly what this entry argued against**. The third instance is HO 655's selector census: a **bare substring match** over `components/` + `app/` attributed `.open` to `AbsenceCardBack.tsx` and `.v2f-id` to `BillIdChip.tsx`, **both false** — `open` matched the English word in a comment and `v2f-id` matched inside the longer string `bill-id-chip`. **A short token cannot fail a substring test over a large tree**, so v1's *“0 missing”* was partly a property of the matcher rather than of the specs. **The two polarities are now named:** instances one and two **cannot MISS** (an artifact, or the instrument's own source, supplies the match); the ±120-char window **cannot MATCH** (unreachable evidence, so everything reports absent). **And the result stood while the instrument did not** — the strict re-run (whole-word on a `className=` line, or `.token` in CSS) returned the SAME 0 with correct attributions, so **nothing in the first output distinguished the broken matcher from the sound one.** Agreement is the silent case; disagreement would have announced the defect.
- **WATCH — `fit-finish.spec.ts:466` (`/nominations`) failed ONCE under the full-glob parallel run and passed twice (HO 655).** *Owner:* Code. Passed in isolation and on a second full `npm run test:e2e`; **1 failure in 3 runs, cause unidentified** — most likely contention against a single local server when both specs run together, but that is a guess and is labelled as one. **Recorded rather than dropped** because an intermittent failure nobody wrote down is how a suite stops being run: the next person sees a red they cannot attribute, learns the suite is unreliable, and the NEXT real failure is skimmed. **Not attributable to HO 655** — that HO touched only the feed-id test, and `/nominations` shares no component with it. *Close:* it recurs and is diagnosed, or three consecutive clean glob runs retire it. **SECOND ROUTE, HO 657: `/news loads clean` failed the same way** — red under the full `npm run test:e2e` glob, **passes in isolation** (`console=0 pageErr=0 bad=0`), and shares no component with that HO's change (the dashboard races panel). **Two unrelated routes now exhibit it, which is evidence for the contention guess and against a per-route cause** — the common factor is the parallel glob against a single local server, not the route. The close criterion is unchanged and now has a wider net: diagnose it, or retire it on three consecutive clean glob runs. **Neither HO's change is implicated**, and both were checked in isolation rather than assumed.

## BANKED

*Gated/dated; each states its gate.*

- **Consolidate the two party-split mechanisms (HO 540).** The D/R/I split over a roll call is now computed two ways: a SQL `GROUP BY` in `getBillAmendmentVotes` step 3, and an in-memory reduce on `/vote/[id]`. They **agree by the structural skip-unless-D/R/I guard** (oddities), but it's two implementations of one number. *Gate:* a third consumer — the HO 428-A extraction trigger (extract to a shared helper when the count hits three, not before). *Owner:* Corey.

- ~~**Procedural amendment-outcome surface (HO 537 M8)**~~ — **PROMOTED to QUEUED, GO'd on measurement (HO 550).** The HO 550 probe closed the "is it worth building?" question — vocabulary CLOSED at 4 classes, 60 amendments would gain a line, polarity 96% against full 64/64 coverage — see QUEUED "Motion-aware procedural-amendment-outcome surface" + roadmap "Also (HO 550–552)". *Original framing retained —* the 64 senate roll calls that TOUCH an amendment without being its up-or-down vote — Motion to Table, Cloture Motion, Decision of the Chair, and `Motion to Waive Budgetary Discipline Re: … Amdt. No. N` — are real signal (amendments that died by tabling or never cleared cloture) but can't ride the "voted" cut's amendment-framed `VoteLine`: their yea/nay is on the motion, inverted or orthogonal to the amendment's fate, and `deriveDisposition` on their `votes.result` is misleading-on-tally (oddities). Needs a **motion-aware model** — invert tallies for a tabling, distinguish cloture from adoption, suppress when an anchored decisive vote already exists (`119-samdt-2360` etc.). Its own arc. *Gate:* appetite, not a fast-follow. *No fixed HO number* (HO 418). *Owner:* Corey.

- **Dead-symbol prune (`market_ticks`)** *(cosmetic, want-not-need)* — the HO 474 recon found ~200 frozen Stooq-era rows (DOW/GOLD/BTC/VIX/DXY/SILVER/NATGAS/ITA/XLK/XLV/XLF/XLE/XLI) inert in `market_ticks` since ~mid-June — not in `MARKET_SYMBOLS`, never rendered. A one-time `DELETE` is pure hygiene, zero functional benefit. *Gate:* only if `market_ticks` size ever matters (it won't at ~74 rows/day).

- **cron-health refinements (HO 477, deferred)** *(monitoring, want-not-need)* — (a) **fmp-specific freshness:** the markets route logs one `route="/api/cron/markets"` for both the bare and fmp crons, so fmp stopping while bare keeps firing isn't row-detectable — needs payload inspection (symbol-count or a `?source=fmp` marker). (b) **window-aware thresholds** for `/api/cron/weekly-report` + `/api/sync-race-ratings`: the flat 9-day `windowOnly` threshold catches a total stop; precise next-fire alerting needs cron-expression parsing. (c) **per-route data-freshness layer:** a route that times out forever while committing nothing reads alive on `cron_runs` status alone — folding HO 135's bills / summary / news freshness reads into `computeCronHealth` would catch the degrade-not-stop mode. All low-priority; the realistic failures (stopped firing, erroring every tick) are already caught.

- **Nominations secondary surfaces** — (i) a **military-confirmations view** (the 1,049 bulk service PNs excluded from the civilian `/nominations` surface; `is_military` makes it a clean filter/toggle) — this item now **also owns the `nominee_count` hydration**: it's military-only (civilian PNs are 1 nominee) and the natural (only) consumer of officer counts, and the same `--hydrate` detail walk reads it cheaply from the embedded `nominees[]` array (the HO 458 rider), so the military view carries both the `is_military` toggle and the `nominee_count` populate; and (ii) a **`/nomination/[id]` detail page** (rows are non-linked in v1). Both gated on demand — smaller, may never be needed. *Owner:* Corey.

- **Prod arm for the `unstable_cache` key-rotation clause (LOW — the mechanism is already grounded in source).** *Owner:* Code. The oddity *An `unstable_cache` key hashes the callback's own source text* (HO 647) states a prod consequence — a rotated key has no prod entry, so the first post-deploy read is a cold miss that builds the new value directly — which is **derived, not observed**. The derivation is sound and handler-agnostic (`unstable-cache.ts:133-134`: `generateCacheKey` receives `fixedKey` + args in Next's shared path *before* any cache handler is consulted), which is why a local `.next/cache` reading carries to the Data Cache at all. It stayed unobserved because **the band's public surface cannot discriminate** — a fat payload is a superset of a thin one and `AbsenceCardBack` renders byte-identically from either — so no number of prod hits settles it. **Design: TWO arms, with OPPOSITE predicted readings, because one arm alone confirms whichever way it lands.** *Arm A* — a deploy touching nothing inside `queryAbsenceWatch`'s body — predicts key **stable** → cache HIT → log **ABSENT** (clause (b), callees are not in the key). *Arm B* — a deploy that edits the body — predicts key **rotates** → MISS → log **PRESENT** (the clause under test). **Arm A NEEDS A POSITIVE CONTROL, because its pass condition is the ABSENCE of a signal** — and an absent log is indistinguishable from a log path that never worked. `/members` calls `getMemberCardExpansion` **directly**, so hit `/members` in the same deploy and confirm the log fires there: the same shared call site that makes the log noisy is what proves it is wired. Without that control arm A is unreadable, and this is the piece the first draft of the design was missing. **Three traps.** (1) **The callback-side instrument is invalid:** a log added *inside* the callback changes its text, rotates the key, guarantees a miss, and so manufactures its own pass — the same-as-success shape entered through the instrument rather than the metric. (2) The log must therefore sit in a **callee**, whose text is not in the key — but `getMemberCardExpansion` is **also called by `/members`**, so it must **identify its caller**. Note this supersedes the tempting alternative of hiding it in a callee only the band reaches: that variant destroys the positive control above, so caller-tagging is required rather than optional. (3) **Read the two outcomes asymmetrically — ABSENT is unambiguous, PRESENT is not.** ABSENT means key stable → hit → the callback never ran (given the control passed). PRESENT has three live explanations: the body genuinely rotated the key (arm B), the bundler **inlined** the callee — which folds the log's own string literal into `cb.toString()`, rotating the key and making the log **fire**, so inlining can never produce a null reading — or the entry was simply gone for an unrelated reason (TTL expiry, a `revalidateTag("votes")` from the votes cron, eviction, a cold region). So arm B confirms nothing on its own; it is arm A's absence, backed by the control, that carries the result. *Gate:* a session already deploying for another reason can take arm A for free; do not spend a deploy on this. *Close:* arm A reads ABSENT with its control firing, or the prod clause is corrected in the oddity. **Deliberately LOW: the cost of the prod arm exceeds what proving it buys, and the clause is flagged derived-and-unfalsified where it lives.**
- ~~**A deploy-liveness gate that stops at the FIRST matching read can green on a promotion transient.** *Owner:* Code. Observed landing `226bd99` (2026-08-12): a poll loop broke on a matching read of `/api/version`, and **the very next request served `daf06b5`** — two instances briefly on different builds mid-promotion. **Not a parse artifact:** the identical parse then read **12/12 clean** once promotion settled, which is what distinguishes a real transient from a broken harness. This is the convergence rule already in oddities (*A shipped VALUE change behind `unstable_cache` doesn't land on the first prod read — verify by convergence, not hit-count*, HO 555) **applied one layer down: to the SHA gate rather than to a cached value** — see that entry for the mechanism rather than a restatement here. **The placement is why it matters.** `/api/version` is the gate every post-deploy measurement runs behind, so a gate that false-greens **hands its doubt to everything taken after it** — a byte reading, a render diff, a latency sample all silently inherit “we were on the new build”. **`scripts/verify-deploy.ts` has exactly this shape today:** it polls until the first match and stops (that is its documented behaviour, not a regression). **Fix:** require a run of **N consecutive** matching reads before declaring live, not one. *Gate:* the next time a deploy check gates a measurement that matters — not worth a session before then. *Close:* `verify:deploy` requires N consecutive matches, or the transient is shown not to recur.~~ — **SHIPPED HO 653** (`0144f66`), and it closes on TWO observations rather than one, which is what makes it a shape instead of an anomaly. **(1) `226bd99` — BANKED, retrospective:** a matching read, then the very next request served `daf06b5`; caught by hand while landing, and filed on the strength of a single sighting. **(2) `4051206` — FIRED, AND CHANGED THE ANSWER:** read 9 matched, **read 10 served `f47b807`**, the run reset, and convergence came at read 15. Under the first-match shape the check exits green at read 9 — one request before prod served the predecessor again. That second instance is the citable evidence: the first showed the transient exists, the second showed the gate would have passed through it. `scripts/verify-deploy.ts` now requires **`REQUIRED_CONSECUTIVE = 5`**, and a break **prints the breaking SHA and resets the run** rather than continuing the count — a silent reset would hide the very event the check exists to catch. **Both branches were exercised before this was called done** (an untriggered guard is unproven): the confirm branch ran live against prod to 5 consecutive and exit 0; the break branch was falsified with a scratch stub sequencing match / match / other / matches, printing `BREAK — served f47b807deadbeef, run reset after 2/5` before restarting and confirming at 5/5, with the scratch reverted clean. **The general form, worth carrying:** the convergence rule (hit until it converges AND HOLDS) applies to a SHA gate exactly as it does to a cached value — and it matters more there, because `/api/version` is what every post-deploy measurement runs behind, so a gate that false-greens hands its doubt to every figure taken after it. Cost is ~40s of extra wall clock at a 10s interval. *Residual:* none — the by-hand convention and the unattended script now agree.

## WATCH

- **New (HO 684) · the NEWS chip's 2-line clamp bites at 2560, and the reason is the type ladder rather than the width.** *Owner:* Code. The clamp was ruled over a single-line ellipsis on the criterion that at **the tightest box** — the 400px `auto-fit` floor, 430 viewport — the live-max **95-character** headline resolves in full across two lines, with the clamp only firing past ~120 ch against a corpus max of 123. **That criterion holds and was verified.** What it could not anticipate is that the tightest box in *pixels* is not the tightest in *characters*: at **2560** the C2 type ladder lifts `--fs-10` to 13px while `auto-fit` simultaneously narrows the card to ~439px, so one of the six live headlines clamps there (honestly, with a visible ellipsis) while every headline resolves at 430, 1440 and 1920. *Measured 2026-09-02, six featured seats, headline lengths 51/57/58/78/79/95.* **Not a defect and deliberately not fixed** — truncating the longest headline at one width is what a clamp is for, and widening it unilaterally would have overridden a ruling. *Watch for:* the clamp biting **more than one** headline at 2560, or biting at 1920 — either means the corpus has lengthened past what the 2-line budget holds, at which point the options are a 3rd line at ≥2400 or scoping the NEWS body's font off the ladder. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/races-chips-captures-684.ts` (local, prod build) prints chars, line count and `clamped` per chip per viewport. **The figures above are a reading of one day's headlines, not a property of the design.**

- ~~**New (HO 669) · the live ODDS strip renders BOTH Fed-cut pairs as "FED CUT SEP" — the `showMonth` disambiguator has stopped disambiguating.** *Owner:* Code. **Observed, not inferred:** the `/` render captured for HO 669's roster gate (local production build, both pre- and post-chore, identical) reads its four ODDS pair labels as **`SHUTDOWN · FED CUT SEP · FED CUT SEP · RECESSION`**. HO 302 deliberately gave `FEDCUT` (July) and `FEDCUT-SEP` (September) the **same** `label: "FED CUT"` and relied on `showMonth` to tell them apart by appending the resolution month read from **each primary's own `marketDate`** — see `DashboardV2Header.tsx`'s `ODDS_PAIRS` and the SKILL `:1994` note. With the July decision now past, `FEDCUT`'s soonest-open Kalshi event has rolled forward, so its `marketDate` resolves to September too and both slots print the same string. **Unchanged by HO 669 and not caused by it** — the strip's two mounts were untouched and the pre/post renders are byte-identical — it was simply visible in the capture, which is the only reason it is on the record. **This is a WATCH rather than a fix because the mechanism may be self-correcting and the right fix depends on which:** if `FEDCUT`'s discovery has genuinely advanced to the September event, the two symbols are now **duplicates** and the roster wants one of them dropped, not a better label; if it is pinned elsewhere and only the displayed month is wrong, `showMonth` wants the fix. **Do not "fix" the label before establishing which** — appending a distinct month to two rows reading the same underlying market would make the tape confidently wrong instead of visibly redundant. **This is a WATCH and not a FIT & FINISH item because the fork is resolvable by WAITING, not by building** — `FEDCUT` has no pinned `kalshiEvent`, so its soonest-open discovery **will move on its own** when the next decision event opens, and whether the collision clears by itself is exactly what discriminates the two branches. *Close:* **on the next Kalshi `KXFEDDECISION` event opening, or on the September FOMC resolving — whichever lands first** — re-read the two symbols' `market_ticks.marketDate` and the discovered event ticker for each, then take the branch the reading names. **(a) The collision CLEARS** — `FEDCUT` has rolled to a later event than `FEDCUT-SEP`, the labels read distinct months again, and the entry closes as self-resolved with the reading recorded; this is the expected outcome and it means `showMonth` was working correctly the whole time, on a roster that had gone momentarily degenerate. **(b) The collision PERSISTS** — both symbols resolve to the same event, which makes them **duplicates**, not a labelling bug: the fix is to drop one from `ODDS_TAPE`/`ODDS_PAIRS` (and decide whether `FEDCUT-SEP`'s `kalshiEvent` pin still earns its place), NOT to append a distinguishing month to two rows reading one market. **Do not relabel before this read resolves** — that would make the tape confidently wrong instead of visibly redundant, which is strictly worse.~~ — **CLOSED HO 681, branch (b), on the reading the entry itself demanded.** Re-read 2026-09-01 against live upstream, not the DB alone: **`FEDCUT` and `FEDCUT-SEP` both resolve `KXFEDDECISION-26SEP`; `POLY-FEDCUT` and `POLY-FEDCUT-SEP` both resolve `fed-decision-in-september-762`**, with K 2.0% / P 0.7% on both halves — **the collision PERSISTED**, so the two symbols are **duplicates and `FEDCUT-SEP`'s `kalshiEvent` pin does not earn its place**. The entry's instruction was followed exactly: the pinned pair was dropped from `ODDS_TAPE`/`ODDS_PAIRS` (and from `/welcome`'s own strip, which the entry did not know about — it labels from `MARKET_SYMBOLS[].label` and so read `FED CUT ODDS` / `FED CUT ODDS (SEP)`, **visually distinct while carrying identical numbers**, which is the harder duplication to notice), and **no distinguishing month was appended to two rows reading one market**. **`showMonth` is exonerated** — it faithfully reported that both pairs had the same resolution month; it stays ON and now names which meeting the roller sits on, rolling to FED CUT OCT after 2026-09-16 by itself. Two corrections the entry could not have made: the two symbols' stored `marketDate` **differed** (`2026-09-16` vs `2026-09-01`) because the pinned path defaults the ticker day to 01, so a `marketDate` comparison alone would have read them as distinct; and the quantity that actually settles it is **identity**, which nothing in the pipeline reported until HO 681 added `resolvedId` and the per-source collision flag. Also on the record: **the entry's own premise that the fork was resolvable by WAITING was correct** — the roller moved on its own, exactly as written.
- ~~**New (HO 667) · first post-deploy `/api/sync` tick — confirm the lead step is gone and the saving is real.** *Owner:* Corey (Vercel runtime logs) **or** Code (the tick's row via a `cron_runs` read; there is no `verify:deploy`-adjacent product reader of that payload — `lib/cron-health.ts` reads none of it, which is exactly why removing `timings.lead` was safe). But the four pre-deploy ticks were read and they retire the expectation: **#10014 18:01 total 4,612ms / lead 2,148ms · #9957 12:01 5,706 / 1,703 · #9898 06:01 6,270 / 2,098 · #9841 00:00 7,327 / 2,357.** So the lead step cost **~1.7–2.4s of a ~4.6–7.3s run** — HO 480/481's collapse of its DB half is fully visible, and **the ~25.3s of a 40.4s run is 2026-05 history, pre-480/481**. Expect the post-deploy total around **2.5–5s** with `timings = {reportCatchup, sync, trades}`; a still-present `lead` key remains the deploy-not-promoted signal, unchanged.~~ — **CLOSED HO 669 on the observation: the lead step is GONE and the rider's yardstick is the one that fits.** Read read-only from `cron_runs` at HEAD `5549d94`, with `verify:deploy` first matching prod to `5549d94` on 5 consecutive reads (so ≥ `52145c1`). Boundary `2026-08-15T21:44:33Z`. **8 post-deploy ticks, 0 carrying `lead`**, every one reading `timings = {reportCatchup, sync, trades}` exactly as predicted: **#10071 00:01 1,157ms · #10124 06:01 4,900 · #10183 12:01 3,984 · #10239 18:01 831 · #10294 00:01 13,347 · #10349 06:03 2,268 · #10409 12:01 2,050 · #10475 18:00 26,765.** The rider's four pre-deploy ticks reproduce exactly (#10014 4,612/2,148 · #9957 5,706/1,703 · #9898 6,270/2,098 · #9841 7,327/2,357). **Judged against the ~1.7–2.4s-off-a-~4.6–7.3s-run yardstick, not the HO 139 headline:** six of eight post-deploy runs land at **831–4,900ms**, at or below the pre-deploy floor. **The two long runs are volume, not lead, and that is checkable rather than assumed** — #10475's own `timings.sync` is **25,739ms of its 26,765ms total** (16 bills seen/upserted), so the total tracks sync work, exactly as the rider warned a naive total-only reading would misjudge. Source-side corroboration, since the console line cannot be read from here: **`grep '[sync] lead'` = 0** and **`lead:` in `app/api/sync/route.ts` = 0**; every surviving `weekly_lead`/`getDashboardLead` mention in the tree is an HO 667 RECORD comment (`queries.ts:1071–1072`, `migrate.ts:80`, `primaries-sync.ts:1864`, `sync-timeout-verify-480.ts`). **Two instrument failures were caught and paid for before any zero was trusted, and both would have closed this WATCH on nothing:** (1) `timings` is **not** at the payload root — the envelope is `{ok, elapsedMs, payload:{timings,…}}`, so reading `p.timings` returned "no timings" for **every** row, pre- and post-deploy alike, which reads exactly like "the lead is gone"; (2) string-comparing an ISO instant to a space-separated boundary is not a date comparison (`"…T18:01Z" > "… 21:44"` because `T` > space), which misclassified all four rider-baseline pre-deploy ticks as post-deploy. The fix is the **control** the entry now carries: a pre-deploy tick must print a **numeric** lead on the same invocation before any post-deploy zero counts — 15 of 16 did (the 16th, #9165, is a `timeout` row with no timings object at all). Probe kept untracked at `scripts/diagnostic/sync-lead-watch-669.ts` with both traps written into its header.

- ~~**New (HO 645) · the absence-band payload doubled at a CONSTANT population, and 96% of it renders nothing.** *Owner:* Code. **The re-price HO 645 §4 called for**, and the number that matters is not the one the criterion gated on. Measured against the live cached `getAbsenceWatch` blob (`scripts/diagnostic/absence-payload-645.ts`, M4, 2026-08-11, prod DB, 2 members): **29,520 bytes — 1.91x the 15,481-byte post-HO-631 reading, so the §4 ship gate (2x) was NOT exceeded and commit B shipped.** But the population did **not** move: the closed entry above reads *"at today's 2 members the payload is not the problem it becomes at ten"*, and it went from **7,740 to 14,760 bytes PER MEMBER on corpus drift alone**, with no code change between the two readings. **A trigger phrased as a population threshold cannot see a per-member one**, which is why it fired unwatched — the same shape as a measured number in a comment going stale while the decision it justified stays put. Re-priced accordingly: **watch bytes-per-member, not member count**, and re-run M4 when either moves. Projection at HO 642's pooled max of 6: ~88.6 KB. **The narrowing is now measured rather than suspected (§4's "note the direction", verified by id):** `AbsenceCardBack` replaced `SponsorExpandedPanel` on this surface and reads exactly three things out of the bundle — `committees[].name`, the first three `recentBills`' type+number, and `stats.total`. The blob is **`recentBills` 78.2% (23,098 B) · `committees` 17.4% (5,127 B) · `topics` 0.8% (224 B) · `stats` 0.6% (191 B) · `affiliations` 0.6% (182 B) · `key` 0.1% (18 B)** — so **`topics` (224 B) and `affiliations` (182 B) are carried, cached and serialized for NO CONSUMER AT ALL** now that the back is not `SponsorExpandedPanel`, and the **23,098 B of `recentBills` exists to print THREE bill numbers**. **NOT taken in HO 645**, deliberately: it is a data change and commit B was scoped to the tier. Two moves when it is picked up — narrow the band's assembly to the three consumed fields (consumer-side, `getMemberCardExpansion` untouched, the HO 631 shape), and the still-unclaimed SQL-side count for `getSponsorTopTopics`. *Close:* **a consumer-side slice at the band's assembly site** — `getMemberCardExpansion` stays untouched, because `/members` reads it too (HO 507) — and M4 re-reads; or bytes-per-member is shown to have stopped growing. **Verify by id, not byte total: a total can coincide (HO 631), which is exactly how the HO 631 slice looked like it had done nothing.**~~ — **SHIPPED HO 647** (`754a0f6`), local-verified; **the narrowing this entry measured is taken, and the payload is now smaller than the post-HO-631 baseline it was filed against.** `queryAbsenceWatch` no longer spreads `MemberCardExpansion`: it projects a named `AbsenceCardPayload` carrying exactly the three keys `AbsenceCardBack` reads. Re-read with the same instrument at the same population (`absence-payload-645` M4, **2 members — MIA 2 / AT RISK 0**, prod DB, 2026-08-12; the cache entry deleted between the two reads, per §4): `recentBills` **23,098 B / 78.2% → 230 B** (1.0% of its pre-value) · `committees` **5,127 B → 1,299 B** · `stats` **191 B → 24 B** (narrowed to `{ total }`) · `topics` **224 B → absent** · `affiliations` **182 B → absent** · `key` **18 B → absent** · **blob 29,520 B (14,760/member) → 2,167 B (1,084/member)**. So the two unread keys are **gone from the object, not merely small** — the PASS criterion was their absence, not their size — and the 23,098 B that existed to print three bill numbers is 230 B. Against the 15,481 B post-HO-631 reading that is **0.14x**, so the WATCH this entry re-priced (**bytes-per-member, not member count**) is satisfied for now and stays worth re-reading when either moves. **Rendered output is unchanged, and that is the load-bearing check rather than the byte figure**: the served `.abw-bk-*` subtrees are byte-identical for both members (md5 `7308325226933bfc`), all four `+N` closers unmoved at +11 / +21 / +4 / +9 — a broken `cmteMore` is exactly what a plausible-looking render would hide, which is why committees are projected but **NOT sliced** (that closer counts off the full array; `recentBills` is sliceable only because HO 631 moved its closer onto `stats.total`). **`ABSENCE_BACK_BILLS_CAP` = 3** is the shared cap now, and `lib/member-card-caps.ts` no longer describes this band at all — its header carried two clauses this change falsified. **NOT closed here, and named rather than dropped:** the **SQL-side count for `getSponsorTopTopics`** is still unclaimed (it has survived two entries in this lineage as a “then consider”; `topics` no longer reaching this payload removes the symptom on THIS surface, not the JS-side count `/members` still runs). **No prod byte figure is reported here, and the reason is NOT the one §4 gives** — this is a correction of that premise, not an inheritance of it. §4 says the key is the `keyParts` literal, so the fat blob would keep serving and shrink only on regeneration. It does not: the key is `${cb.toString()}-${keyParts}` (Next 15.5 `unstable-cache.ts:95`), this edit was inside the callback body, so the key ROTATED and prod holds no entry under it. The first read after deploy is therefore a **cold miss that builds the small blob directly** — there is no convergence window and nothing shrinks on regeneration; the fat pre-647 entry is orphaned under the old key and ages out unread. The figures above are local, and a prod re-read should agree on the first hit rather than after settling. The three HO 646 lines — the unfired `card === null` cause, the 119TH denominator, the `SponsorPhoto` fallback residue — are untouched by this build and stay open.

Not a defect and not worth manufacturing a 404 to exercise; filed so the gap is on the record rather than inside a claim of byte-identity. *Close:* a member with no resolvable photo renders on `/members` and the branch is looked at, or the claim is narrowed in place to name the photo path only.

*Standing observations; each notes what would promote it.*

- **The doc-sweep deletion gate is `git diff --numstat`'s deletions column — `grep -c '^-[^-]'` is not a deletion count (owed from HO 639–641, filed HO 643).** *Owner:* Code/Corey, standing. In unified diff a deleted markdown bullet `- **text**` renders as `-- **text**`, which `^-[^-]` **excludes by construction** — so the count reads **0** on a diff that deleted bullets, and 0 is indistinguishable from a clean append. It was reported as verification by two different readers across the 639–641 arc before anything caught it. Use **`git diff --numstat HEAD~1 HEAD -- <file>`** and read the deletions column (normal-format `^<` also works). **Do NOT diff against `git show HEAD:file`** — `core.autocrlf` is true with no `.gitattributes`, so an LF blob against a CRLF worktree reports every line changed. Mechanism is filed in oddities as instance 3 of the proxy-instrument family. *Promote to SKILL as a doc-sweep verification rule when:* a third arc either misreports on it or has to rediscover it — two arcs is an oddity, three is a convention.

- **A DOCUMENTED COVERAGE RATIO GOES FALSE WHEN ITS DENOMINATOR GROWS, AND NOTHING RE-RUNS THE BACKFILL THAT WOULD FIX IT (opened HO 643).** *Owner:* Code/Corey, standing. Two halves, and the first is why the second stays invisible.
  **(a) The doc-form failure.** A sentence of the form *"N of M, gaps are X and Y"* is **exhaustive when written and stays grammatically true forever**. When M grows, the sentence does not become ungrammatical, self-contradictory, or greppable — **no word in it changes**, so no sweep, no typecheck and no diff flags it. It just quietly stops meaning what it says: the gap list goes from *complete* to *a sample*, and a reader with no reason to doubt it treats the un-listed remainder as non-existent. This is the measured-number rule's blind spot — that rule catches a figure that is *wrong*, and this failure keeps the figure *right* while the ratio around it rots.
  **(b) The operational gap.** **There is no trigger that re-runs a backfill when its parent set is re-seeded.** The backfills are manual/quarterly and the sets they cover (`race_ratings` re-seeds, `sync:members`) grow independently, so every re-seed silently widens every ratio taken over it. **Un-run is not un-resolvable**, and the docs cannot tell them apart: `backfill:2024-margin` has reached 106 House rated seats against a House rated set of **151**, so ~**45** have no margin because nobody has re-run it — while the entry's two named gaps (ME-02 RCV, TX-09 uncontested) describe seats that are genuinely unresolvable. Same shape one table over: `sync:crosswalk` is gated on `members`, so a member added by a later `sync:members` sits `skipped_no_member` until it is re-run by hand (the Gallagher CA-01 instance is already in SKILL).
  **Exhibits.** **(1) the challenger harvest — see the harvest-currency QUEUED line above**, which owns the scoping and is not duplicated here; it is the fullest instance (reach frozen at `primary_date` 2026-06-02, 57/186). **(2) `backfill:2024-margin`, 106/151** — corrected in SKILL at HO 643 with its gap list explicitly de-exhausted, NOT re-run. **(3) the rated-set predicate figures themselves** — 137/44 and 61 drifted to 186/46 and 59 with nothing re-checking them, which is how the class was noticed at all. **Also in the class, dated at HO 643 and therefore accurate TODAY rather than fixed:** incumbent cash 177/186, Kalshi 185/186, incumbent photo 184/186 — one denominator, and they will all go stale together at the next re-seed. **Explicit NON-member, because it shows what the class is actually about:** `backfill:primary-results` states ~47% and is *not* in it — the forward sync fills each contest as it votes, so it **names its own trigger** and self-heals. The defect is the absent trigger, not the ratio.
  *Promote to SKILL as an authoring convention when:* a **second arc** hits this — three exhibits inside ONE arc is an oddity with three instances, not yet a convention, and promoting on a single arc's evidence would be the same over-reach the two-arcs rule exists to prevent. **That rule is being applied to itself here, deliberately.** The convention it would become: *a documented ratio over a growing set carries its denominator's provenance and its backfill's last-run reach, or it states no gap list at all.*

- **`primaries.primary_type` is NULL on 91 of 876 rows and the MECHANISM IS UNKNOWN (HO 641, measured HO 640).** *Owner:* Code. **91 NULL of 876 overall; 75 NULL of the 718 completed**, spread across 12 state+chamber groups — NY house 36, AZ house 14, CT house 10, **WA house 10**, NV 5, UT 5, HI 4, LA senate 2, VT 2, GA senate 1, PA 1, SC 1. **WA is the only special-system state left NULL**: CA `top_two` 52, AK `top_four` 4, ME `ranked_choice` 6, LA `jungle` 6 / `open` 2 / NULL 2 — and WA **NULL 10**. **Mechanism unknown, in those words.** `primaryTypeFor` (`lib/primary-calendar-scrape.ts:62-67`) is **ruled out**: its fallback is `return "open"`, so it cannot emit NULL. Nothing else has been read. **This is NOT the HO 584 fallback-default WATCH** — that one is about `open` being an unverified catch-all across ~45 states, a different value and a different mechanism, and citing it here would file a live gap under a cause that reads as already understood. **Consequence, and it is the reason this is worth carrying:** a branch on `primary_type = 'top_two'` catches **52 CA and 0 WA** — it silently misses an entire top-two state, and the miss looks like an absence of data rather than a bug. *Promote* the moment anything is about to branch on this column; read the write path before writing the branch.

- **The candidate status-precedence ladder is transcribed TWICE in `lib/queries.ts` with no shared constant (HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `getRaceCandidates` (the `/race/[id]` hub + the prefetched dashboard card) and `getRaceCandidatesForCycle` (`/electoral`) each carry their own `CASE … WHEN status IN ('won_primary','nominee') THEN 0 …` rung list. HO 638 updated both and left cross-referencing comments at each site, so they are **in step today**. **What makes this a WATCH rather than a nit is the failure mode: a status added to one and missed in the other does not error — it falls to the `ELSE` rung and sorts the new status BELOW withdrawn candidates**, i.e. a silently wrong order on exactly the surface the new status was added to serve. *Promote* when a third status enters the vocabulary, or when either ladder is opened for any other reason — extract the rung mapping to one constant then. Same shape as the four inline `median()` copies (HO 427/428): duplicated-and-currently-correct, with no mechanism that keeps it correct.

- **`seed:races` validates ratings but NOT candidate status — an invalid value inserts silently and sorts to `ELSE` (HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/seed-races.ts` carries a `VALID_RATINGS` set and warns-and-skips an unrecognized rating (deliberately non-fatal, so one bad rating can't abort a seed run); the candidate `status` field goes straight into the upsert as `c.status ?? null` with **no membership check at all**. A typo — `nominnee`, `Nominee` — therefore **inserts clean**, and because both ordering ladders route anything unrecognized to the `ELSE` rung, it renders in the **wrong position rather than erroring**. **This is why HO 638 read the raw status column back as the literal string `nominee` before trusting either ordering fire: at the seed step, a typo and a correct value produce a byte-identical failure signature.** *Promote* on the second status typo reaching the table, or fold a `VALID_STATUSES` set in beside `VALID_RATINGS` the next time `seed-races.ts` is opened for any reason — the surrounding pattern already exists, so it is a ride-along, not a build.

- ~~**`bills.stage_changed_at` records WHEN THE SYNC OBSERVED the advance, not when the stage changed — and HO 632's day-grouping reads that field (verified HO 634, filed NOT fixed; NOT 634 scope).**~~ — **CLOSED HO 635** (`9e95b21` ENACTED · `13acf1e` expand · `e2dcdd2` cutover · `bb54c22` contract), live on prod. The observation was confirmed and acted on, so it stops being a standing observation. The field is now **`bills.stage_observed_at`** (and `stage_transitions.changed_at` → `observed_at`, folded into the same expand/contract), so all **fifteen** readers now confront what they are reading. **The rename fixes the CLASS, not the instances** — it converts every remaining reader from an unexamined default into a deliberate choice. **One instance moved:** ENACTED now windows on `latest_action_date` (for an enacted bill the latest action IS the enactment, so this is exact rather than a proxy), and both halves of its WoW delta moved together; the strip went `● 0 enacted ▼3` → `±0`, because the shipped ▼3 was three enactments we *noticed* last week that had happened a month earlier. **Eight class-(C) counting readers deliberately did NOT move** and carry forward as their own QUEUED entry with disposition (4) already priced; the six class-(B) display-age readers and three class-(A) ordering readers are unmoved because nothing in the probe says they must be, and observation time is arguably right for ordering a terminal. **The census classes overlap** (`getStageChanges` both windows and orders), so those class counts do not partition the fifteen. **Two corrections the probe produced, recorded so the record does not carry them wrong:** `getStaleBills` windows on `latest_action_date` and was **never exposed** to this field (the HO 634 write-up misattributed it), and the legacy-NULL cohort is **15,631**, not the `~14k` `components/StagePillStrip.tsx` still documents (comment fix QUEUED). *Instrument retired:* `docs/handoffs/634-artifacts/stage-clock-probe.ts` — superseded by `docs/handoffs/635-artifacts/true-occurrence-635.ts` (the ground-truth join) and `verify-enacted-635.ts`.

- ~~**`/lobbying` M1: register says 1, a crawl reads 3 (HO 633, incidental).**~~ — **CLOSED HO 634. Attributed, priced, and PROMOTED to a second permanent-residue register line; site M1 restated 13-with-up-to-2-unexplained → 15 and the closing identity BALANCES.** *Attribution:* **not a commit — the data crossed under a constant.** The 340px cap was fitted at HO 614 to a then-current **minimum** rc ink of 239, and the corpus has since produced registrant→client strings shorter than anything in that sample; `gap ≈ 340 − rendered_ink + 12`, so every pixel below 239 goes straight into the gap. **Both candidate windows (HO 597–598, and after-620) are retired as retired: neither contains the change because the change is not in the code** — the `.lob-filing-row` block is **byte-identical across the whole window, md5 `bc49313d103615a3f976173dd7791770` at both `62c25e4` and `1e6435a`**, with `git log 62c25e4..HEAD -- app/lobbying components/FilingRow.tsx` returning **ZERO** commits as corroboration. *Disposition:* **(a) accepted residue**, ADOPTED-UNVIEWED off the written recommendation; Recorded as **UNPROVEN, not protection** (the HO 549 discipline). **(1) `classifyMotion → null` (tail drop):** measured tail = 0 across the residual, so the drop has never executed; it's the guard against an unrecognized motion form appearing in a future sync. **(2) The `chair` label:** the sole Decision-of-the-Chair row (`119-samdt-2360`) is anchored, so it's suppressed and no chair-labelled line renders anywhere in prod. Note the precision — the **orthogonal branch itself IS exercised** (cloture, e.g. `119-samdt-4732`, renders tally-only with no arrow), so what's unproven is the chair *label string*, not the code path. *Close:* eyeball the next unanchored chair motion or the next unrecognized form; both are cheap and inert until then.

Still gentle at once-daily + on `deployment_status`, and the 2× is the whole point (the hit path is where HO 533 lives) — but the headroom halved. *Promote to a fix* if the crawl ever trips a rate limit / BotID challenge, or if `ROUTES` grows enough that a 2× run approaches a timeout: the lever is to split the double-hit onto a **subset** of routes (the `unstable_cache`-backed dynamic ones — every route today, but a future static route needn't double-hit) rather than doubling the whole list. *Instrument:* the run wall-clock (**1.9m** for the full smoke suite — the 37-route crawl × 2 hits, plus the gate + interaction tests, = **41 test cases**) + any 429/403 in the crawl's own bad-subrequest log. **HO 685 adds 3 full crawls = 222 documents**, accounted here so the load stays honest: the STEP 2 dispatch on the review ref, the FF's own Production `deployment_status`, and the STEP 5.5 dispatch on `main`. **The four other runs the HO generates cost ZERO documents** — two review-ref Preview events and two scratch-branch Preview events all skip at the job `if` — and that is now the fix's own arithmetic rather than a coincidence: pre-685 each of those four could instead have *cancelled* a crawl mid-flight, spending a partial 74 and returning no verdict. The next scheduled daily is **not** an add (it fires regardless).
- **`billIds` shape asymmetry between the precomputed and live LDA paths (HO 570).** *Owner:* Code. The two now converge only because `FilingRow` dedupes both chip arrays at render (HO 570 C3). **Inert** — the HO 440 distinct-filings ranking is safe by the `Set<string>` inversion in `computeBillDrill` (`lda-rollup.ts:459-464`), so a bill repeated inside one filing still contributes its uuid once, and nothing counts the raw array. *Promote to a fix* (add `DISTINCT` to `readLdaTables`, or dedupe in `rowToFilingSummary`) if a **second** consumer of `FilingSummary.billIds` appears that doesn't dedupe, or if anything ever counts the raw array. *Instrument:* `grep -rn "\.billIds" app components lib` — a consumer outside `FilingRow` that maps or counts it unguarded.

The reading to watch: a **transient** sync lag shows `deferred` non-zero on one run and back to 0 the next; a **permanent** gap (a `voteId` that never syncs) shows `deferred` **PINNED** across consecutive runs with `remaining` stable — the walk re-fetches `/actions` (one GET) per stuck HAMDT every run indefinitely.
