- ~~**`e2e-prod.yml` global concurrency group (HO 504).** The workflow's `concurrency.group` is `e2e-prod` — **global, not per-ref** — with `cancel-in-progress: true`, so a deploy burst can cancel an in-flight scheduled run, and a cancelled run reports as **neither green nor red**. Low probability at once-daily, and arguably correct (newer state wins). Did **not** occur on the HO 504 push (one deploy, one event, nothing cancelled) — latent, not demonstrated. **Recognition symptom:** if the daily crawl ever appears to *stop reporting*, check for cancelled runs before debugging the schedule. *Promote* only if it bites. **Checked HO 572 — did not fire:** the 07-29/07-30 reds are `failure`, not `cancelled` (the recognition symptom made the check cheap), so this WATCH is looked-at-and-inert, not the cause of the `pageErr` finding below. **FIRED AT HO 683 — no longer latent, and in a SHARPER form than this entry describes.** Demonstrated live: dispatch `33673317612` was cancelled 47s in by run `33673395814`, a `deployment_status` event from the **Preview** deploy of the `683-review` push — a run the job `if` then **SKIPPED**. So **concurrency is evaluated BEFORE the job `if`**, and a run that does no work at all still cancels the run doing the work. The cancellation window is therefore **any `deployment_status` event landing during the crawl's ~3 minutes**, not the "deploy burst" this entry assumed, and it is reachable by an ordinary push to any branch rather than only by a production deploy. Re-dispatched clean as `33673524519`. **This now blocks something concrete:** the HO 683 overflow alarm is armed on `schedule`, so a cancelled scheduled run files nothing and the alarm silently misses a day. *Promote from WATCH — it bit.* Cheapest fix to weigh first: make the group per-event (`e2e-prod-${{ github.event_name }}`) or drop `cancel-in-progress` for non-`deployment_status` events.~~ — **CLOSED HO 685 (`88d776f`), and the sharper instance is the one to lead with: the alarm's blind window was not hypothetical, it swallowed a day.** **A three-run cancellation chain, 2026-09-02 18:25–18:31, three runs and ZERO completed crawls in six minutes:** `33667151345` (a working `deployment_status` crawl, job 18:25:58) killed at 18:28:14 by `33667308181`, **the ARMED `0 15 * * *` daily** (GitHub-delivered 18:27, best-effort per HO 572); which was itself killed at 18:31:04, **2m46s in**, by `33667584259` — a Preview `deployment_status` run whose job then **skipped** at 18:31:05. So the overflow alarm filed nothing for 2026-09-02 and that day is **indistinguishable from a quiet prod** — the blind window realized on its actual carrier, and precisely what Corey reported as a live fire. **The prior instance, restated with its figure corrected:** dispatch `33673317612` was **cancelled 89s into its ~3-minute crawl** by `33673395814`, a run created **47s after it** — the filed "47s in" measures the gap between the two runs' *creation* timestamps, not depth into the crawl. Re-dispatched clean as `33673524519`. **The fix is the GROUP KEY, not the cancel flag**: `e2e-prod-${{ github.event_name }}-${{ …deployment_status.environment || 'na' }}-${{ …deployment_status.state || 'na' }}` — the job `if`'s own three discriminators, so the group partitions along the same line the skip decision does and every skipped class collides only with itself. **Disposition of all four cancellations in the 72h sweep under the new key:** the 09-01 `33561461443` pair is **preserved** (both Production+success — identical key inputs, same group, the documented deploy-burst collapse, which was the original purpose and is byte-unchanged within its group); chain links 1, 2 and 3 above are all **prevented**. **`state` is kept for a totality property, not a live distinction** — measured at STEP 0 it is a constant `success` across the 62-status corpus (Preview 36 / Production 26), and what it buys is that a group is working iff `event != deployment_status OR (environment == Production AND state == success)`, exactly the key's three discriminators, so a working and a skipped run cannot share a group **for any value Vercel might emit**, including states never observed. **Per-event-only was weighed and rejected:** it closes the alarm's window but leaves a skipped Preview run able to cancel a working Production crawl inside a shared deploy group — same mechanism, narrower victim. **Proven pre-FF, and the proof is real rather than trivial because one handoff premise was false:** `deployment_status` runs check out the **deployment's own ref**, not `main` (`33673395814` ran at `5c9503a` on `683-review`), so the branch already exercised the end-state key on both sides — dispatch `33709162259` logged its group as **`e2e-prod-workflow_dispatch-na-na`**, ran armed, and completed **success, uncancelled**, while `33709247204` (a Preview event pushed mid-crawl at 02:52:12, inside its 02:50:53–02:54:35 job window) **skipped**. Pre-fix that exact shape is the incident. **Transition residual, bounded and harmless:** deploys of pre-fix commits run the old yaml under bare `e2e-prod`, and group matching is exact-string, so they can only cancel each other and can never reach a new-key group.
- ~~**WATCH (HO 685) — the first PROTECTED daily is pending and is not claimed.**~~ — **CLOSED HO 687 (2026-09-03).** The daily fired at **18:22:58Z** (`33790157186`, ~3h22m past its `0 15 * * *` slot — inside the HO 572 best-effort calibration this entry predicted) and **ran to completion**. **The reading needs the honesty this entry's own text demands: its conclusion is `failure`, and that is the close, not a miss.** The protected property is **completion, not greenness** — the fix exists so a crawl is not cancelled by a run doing no work, and this crawl produced a full 42-line route set where the three cancelled predecessors produced 29, 0 and 0. Arming is confirmed in the log (`overflow gate: armed, 1 checks` … `armed, 14 checks`), and **the overflow alarm found nothing** — every armed check clean, no `smoke-overflow-*` row, no issue filed. **The failure is the extant `:50` fire class, not a new one:** 8 fire-lines carrying the byte-identical `Minified React error #418 … args[]=HTML` string, with `home-stage-enacted` firing on all three attempts and so exhausting `--retries=2` — a conclusion shape this ledger has seen on 08-16, 08-23 and 08-28a. **No `deployment_status` event landed inside the run's window**, so this closes on the **bare completion**, not the stronger paired reading: the session's two deploy events (`33788098861` skipped Preview, `33788643959` Production success) both preceded the daily by ~15 minutes. The collision proof already on the record carries the mechanism. *(Superseded WATCH text follows.)* *Owner:* Code. The concurrency fix is verified on `workflow_dispatch` and on a Production `deployment_status` (both post-FF, both quoted in the roadmap block), and the end-state collision passed twice — but **the run class the fix exists to protect is `schedule`, and no scheduled run has fired since it landed** (`9fb71bf`, 2026-09-03 03:26Z; the 09-02 daily is the one that was clipped). Same shape as the HO 660 scheduled-fire WATCH and the same reason: a dispatch proves the route, never the schedule. *Close:* the next `0 15 * * *` run's conclusion + its `overflow gate: armed` line, read off `gh run list --repo CSU-J3/cbt --workflow e2e-prod.yml` — expect it **~3h late**, per the HO 572 best-effort calibration (the 09-01 and 09-02 dailies landed 18:12 and 18:27). **The stronger close, if it happens to arrive:** a `deployment_status` event landing inside that run's window with both conclusions quoted — the daily green and the deploy event skipped — which is the blind window closed on its own carrier rather than on a proxy. Do not manufacture that pair; if no deploy lands in the window, the bare green closes this and the collision proof already on the record carries the mechanism.
- **`e2e/console-noise.ts` is gate-critical (HO 504).** The shared console-error allowlist (extracted HO 504, imported by both specs) now gates a **real** unattended run — `e2e-prod.yml` reds on any un-allowlisted console error — so adding an entry has teeth, not housekeeping. **One of its four entries** is an open loop whose fix will delete it (`MissingSecret` → the NextAuth `AUTH_SECRET` OPEN LOOP above); the file carries per-entry provenance so it stays grep-findable when that loop closes. (The `Encountered two children with the same key` entry was **retired at HO 570 C3** once the `FilingRow` dup-key was fixed at the source — the demonstrated close of a gate entry.) *Promote* if an entry is ever added to silence a *real* regression rather than known-benign noise.
- **Doc duplication between SKILL.md and oddities.md (flagged HO 494; third instance HO 502; fourth instance HO 505 — PROMOTED).** *Owner:* Corey/Code. SKILL.md and oddities.md duplicate the same facts, so every correction is a two-file operation with nothing enforcing the second file. **The first three were caught by noticing** — `/lobbying` went stale twice after shipped changes (HO 491, 494), and the force-dynamic mechanism was corrected in oddities while SKILL kept asserting the old one (HO 502). The fix adopted is the **ownership split**: decide which file OWNS a fact so the other points at it instead of restating it. **What's different about the fourth — why this is a promotion, not another failure:** HO 505 wrote the trigger down *in advance* ("if you catch yourself writing the same sentence into both files, that's the fourth instance"), and it **fired during this sweep, pre-commit** — SKILL's first draft restated the oddities guard-inversion mechanism, was caught against that trigger, and cut to a pointer before the commit landed (oddities owns the mechanism + the 73-test numbers; SKILL names it and links). So the ownership split now works as a **checkable pre-commit rule**, not just a post-hoc catch — the practice succeeded on contact. **Open question (the real residue):** nothing *enforces* the split when no handoff is scripting the check — it held here only because HO 505 had the rule in hand. *Close / next:* decide whether the split can be made self-enforcing (e.g. a doc-test that flags a distinctive sentence appearing in both files) or whether it stays a discipline that depends on someone holding the handoff — until then it's a promoted-but-unenforced practice, not a solved problem. **Fifth instance (HO 538):** SKILL's `getBillAmendmentVotes` line restated the stale "~114" Senate count that HO 537 M8 had corrected to 97 — caught only by *reading the SKILL diff out of the `537-review` ref* (the review-ref route, now written into SKILL.md), not by anything enforcing the split; still unenforced.
- **`fit-finish.spec.ts` manual posture — cost now demonstrated (HO 570).** *Owner:* Corey/Code. The manual/local-only posture is a **deliberate HO 504 call**, not an oversight — oddities records it at the skip-on-empty entry ("still carries the bulk of the guards and stays manual, by design"), and `e2e-prod.yml` runs `smoke.spec.ts` explicitly and never globs `e2e/`. What HO 570 adds is the **demonstrated cost**: the `#lobby-drill` soft-assert red (FIT & FINISH above) sat undetected until someone ran the suite by hand during HO 570 verification — a suite nobody runs doesn't even emit the green the HO 503 skip-on-empty warned about. *Decision to scope when appetite arrives (three named options, not a chore):* (a) add `fit-finish.spec.ts` to `e2e-prod.yml`; (b) promote its stable subset into `smoke.spec.ts`; or (c) keep it manual and **stop counting its greens as coverage**. Until then the posture holds — record the cost, don't reframe the decision. **HO 572 update — the blocker cleared:** the `#lobby-drill` red was fixed (HO 572) and the suite is now green (41 pass / 0 fail), so the three-option decision is **takeable**. One caution before wiring: the newly-found `pageErr` intermittent (OPEN LOOP below) means `smoke.spec.ts` itself is **not reliably green right now**, so adding *more* suites to the unattended runner before that's characterized would pile noise onto a signal that's already firing. **(HO 574 update:** that signal is now *characterized* — React #418 hydration — but still unfixed, so `smoke.spec.ts` remains not-reliably-green and the caution stands.)

- **B-sync transition-logging path unproven (HO 383).** 383's **sync-side** stage-transition logging is typechecked but only fires when a live sync observes a real stage advance (vs the summarize runner, which logs on re-summarize). *Promote / close:* on the first natural stage advance after `cae39fa`, confirm a `stage_transitions` row written **by sync** (not the runner), that it surfaces on `/changes`, and that the runner **noops** on the later re-summarize (no duplicate row). *Instrument:* `validate-stage-transitions-232.ts` + eyeball `/changes` after the first observed advance.
- **Manual `sync:votes` leaves `member_participation` stale (HO 595/596).** *Owner:* Code. The participation refresh hangs off the **route** `/api/sync-votes`, not off the writers. `npm run sync:votes` / `npm run sync:senate-votes` call `runVotesSync` / `runSenateVotesSync` directly, so a manual run writes `member_votes` **without** triggering the refresh and the materialized table sits stale until the next `0 10 * * *` cron. *Instrument (this is the whole reason it exists):* the `refreshed_at` stamp — compare it against `MAX(vote_date)`; `scripts/diagnostic/participation-falsify-595.ts --stamp` prints it. There is **no standalone refresh entry point** today. *Close:* either hang `refreshMemberParticipation()` off the **writer** (`lib/votes-sync.ts` / `lib/senate-votes-sync.ts`) so every write path triggers it, or add an `npm run refresh:participation`. The writer option is the better shape — it makes the invariant "member_votes changed implies the table was rebuilt" structural rather than conventional.
- **Participation refresh headroom (HO 595).** *Owner:* Code. The refresh's own worst was **11.95s WITH the covering index** (M3), which is why it runs on a **dedicated 30s client** rather than `getDb()`'s 10s — the refresh breaches the very bound it exists to let the request path escape. Without the index it is 15.44s median / 27.14s worst, so the index is now load-bearing on the **refresh** path (its purpose inverted; see SKILL). *Watch:* that 30s headroom shrinks as `member_votes` grows (365,996 rows today, ~661/member). *Promote* if the refresh ever times out — the `[participation] refresh-failed:` log line and a non-advancing `refreshed_at` are the tells, and the non-destructive path means a failure shows up as **stale, not empty**. Fix direction would be an incremental refresh (only members whose votes changed) rather than a full rebuild.
- **FEED_DATE_DRIVE_MIN margin watch (HO 382).** The `2000` threshold that routes the giant-bucket feed onto the date-drive index is safe **only** because `committee` (~14.8k) is the lone bucket above it and `floor`'s next is ~800 — a wide margin. It's **corpus-distribution-dependent**: a second bucket climbing past 2000 silently flips onto the date-drive too (a plan change nobody asked for). *Promote* to a re-check if the funnel shape shifts materially (a stage backlog growing). *Glance* whenever the funnel distribution changes; no action while committee stays the lone bucket over 2000.

- **Weekly-report cron mode-specific patches (HO 285 residue).** The HO 285 daily catch-up makes the report cron reliable (a dropped week lands the next day), so these are now optional hardening, not reliability-critical: (1) **tighten the Gemini 503 retry ladder** in `generateReportWithRetry` (`[2000,4000,8000]` ~14s exhausts on a sustained-overload day — #74/#82); (2) **warm/guard the cold Turso connection** on the weekly Monday tick so the first `gatherReportData` query doesn't eat the 10s+10s `boundedFetch` double-timeout (#287 — the HO 238 cold-stall class). The `gatherReportData` ~10-query cold-stall **folds into the gated-aggregate systemic-pass WATCH above** (same statless-planner family). *Promote* only if the catch-up itself starts visibly lagging (a multi-week gap that doesn't drain one-per-day).
- **`/api/sync` heavy-cron soft-timeouts — the absorb-vs-fix call (HO 312 update).** The morning `/api/sync` run soft-times-out intermittently (Turso cold-stall / slow-Gemini, the HO 238 / gated-aggregate family that also touches markets, bills, members) — and **HO 312 confirmed it now delays the report catch-up too**: the Jun 19/20 morning syncs timed out, so the June 8 report didn't generate until the Jun 20 16:57 run, about a day late (absorbed by the catch-up, no user-visible bug). - ~~**Two HO 661 settle windows expire on dates that cross the migration boundary — 2026-08-20 and 2026-09-03 (filed HO 662).**~~ — **READ AND CLOSED HO 686 (2026-09-03).** **Three HEALED, one UNHEALED-with-no-recovery-available, zero recovery owed.** `house-VA-10-2026-R` — winner Dave Beckwith 72.5%, stamped **2026-08-17**, 18 days before its floor. `house-WA-05-2026-open` — two winners (Baumgartner 47.4% R / Conroy 18.9% D; top-two advances two, so two ★ is correct), stamped **2026-08-25**, 10 days before floor. `house-WA-08-2026-open` — two winners (Schrier 53.8% D / Meline 15.9% R), same stamp, same margin. `house-AZ-03-2026-R` — **settled unhealed by expiry**, its own line below. **THE FILED FLOOR DATES WERE ALL ONE DAY EARLY, and the correction is why this entry cannot be re-opened tomorrow.** They were hand arithmetic (`primary_date + 30`) beside a **strict** expiry term (`primary_date < today − 30d`), which first flips at **+31**: AZ-03 floors **2026-08-21** (not 08-20), and the other three float to **2026-09-04** (not 09-03). Re-derived by simulation through the shipped `settleWindowFloor`, never by arithmetic beside it (oddities). **The error was benign twice over.** The three 09-04 floors are moot — all three rows are already frozen on the **decided** term, not the expired one, so **this read is complete and final today, a day before their expiry term could bite**; and the late AZ read lost nothing, because the sync had re-visited that row **inside** its window anyway (last roster write 2026-08-14, seven days before the floor) and the source still carried no winner then. **Premise corrections to this entry, both mine:** it named `race_candidates.updated_at` as the post-hoc timing instrument — wrong table, these are `primary_candidates` rows; the right column is `primary_candidates.updated_at`, a per-run whole-roster write stamp, so the capability was intact and only the pointer was wrong. And its recovery clause promised more than the scripts can deliver — see the AZ-03 line. *Instruments (new, tracked):* `scripts/diagnostic/settle-window-686.ts` (per-row state + floor by simulation, positional ids, positive and negative controls) and `scripts/diagnostic/settle-upstream-686.ts` (live upstream winner read through the scrape path the recovery itself uses, control-validated). The HO 661 set-level instrument was run too and reconciles (`PASS`, +82 observed == +82 predicted).
- **New (HO 686) · NEITHER recovery script can be scoped to one contest id.** *Owner:* Code. Surfaced by HO 686's STEP 0 row 4 and **HALT-worthy on its own** — the settle-window entry above promised `backfill:primary-results` / `reingest:primary-slate` as the per-row recovery, and neither takes a contest. `backfill:primary-results` has exactly one flag, `--all`, which *widens* it; its selection is `SELECT DISTINCT chamber, state, district … WHERE election_round='primary' [AND primary_date < date('now')] AND EXISTS roster` — **every rostered seat corpus-wide**, hundreds of live Ballotpedia fetches. `reingest:primary-slate` takes positional `(STATE, DATE)`, so the narrowest AZ-03 invocation is `AZ 2026-07-21` → **all 18 AZ rows across 9 pages**. Both are narrow in *kind* (UPDATE-only on `(primary_id, name)`, written only when the scraped share is non-NULL, never delete/re-insert, and both bypass `isSettled` — the documented escape hatch), so the blast radius is re-writing scraped values over scraped values rather than erasure. **Do NOT read that as safe-therefore-fine:** it means a one-row correction cannot be requested, only a slate-or-corpus re-scrape that happens to include it. *Promote to a fix* — a `--primary <id>` flag on `reingest-primary-slate` is the small version — the first time a recovery is genuinely owed on a single contest. Today none is.
- **New (HO 686) · `house-AZ-03-2026-R` settled unhealed, and the gap is UPSTREAM — trigger-gated.** *Owner:* Code. Settled by **expiry** on 2026-08-21 carrying one candidate, Nicholas Glenn (R) at 100%, `status='running'`, no winner. **Ballotpedia genuinely carries no winner for that one contest** — read live through the scrape path, `status=ok`, and **control-validated** against WA-08 on the same run (upstream 2 winners, matching our DB exactly), so the zero is real absence and not a blind parser. **It is not the uncontested-contest class:** AZ-03-R is the **only one of 18 AZ contests** without a winner, including seven *other* single-candidate contests and the **D contest on the very same page** (Ansari, 100%, marked winner). A genuine single-contest source anomaly. **Both recovery scripts are structurally no-ops on it** — each re-scrapes that page and writes `status = c.isWinner ? 'winner' : 'running'`, i.e. `running`, the value already stored. **Severity is bounded and that is why this is a WATCH and not a loop:** `AZ-03-2026` is **unrated** (`race_ratings` = 0), so `getRacesIndex` excludes it and `harvestChallengers` never reaches it — the missing winner has no downstream consumer today. *Promote:* if AZ-03-2026 ever acquires a rating or enters the races index, the missing winner becomes visible and the row needs a **hand decision**, since no script can supply it. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/settle-upstream-686.ts`.
- **New (HO 686) · 78 rows newly settle by expiry, 75 of them pageless — and this is NOT the 32-non-parsing-districts family.** *Owner:* Code. Class 4 of the HO 661 instrument (past-dated, shareless, uncalled, age ≥ 30d). **Checked against that family rather than assumed onto it, and they do not match:** only **29** of the 75 pageless rows fall inside the sixteen non-parsing districts above; **46 rows across 45 other seats** sit outside it — AL-01/03/05/07 · AR-04 · KY-01/03/05 · NC-01/03/04/06/07/08/09 · NJ-08 · NV-04 · NY-01/04/06/07/09/10/11/12/13/14/15/17/19/23/24/25 · OK-01/05 · PA-03 · SC-03/04/05/07 · SD-SEN · SD-00 · UT-01/02/03. So the known non-parsing set describes well under half of the pageless population and a broader coverage gap is unaccounted for. The remaining **3** with-roster rows are a third thing again: the seeded runoffs `senate-GA-2026-R-runoff`, `senate-LA-2026-D-runoff`, `senate-LA-2026-R-runoff`, seeded (HO 107/174) and never results-backfilled. **These rows settling is not itself a defect** — expiry is the predicate working as designed on contests nothing will ever mark. What is filed here is the **size and shape of the pageless population**, which nothing else carries. *Promote:* re-run the HO 563 `no_section` probe against the 45 outside seats to find whether they are a second non-parsing cohort or a different mechanism. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/settle-predicate-661.ts` class 4.
- **New (HO 686) · the next settle windows: `house-AK-00-2026-open` and `senate-AK-2026-open`, floor 2026-09-18.** *Owner:* Code/cron. The successor to the entry struck above. Both are `primary_date` **2026-08-18**, `primary_type` **top_four**, scored, **uncalled**, and **not yet frozen** as of 2026-09-03 (15 days before floor) — house 15 candidates led by Begich (R, INC) 46.1%, senate 16 led by Peltola (D) 48.1% over Sullivan (R, INC) 42.7%. **The floors are DERIVED BY RUNNING THE INSTRUMENT** (`npx tsx scripts/diagnostic/settle-window-686.ts house-AK-00-2026-open senate-AK-2026-open`), **not by arithmetic** — that is the whole correction the struck entry carries, and stating it here is what stops the off-by-one recurring. **Expect these to settle unhealed, and that is probably correct rather than a defect:** top-four RCV is the structurally-unmarkable shape HO 661 named — there is no single advancer for a votebox to mark — so the expiry term exists precisely so these stop being re-hammered forever. **What to read on 2026-09-18:** whether Ballotpedia marked advancers before the floor passed, using the same two instruments; if it did not, confirm the shape is RCV-structural before treating it as owed anything. the decided-or-expired predicate (`SETTLE_WINDOW_DAYS` = **30**), an unfrozen contest is re-check-eligible until its floor passes and then settles regardless of whether anyone marked a winner. **Four rows are inside their window right now:** `house-AZ-03-2026-R` (unfrozen Jul 21) expires **2026-08-20**; `house-VA-10-2026-R`, `house-WA-05-2026-open` and `house-WA-08-2026-open` (all Aug 4) expire **2026-09-03**. **What to read on each date:** whether Ballotpedia marked a winner before the floor passed. If it did, the row healed itself and nothing is owed. If it did not, the row settles **unhealed** and the recovery is `backfill:primary-results` / `reingest:primary-slate` — which is already today's reality for that class, since the pre-661 eternal retries never healed them either. **This is stated here because nothing else carries it:** the dates live in the HO 661 roadmap block as narrative, chat history does not survive the migration, and the windows close silently either way.
- **README's cron paragraph is three topologies stale (found HO 662 §1, deliberately not widened).** *Owner:* Code, cheap, rides the next README touch. `README.md:82` still reads *"Vercel Hobby caps cron at once per day, so the topology is split"* — **Pro since HO 433**, and `vercel.json` now declares **17 native crons** including sub-daily ones (`/api/sync` every 6h, summarize `*/10`, news `*/30`, markets `0 */4` plus the FMP window). `README.md:89`'s **GitHub Actions** bullet describes `markets-tick.yml` / `markets-daily.yml` / `kalshi-tick.yml` as supplying the intraday refreshes — **all three were retired at HO 475**; `.github/workflows/` holds only `ci.yml` and `e2e-prod.yml`. And the surviving Vercel bullet's "staggered daily chain" cadences no longer match the file. **Not fixed here** because HO 662 §1's scope was the Project-structure section and the sweep does not widen into neighbouring prose; the correction is a README commit of its own.
- **New (HO 678) · `TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN` is non-expiring, and it reaches Preview.** *Owner:* Corey. Decoded from the local `.env`: `EdDSA`, claims `a, iat, id, rid`, `iat` **2026-05-02T18:00:20Z**, and **no `exp` claim at all** — so it does not age out, and its only end is an explicit invalidation. Age **121 days** by the JWT, **120d** by Vercel's `created` column; the two agree within a day, which is the one place in this audit where a provider-independent age instrument existed and it corroborated Vercel's. Held **Production + Preview**, so it ships to every preview build, **including every review-ref push**. No exposure found (history, tracked tree, ignored files, bundle and all 58 DB tables all clean), so this is a standing observation rather than a rotation. **Note for whoever does rotate it:** `turso db tokens invalidate` kills **every** token for the database including one just minted, so the order is invalidate → create → `vercel env add` per environment → `.env` → redeploy, with a short outage — and the Turso CLI is **not installed on the Windows box**, so that path is the web dashboard today.
- **New (HO 689) · the first CRON-generated week summary is unverified — every stored row today is a backfill.** *Owner:* Code/cron. HO 689 wired three-sentence generation into `/api/cron/weekly-report` (Mon 09:30 UTC) and proved the path by running it, but through `npm run backfill:week-summaries`, which calls the same `buildWeekSummaryPayload → generateWeekSummary → UPDATE` the route calls. **Same functions is not the same carrier** — the route adds the Gemini client construction, the non-fatal try/catch, the `chronicErr` forwarding and the `payload.report.summary` line, none of which a backfill exercises (the HO 685 protected-daily shape: a dispatch proves the route, never the schedule). **Close:** the first unattended Monday tick — quote its `cron_runs` row, specifically `payload.report.summary` (`{chars}` on success, `null` on refusal) and any `chronicErr` beginning `week-summary`, and confirm the stored `summary_text` for that week. **Both outcomes are informative and neither is a failure of this WATCH:** a stored summary closes it; a refusal closes it too, provided the refusal is legible in the row rather than silent — that legibility is the actual thing being watched.
- **New (HO 689) · FILED · the grounding gate is a SUBSET check, not a semantic one — it cannot catch a misattributed number.** *Owner:* Code. `groundingGate` (`lib/week-summary.ts`) requires every numeral in the generated text to appear in the payload, which kills invented magnitudes (control: `9999 / 4242 / 777` → red, run through the shipped gate). **It cannot distinguish a real number attached to the wrong label**: "technology with 15 counts" passes when 15 belongs to `government_operations`, because 15 is in the payload. **Deliberately not built at HO 689 and the reason is the cost of a bad fix, not the cost of a good one** — robust numeral↔label pairing over free prose is a semantic parse, and a cheap heuristic would reject VALID summaries, which is strictly worse than a stated limit (a false red on a correct summary silently degrades the surface to the stale line). *Promote* if a generated summary is ever observed misattributing a figure; the limit is stated at the function so a reader cannot over-trust it.

- **New (HO 691) · primary-phase PAC lines age into the general, and a target the classifier cannot place keeps rendering in the present tense.** *Owner:* Code. `getPacIeSpending` classifies every `pac_ie_spending` target `active` / `lost` / `withdrew` / `unknown`, and **`unknown` renders exactly as an `active` target does** — deliberately, because the alternative is a real FEC filing disappearing off the surface whenever a name cannot be matched. That safety choice means a classifier gap is **invisible on screen by design**, so it has to be watched in the data instead. **TWO ways a line stays `unknown`, and the second is the one HO 691 added:** (1) the seat's primary result was never backfilled into `primary_candidates` — no contest row, or rows with no `vote_pct` — so nothing can be derived; (2) **the ROUND dimension** — the target won a primary round whose seat carries a past-dated `runoff_date` and the **runoff round was never scraped**, so we know they advanced and not what happened next. **Instrument:** `npx tsx scripts/diagnostic/pac-target-status-691.ts`, run at each `sync:pac-ie`; it prints the per-row verdict with the classifier's own `why` string, plus a per-seat ROUNDS line that flags `later round known, NO resulted row`. **Reading at 2026-09-04: 7 `active` · 6 `lost` · 0 `withdrew` · 5 `unknown`** — Hostettler (IN-08) and Bowman (NY-16) have no contest row at all, Malinowski (NJ-11, two `candidate_id`s rendering as one item) is absent from that seat's roster, and Herrera (TX-23) is case (2), live. **What a zero here would mean, and what it would NOT:** `unknown = 0` means every stored target was placed against a resulted contest — it does **not** mean the classifications are right, because a target matched into the *wrong* contest also classifies confidently. And a **rising** `unknown` count is the real signal: it means new spend is landing on seats whose results this corpus has not ingested, i.e. the surface is quietly drifting back toward the pre-691 state one seat at a time. **The count is not self-clearing** — the fix for (1) is `backfill:primary-results` / `reingest:primary-slate`, and there is **no path at all** for (2): nothing in the repo scrapes a runoff round (the corpus holds 3 runoff rows, all hand-seeded by HO 107/174), so TX-23 stays `unknown` until a runoff ingestion exists or the general resolves it. *Close criterion:* not a loop that closes on a number — it expires when either a runoff-round ingestion lands (removing cause 2) or the PAC's target set is empty for the cycle.

- **New (HO 692) · a new prediction-market render site shipped WITHOUT the odds class is invisible to every gate this repo has.** *Owner:* Code. Fifteen sites are gated by `html[data-odds="off"]` via `.odds-only` / `.odds-off-only`; a sixteenth added later simply keeps rendering when a reader has turned the markets off. **Nothing catches it automatically, and the reason is structural: the unattended crawl never sets the preference, so CI only ever measures the ON path** — where an ungated site is, by definition, correct. The typechecker cannot see a missing class, and the ON-path baseline (`scripts/diagnostic/artifacts-692/on-*.json`) is designed to prove the ON render did NOT change, so it stays green too. **Instrument:** re-run the row-2 enumeration at each HO touching races, the tapes, or the bill panel — `grep -rni "kalshi\|polymarket\|SourceTag\|impliedPct\|favoriteLabel" components app --include=*.tsx` — and classify every hit either into the gated set or into "not gated" with a reason; **zero means no ungated market site**, and at HO 692 the four hits outside the table were a news `sourceTag()` false positive, a comment, and two `KalshiLine` consumers already covered by the shared root class. **Second instrument, for the one site a grep cannot judge:** `scripts/diagnostic/odds-leader-fallback-692.ts`, which renders `RaceCard` through `react-dom/server` and asserts the `leader` line's dual render — that the market reading sits inside `odds-only` and the fallback is byte-identical to a real `nolead` card's own challenger line. **That harness is the ONLY check on that branch**, because there is no `leader` seat on the live dashboard to capture (all six featured cards are general/nominee/nolead; `.rc-dagger` counts zero), and it will stay that way while primaries keep resolving — so its absence from a future run is not evidence the branch is fine. **What a zero does NOT mean:** it does not mean a gated site still WORKS — a site can carry the class and be defeated by a later CSS rule or a grid re-flow — which is what the geometry baseline and the OFF capture are for. *Close criterion:* not a loop that closes on a number; it expires if the preference is ever removed, or if the crawl is taught to run a second pass with the preference set (which would move this from a review rule to a gate, and is the fix worth making if a site is ever found missing).

## FIT & FINISH

- **New (HO 682) · the `min-width: auto` latent class — 21 sites on `/` share the signature, none is defective today.** *Owner:* Code, low; **forward-scoped, NOT a sweep** (SKILL carries the rule for new and touched code). Enumerated on the live page at 2560 and 1440, signature = a **grid or flex item whose computed `min-width` is `auto` and whose subtree contains a `nowrap` + `ellipsis` + `overflow:hidden` descendant**: `ul.abw-card > li` ×14 (Absence Watch cards), `ul.topic-dist > li` ×8, `ul.stage-funnel > li` ×6, the breaking-panel `ul`, and the two `.hsch-wrap > div` this HO fixed; off `/`, `/bills` carries 25 (`li.feed-row … span.row-meta`) and `/lobbying` 23. **Only three elements actually overflowed and all three were on the hearings chain**, so the class is latent everywhere else — the others are sound only because nothing has yet handed them an unbounded string, which is exactly the condition that changed on `/` on 2026-09-02. `/bills`, `/members`, `/lobbying`, `/news` and `/hearings` all read `docScrollWidth == docClientWidth` at both widths. **Named so the next reader does not re-find them:** the markets tape (7926/899), the `v2f-title` spans and the `breaking-row-headline` anchors DO each overflow their own box and are **fine by construction** — every one of them clips itself, so none reaches the document scroller; they appear in any overflow enumeration and are not findings. *Close:* this line is a register, not a task — it is struck when the SKILL rule has been applied to each site by ordinary touching, or tombstoned if a later HO rules the sweep worth doing in one pass. **Instrument note (HO 682 review):** M0's predicate is `over > 1`, a 1px subpixel tolerance its comment block does not state; fold the clause into the comment on the next touch of `layout-audit-606.ts`. **ANNOTATED HO 684 — the register gained two sites and lost a premise.** The two are `.rc-moved` and `.rc-new`, which HO 684 turned from bare labels into content-carrying chips; both were built **compliant by construction** (`min-width: 0` + `max-width: 100%` on the chip, the ellipsis kit on an inner body span), so they enter the enumeration already discharged rather than as debt. **`.race-card` was severed in the same commit** and is the more useful half: it is a grid item at `min-width: auto` and was **not** in this register's enumeration, which is a coverage gap in the enumeration rather than in the rule — the sweep looked for items whose subtree *already* held a nowrap+ellipsis descendant, and this card acquired one only when HO 684 put it there. **A latent-class register keyed on today's descendants cannot see a site that becomes a member later**, which is precisely the forward-scoping the SKILL rule exists for. **And one premise this register inherits is now measured false on `/`:** HO 610's note that the race grid's overflow was *"invisible as a document scroll only because an ancestor clipped it"* does not describe the live DOM — `RacesBoxTabs` is a direct child of `.dash-left` and the dashboard's only `overflow:hidden` is `.home-quadrant`/`-body`, which is not on that chain, so an overflowing card reaches the document scroller.

- **74 bills of the 119th Congress are referenced as related bills but are absent from `bills` (found HO 674).** The roster ingestion surfaced a completeness gap in the corpus itself: of 10,254 stored related-bill rows, **82 do not resolve to a `bills.id` (99.20% do)**, and **74 of those 82 are 119th-Congress ids** — the Congress the corpus is supposed to hold in full. Only 8 are prior-Congress (118: 6, 117: 2), which are expected and unremarkable since the corpus is 119th-only. The 74 are **ordinary types the sync does ingest** — `s` 41, `hr` 17, `sres` 11, `hres` 3, `hconres` 2, against a corpus holding 5,088 `s` and 9,835 `hr` — and they skew to high numbers (`119-hr-9189`, `119-hr-9224`, `119-hr-9440`), which points at recent introductions the paged sync has not reached rather than at a type or filter exclusion. Not investigated further: HO 674's scope was ingestion of the rosters, not of `bills`. **Instrument note:** the first attempt to characterise these grouped by an unqualified alias and reported *all 82 as 117th-Congress*, which would have made this a nothing-to-see prior-Congress artifact; the corrected query reports the 119-dominant split (oddities). *Close:* either the 74 are ingested and the resolve rate goes to ~99.9% (the 8 prior-Congress ids never resolve by design), or the sync gap that omits them is named and recorded as accepted, with the count re-measured either way.
- **`/` overflows horizontally by 169px at 430 (found HO 673, pre-existing — NOT caused by that HO).** `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` reads **599** against a `clientWidth` of **430** with a feed row expanded. Measured on **both sides** of the HO 673 change and **identical** in each — 599/430 before, 599/430 after — so the always-on portrait neither causes nor worsens it; the control is what makes that a finding about the page rather than about the commit. Every wider viewport in the set (1920 / 1100 / 1440, including the reduced-motion pass) reads no overflow, so it is specific to the narrow breakpoint. Not investigated further: HO 673's ruling was the sponsor block, and the overflowing element was not identified. *Close:* name the element whose box exceeds the viewport at 430 (not the page — the element), fix it, and show `scrollWidth == clientWidth` at 430 with a row expanded; the before-reading of 599 is the control that makes the after-reading meaningful. **AMENDED HO 682 — the control does not reproduce, and this entry is NOT closed.** Re-measured 2026-09-02 on the HO 682 build, 430 viewport, first feed row expanded: **430 / 430, clean** — not the 599 recorded here. **It is not this HO's fix:** with `.hsch-wrap > div { min-width: auto }` reconstructed in the page, the same configuration reads **4586 / 430**, which is the HO 682 hearings blowout and not a 169px anything, and re-removing it returns 430 / 430. So the element this line names is simply **not overflowing today** and was never measured against a RED control in that state, which leaves three live readings — 599 (HO 673), 4586 (HO 682 defect), 430 (now) — and no attribution between the first and the third. Most likely the 169px is data-dependent too, in which case a clean reading proves as little here as the green captures did for HO 682. **Stays open with its original close criterion**, which needs one addition: name the element, and take the RED control **in the same session** as the green, since a control recorded on a different day's data is not a control.
- **The sponsor party bracket renders twice on the raw-name fallback path (found HO 673, pre-existing).** When a surface's `FeedBill` carries no `sponsor_first_name`/`sponsor_last_name`, `SponsorMeta` falls back to the raw `sponsor_name`, which **already embeds the bracket** (`"Sen. Armstrong, Alan [R-OK]"`), and then renders `bill.sponsor_party`/`sponsor_state` as a second bracket beside it — `"Sen. Armstrong, Alan [R-OK] [R-OK]"`. Reproduced on `/members/A000383` and `/members/G000608`, where the sponsor enrichment degrades to null (the documented behaviour at `lib/queries.ts:146`). **Pre-existing and captured on both sides of HO 673**: the before capture shows the duplication inline on one line, the after shows it stacked (name line, then bracket line) because that HO moved the bracket into a column. HO 673 did not fix it — the ruling was the portrait, and the fix is a decision about which bracket wins, not a layout tweak. *Close:* on a surface with no enriched name, exactly one bracket renders; verified on both named member hubs above, with a control on a surface that DOES enrich (`/bills`, where the name is built from first/last and only one bracket has ever rendered) to prove the fix did not just delete the bracket everywhere.
*Audit findings, severity-tagged. P1 = wrong data shown · P2 = inconsistent across surfaces / misleading · P3 = cosmetic.*

- **P3 · C5 — the dashboard renders the SAME two counts twice each, and both pairs are ONE predicate (found by eye HO 634, verified, filed NOT fixed; NOT 634 scope).** *Owner:* Corey. **C5 is a review rule, so an eye-catch is its intended mechanism — but "two call sites that agree today" is not a C5 hit, and that is what the verification settles.** Both pairs resolve to the **same cached function**, not to two predicates that happen to agree: **243** — `getStageChangesCount({}, 7)` at `components/WeeklyBand.tsx:166` renders "243 STAGE TRANSITIONS ▲15", and `getStageChangesCount({}, 7, filters)` at `app/page.tsx:102` feeds `activityCount.total` → `ActivityTabs` "MOVERS (243)". **138** — `getNewBillsThisWeekCount()` at `components/WeeklyBand.tsx:167` renders "138 NEW BILLS ▲33", and the **identical zero-arg call** at `app/page.tsx:103` feeds `newCount` → "NEW THIS WEEK (138)". The stage-changes count is in fact read at a **third** site on the same page, `components/ActivityTicker.tsx:57`, for its `+N more` remainder. *The one real asymmetry, and it is by design:* the tab count passes `filters` and the week strip does not — the strip is corpus-wide, the tab rebases on an active filter — so the two **legitimately diverge under a filter** and are identical unfiltered, which is the state they were seen in. *Recommendation:* **the week strip survives** — it carries the WoW deltas (▲15, ▲33), the sparkline and the hover breakdown, which the bare tab count does not; the tab label is the redundant render. *Promote* on a dashboard density pass. *Do not* "fix" by making the strip read the filtered count — that would break the strip's corpus-wide contract.

- ~~**P3 — `ActiveFilterStrip` is a far-right-anchored row (1,347px interior @2560), visible only when a filter is active; pack left per C1.**~~ — **CLOSED HO 611** (`c42ddb1`), as the predicted rider on P4's first HO. **The lesson outlives the fix and is why the entry keeps its text: this row was invisible to every unfiltered crawl.** `/` read M1 0 at the end of P3 while `/?stage=committee` — the same page with a filter active — held one row at 1,347px. **Conditional chrome is a coverage class, not a defect class:** any element that renders only in a state the crawl does not enter is unmeasured, and the audit's route list is where that gets decided. `--route home-stage-committee` exists in the route list for exactly this reason and should not be pruned as a duplicate of `/`.
- **P3 — narrow-width debt on `/` (M5), opened by HO 610.** *Owner:* Code. **P3-cosmetic, wide-viewport phase deliberately did not chase it.** The dashboard's narrow ladder moved slightly the WRONG way across P3 slice 3: at **720px, overflowing elements 82 → 89**, and **extra-wrap +1 at both 900 and 720** (the audit's flex-line count vs the same containers at 2560). Two causes, both from HO 610 and both understood: the hearings **week ribbon** is five fixed 30px cells plus a trailing total on one line, and the **masthead** gained SIGN IN as an inline item when its spacer was deleted, so the line has one more thing to wrap. Neither is a defect at wide widths, which is the only place P3 was scored. Full ladder for the record at the P4 baseline (`e6bbc08`): `/` **1024 → 88 overflow / 4 extra-wrap · 900 → 78 / 4 · 720 → 89 / 3**. **Do not fix this by re-pinning anything to the right** — that trades a narrow-width wrap for the C1 defect the phase just removed; the ribbon wants a wrap rule and the masthead wants its items to wrap in a sensible order. `/members` remains far worse (721/222/1297) and is P4's C8 work, not this.
  **UPDATE HO 615 — THE INSTRUMENT HALF IS RESOLVED AND MOST OF THIS ENTRY'S ANXIETY WAS ABOUT A NUMBER WITH NO DEFECT IN IT.** M5's overflow population now splits: **M5x** = the element's OWN computed style declares `text-overflow: ellipsis` or a line clamp (authored truncation, permanent, un-actionable) vs **bucket (b)** = real. Re-measured under v3: **`/members` 721 / 4 / 472 at 1024/900/720 is 100% M5x, bucket (b) 0/0/0** — every one of the 1,297 is an authored `truncate`, and the "worst narrow-width surface on the site" had nothing to fix. `/bills` 3/3/0 is also all M5x. **The product residue that survives, with its numbers:** `/` bucket (b) **88 / 78 / 86**, of which the overwhelming majority is **the markets marquee counted at six nesting levels** (`markets-tape--scroll` 7519/976 · `-track` 7542/7538 · `-half` · `-set` · `-item`), plus at 720 the three ancestors carrying its real **+85px document overflow** (`home-shell` / `home-main` / `dash-left`, all 805/720). **So `/`'s one genuine narrow-width defect is the +85px document overflow at 720, caused by the tape, and it is a single item rather than 86.** The ribbon/masthead wrap causes above are unchanged and still un-chased. **Do not fix by re-pinning anything right** — unchanged. The marquee's classification is its own new entry below.

- **P3 · the HEARINGS↔RACES flip jumps the box 980px → 102px, and nothing steadies it (measured HO 657; PRE-EXISTING, not a 657 regression).** *Owner:* Code. **657 did not cause this and did not change it in either direction** — it measured the jump only while falsifying the handoff's height-lock premise (§2.3 asked to "keep whatever prevents the box from jumping", on the assumption that something did). **CORRECTED HO 684 — BOTH FIGURES BELOW ARE STALE, and the jump gained a second variable.** Re-measured at 1440 on the HO 684 build: the flip is **485px on HEARINGS → 1861px on RACES**, not the 980 → 102 recorded here; the hearings interior and the races body have each grown since HO 657, so the entry's *ratio* survived while both its numbers expired. **The new variable is visit state:** with the HO 684 chips on screen RACES renders **1861px on a first visit and 1654px on a re-open**, a **207px** swing that is the chips being present or absent. The box height is therefore visit-state-dependent by design, which a height treatment has to account for and which no earlier framing of this entry could have. The original reading follows, as the record of what HO 657 measured — **What the measurement found:** the box goes **980px on RACES to 102px on HEARINGS**, and `.races-panel-body`'s `min-height` is **inert at current content** — the body renders **879px against a 240px floor**, so the floor does not bind and the rule is not what was holding the height. It was **SUB-tab parity besides**: it steadied COMPETITIVE↔PRIMARIES, a flip that no longer exists. The floor is kept as a floor for a shrunken body, not as a jump guard. *Close:* **a deliberate height treatment under its own number, or the jump accepted on the record.** The treatment is deliberately **not proposed here** — scoping it is the future HO's work, and picking one in this entry would pre-empt that.

- **NEW (HO 685, carried from the HO 684 close) · `fit-finish.spec.ts` and `playwright.config.ts` disagree about where "no `BASE_URL`" points, and the disagreement fails as a FALSE RED.** *Owner:* Code. `e2e/fit-finish.spec.ts:25` defaults `BASE_URL` to **`http://localhost:3000`**; `playwright.config.ts:6-7` defaults `baseURL` to **prod**, and `:21` is what every relative `page.goto` resolves against. The spec pins its gate cookie to its own constant — `GATE_COOKIE = { name: "ct_seen", …, url: BASE_URL }` at `:48`, added per test at `:95` — so a prod-pointed run with the env var unset **sets the cookie on localhost and navigates to prod**, arrives without `ct_seen`, and is bounced to `/welcome`. Every `/`-dependent locator then misses. **The reason this earns a line rather than a shrug is the failure's shape: at the locator level it is indistinguishable from BotID withholding content and from a genuine regression** — three very different causes, one identical red, and nothing in the output names which. Demonstrated at the HO 684 STEP 5 first prod run and discriminated there only by a raw fetch. **`smoke.spec.ts:23-24` defaults to prod and therefore agrees with the config** — the divergence is fit-finish's alone, which is also why it stayed invisible: the spec that runs unattended is the one that is consistent. *Close:* one default, or the spec reads the config's `baseURL` instead of keeping its own. Related but distinct from the duplicated-collector line below — same two files, different defect (that one is drift between copies, this one is a copy that was never right).

*Commit-2 audit (2026-06-12, measure-only):*

- **P2 · `/changes` · corpus-query latency near the HO 238 bound.** Prod renders 200 but in **6.69s** — within ~3.3s of the 10s `DB_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`. The HO 238 bound converts *slow-but-working* into a hard 500: corpus growth or a slow tick would push this corpus-wide aggregation past 10s → 500, not just slow. Same latency clock as the `/trends` force-dynamic tombstone. *Mitigation:* the BANKED dashboard fan-out/caching pass. (`/patterns` prod 0.82s — fine.) **Promote-criterion (the fuse):** move the BANKED caching pass to QUEUED-top when `/changes` prod latency crosses **8.5s**, or on its **first 10s-bound 500**. The 238 bound made latency a hard failure mode, so the headroom number is a fuse — act on it before it blows, not after. **HO 404 re-measure — well under fuse:** prod `/changes` returned 200 in **0.25–0.76s** (warm) at the audit; the cold-start query-plan audit shows `getStageChanges` index-only (GOOD) and `getStageChangesCount` timed 29ms. So there's no structural latency issue on the current corpus — the 6.69s Jun-12 reading was not reproduced (a true cold read wasn't captured; the function stayed warm). Fuse unchanged; item stays as a standing watch, not an active concern.

The local suite landed 28 pass / 3 fail BY DESIGN: the 3 reds were the three findings below (committee #418 · lobbying `?issue=` drop · feed-Esc), asserted as soft-fails so the suite doubled as the pass-criteria for the fix HO. **HO 473 (2026-07-16) fixed all three** — the two soft-asserts were promoted to hard, and the suite is now **31 pass / 0 fail**; findings #1–#3 are struck below, #4 stays banked. (**CI is still not wired** — CI-hardening OPEN LOOP.)*

- ~~**P2 · `/committee/[systemCode]` (+ `/trends`) · uncaught React hydration mismatch (#418) on every committee page.**~~ **RESOLVED HO 473** (`fd74357`) — collapsed the four multi-child SVG `<title>` sites to a single child (`CommitteeActivityChart` + `BillsTimeSeries` were the live/latent throwers; `PrimaryDistrictModal` + `RaceDistrictModal` fixed for class-consistency, byte-identical text). `/committee/[systemCode]` + `/trends` now console-clean, no #418; `grep "<title>"` shows only single-child titles. *Original:* loaded `/committee/hlig00` (+ `ssju00`, `hsju00`) in a real browser; each throws an uncaught `#418` ("Hydration failed because the server rendered HTML didn't match the client. As a result this tree will be regenerated on the client"). Data-independent (all 3 committees). *Root cause (dev-trace-pinned):* `components/CommitteeActivityChart.tsx:145-147` — the bar `<title>` has **multiple adjacent JSX children** `{formatMonthLabel(month)} · {bucket} · {value}` inside an SVG `<title>`; React's SSR text-segment markers don't survive the browser's SVG-`<title>` parse → hydration fails → React client-regenerates the chart subtree (flash + wasted client work; no wrong data shown, hence P2 not P1). The **same idiom is in `components/BillsTimeSeries.tsx:124`** (`{formatMonthLabel(month)} · {topicLabel(topic)} · {value}`), so `/trends` carries the same latent bug (not exercised locally — force-dynamic corpus page). `PrimaryTimeline`'s `<title>` uses a single template-literal child → unaffected — which is the fix shape: collapse each to one string child, `{`${formatMonthLabel(month)} · ${bucket} · ${value}`}`. *Reproduced on the local prod build; near-certainly live on Vercel prod (same build output).*
- ~~**P2 · `/lobbying` · pagination silently drops the selected `?issue=` drill.**~~ **RESOLVED HO 473** (`cc416fa`) — `app/lobbying/page.tsx` now builds a `carry` from the raw `params.issue` (only when present, so it never injects `?issue=` on the unfiltered feed) and hands it to `<Pagination>`, matching `/amendments` + `/nominations`. *Original:* loaded `/lobbying?issue=HCR`, clicked NEXT in the recent-filings pager → URL became `/lobbying?page=2` (the `issue=HCR` is gone). The Section-2 IssueDrill then re-selects the default top issue (`issues[0]`), so a reader paging the feed loses the per-code drill they were reading. *Root cause:* `app/lobbying/page.tsx` passes `carry={new URLSearchParams()}` (empty) to `<Pagination>`, unlike `/nominations`, `/amendments`, and `/trades` — all of which pass a populated `carry` that preserves their filters across pagination. So `/lobbying` is the lone aggregate surface that drops its filter param on NEXT. *Close:* build the `carry` from `issue` (mirror the other three surfaces).
- ~~**P3 · `/` feed row expand · Esc does not close an expanded row.**~~ **RESOLVED HO 473** (`156dfe5`) — added an Escape `keydown` effect to `components/useSingleOpenPanel.ts` (guarded on `expandedId === null`), so all four consumers (`BillRowList`, `TopStallsList`, `V2FeedList`, `CompetitiveRacesStrip`) inherit Esc-to-close; the electoral modal's independent handler is untouched. *Original:* expanded a `.v2f-row`, pressed Escape — the row stays open (`.v2f-group.open` count = 1). The single-open invariant and re-click-to-close both work correctly; **only Esc is a no-op.** *Root cause:* `components/V2FeedList.tsx` / `useSingleOpenPanel` wires Enter/Space to toggle but has no Escape-to-close handler. Note the app is internally inconsistent here — the electoral `RaceDistrictModal` DOES close on Esc (verified), the inline feed expand doesn't. Arguably an inline accordion doesn't require Esc (hence P3), but the HO 472 brief named "re-click / Esc closes" as a thing to verify, and Esc fails. *Close:* add an Escape keydown to close the open panel, or accept as by-design.
- **P3 · `/amendments` status hero is a non-interactive strip while `/nominations`' equivalent disposition strip filters.** *What:* the `/amendments` "By status" segmented bar segments are title-only `<div>`s (0 clickable — logged live); the sibling `/nominations` disposition strip segments are `<Link>`s that rebase `?disposition=`. *Judgment:* **not a dead affordance** — `/amendments` offers the same disposition filter via its "Acted"/"Filed-only" chips, so the hero bar is an informational distribution readout, not a broken control. Filed only as a **cross-surface consistency** nit: two sibling aggregate surfaces present the same "status bar" affordance differently. *Close (optional):* either make the amendments hero segments filter-links too, or leave as an intentional readout.

*Audited and correct (no finding), recorded so the audit is legible (HO 472 CDP pass):*

## DONE

- ~~**`BillExpandPanel` collapses in a narrow container (HO 609 finding → HO 610)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (`2ca2c96`), prod-verified at `a620e20`. Inside `/`'s 400px feed column the panel is 372px and its 1.35fr/1fr body resolved to **99px / 273px — 71px of prose, 1.4 words per line over 48 lines**. **The fr ratio was not the cause:** the metabox's ~273px min-content is a floor the 1fr track cannot go under, so the entire squeeze lands on the summary — worth knowing before tuning fr values on any two-column panel. Fixed with a **container query on the panel's own width** (stacks below ~440px), NOT a viewport query and NOT a raised column floor: `/bills` renders the same component at 1108–2228px and never crosses the threshold, so one component serves two containers with no route branch. After: **6.9 words/line over 10 lines** on `/`, `/bills` unchanged in shape. **The container query also made `.bxp` a containing block for absolute descendants** — the sponsor hover card lives inside it and was re-checked (still resolves against `.bxp-sponsor`, still on screen).

Code §7-11-55** because the deceased Republican nominee had general-election opposition (verified 2026-08-05, SC Election Commission notice + Ballotpedia corroboration). **Not dormant** — it fed `getPrimaryCalendar` -> `PrimaryTimeline` and `getDashboardPrimaries`, inflating the Aug 11 tick **48 -> 47** contests; the footprint was a **count inflation, not a rendered label**. Both halves in one commit (the seed loader has no delete pass, so either half alone is undone by the other); delete was one literal id, print-before-delete, dry-run default, zero-candidate gate enforced at runtime. Seed note rewritten with the statute, its sources and the verification date. See roadmap "Also (HO 600-601)". Docs: HO 602.

Also: specialness-aware dedup key (Mark Lynch runs in both SC contests), the C2 regular-page fallback kept for a **cursor-driven** reason (the senate slice may not reach SC before Aug 11), the special pass filtered to `isSpecial` so it writes only its invoked ids, and C3's result object replacing a `string[]` that **could not read FAIL**. - ~~**member_votes self-heal (HO 567)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (diag `26879b5` · C1 `547d358` · C2 `f6aab1b`), prod-verified. Closes the zero-roster inflow race (tally publishes before the member positions; the WINDOWED watermark then freezes the empty roster). **STEP 0 gated the cause** — all four zero-roster votes returned populated rosters upstream with tallies matching stored exactly (transient publication, not memberless rolls). Chosen: a **bounded self-heal pass** over holding the vote row (tallies stay live, permanent loss → bounded lag, repair + fix one mechanism — the first run drained the 4, no backfill). Predicate **zero-roster only** (`tally>0 AND NOT EXISTS member_votes`, newest-first, `HEAL_CAP=10`/chamber) — self-limiting (a partial heal exits the set), so no deficit-predicate spin; reuses the per-vote write unchanged; permanent retry (553). **Both branches proven** (549/553): scratch-forced `269` → `pending`, row unchanged, `253`/`254` healed live; revert clean; re-run healed. Drain: all four to full tally, zero residual (the 566 probe re-ran as the regression instrument — M2(a) 0/0). **Participation distortion (per the §0.4 read):** a zero-roster vote **deflates** the 119th participation denominator — `getParticipationStrip` / `participationAggCte` / `getChamberParticipationContext` compute `not_voting / COUNT(*)` over the member's OWN `member_votes`, so a vote with no rows is absent from every member's count (not a per-member MISSED inflation); the career path (`member_career_votes`, Voteview) is unaffected. **Render payoff:** `senate-119-2-146` (Kim Amdt 5545 budget waiver) motion-line split — all-zero → HO 533-suppressed — now renders 44 D + 2 I yea / 53 R nay / 1 D nv. `memberVotesHealed` / `memberVotesHealPending` → payload (id 6056). No theme-% change. See roadmap "Also (HO 566–568)" + oddities + SKILL. Docs: HO 569.

Post-re-enable confirm tick `cron_runs` id 6042 `rosterDeletesRefused: []` — wiring **live in the prod payload**, not guard-evidence (that's the MD-05 falsification). No theme-% change. The build target is dead code: `buildPrimariesCartogram` / `PrimaryDistrictCard` / `PrimaryRow` / `primariesFill` / `PrimariesLegend` all ORPHAN, and `PrimaryMapCard` + the `variant === "primaries"` branches live only inside `CartogramShell`'s primaries fork — unreachable because the sole `<CartogramShell>` (`ElectoralBoard.tsx:103`) passes `variant="races"`. `PrimaryTimeline` (`c.date <= todayISO`) already ships the date-driven model; M3 confirmed **no user-visible wrongness survived HO 333** (`/race/[id]` is runoff-round-only, the member chip date-only). The owed work is a **deletion sweep** (QUEUED), not a fix. No build. See roadmap "Also (HO 559–561)". Docs: HO 562.

- ~~**Motion-aware procedural-amendment-outcome surface (HO 537 M8 → GO'd 550 → STEP-0'd 554 → SHIPPED 557)**~~ — **SHIPPED** (`568d58d` leaf · `7027d10` query · `b40b462` surface; diagnostics `7798997` / `d213cb7` / `70a23ae`), prod-verified at `bf820bd`. The 64 Senate roll calls that touch an amendment without being its up-or-down vote now render a motion line — motion, tally, party split, `/vote/[id]` drill, and a fate clause where the motion decides; **60 amendments gain a line** — 55 with a "→ amendment killed" clause, the rest tally-only; the 4 anchored are suppressed and **none of them are killed rows** (a motion-killed amendment never receives its own up-or-down vote, so suppression can't hide a fate — HO 558 M2). **The STEP 0 gate fired and changed the model.** HO 554's DISAGREE=0 tripwire had been measured against the pre-555 classifier; reconciling the probe's pinned `deriveDisposition` copy (the HO 556 WATCH, discharged) took it 0→2 on `119-samdt-3946`/`3947` — the pair HO 550's 54/56 polarity validation missed. Cause: `impliedFate` described the amendment's status **at the motion**, the dot describes its **outcome**; a failed tabling motion leaves an amendment pending, and it can still die later (both fell with their parents). Fixed by narrowing to **`motionFate → "killed" | "undecided" | "orthogonal"`** — **no positive fate**, since tabling and waiver are kill mechanisms that decide in one direction only (tabling agreed → killed, waiver failed → killed, opposites → undecided). Re-gate `dot=agreed AND fate=killed` measured **0**; `killed=55 · undecided=4 · orthogonal=5`. Rejected: comparing fate against the dot at render time (couples the motion model to `deriveDisposition`'s vocabulary — a future widening would silently change behavior). **No writes, no migration, no index, no sync/cron touch, Senate only, dot untouched, `getAmendments` filter semantics untouched** (hero byte-identical: 336/254/35 + 187 voted). **No theme-% change** — combined bar holds at 92. See roadmap "Also (HO 557)" + oddities ×2. Docs: HO 558.

HO 554's motion-model STEP 0 found the prod dot rendering muted `other` on 53/64 procedural-residual rows whose `latest_action_text` states an unambiguous fate; HO 555 refused to patch the examples and clustered the FULL `other` bucket (115/625 acted): the failed-family gains anchored "ruled out of order" + word-boundaried "fell", rescuing **80/115** (agreed 337→336 · failed 173→254 · otherActed 115→35). Withdrawn / rendered-moot / cloture-not-invoked deliberately stay `other`; "stricken" is not a kill token. Zero collision with the 4-string `votes.result` vocabulary, so one shared function serves both input families. **One accepted flip:** `119-samdt-3963` agreed→failed — the OLD read was the wrong one (a trailing "agreed to" from a subordinate "fell when …" clause; the failed-first ordering now decides it — oddities). Voted block untouched (0 flips); the `?disposition=` filter + voted EXISTS bypass `deriveDisposition` (M4 grep), so filter semantics never moved. M5 (the subordinate-clause instrument) reads 0 → WATCH. **No theme-% change** — combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92. See roadmap "Also (HO 554–555)". Docs: HO 556.

probe `9383b9f`), prod cache-hit verified. `lib/amendment-votes-walk.ts` (HO 532) linked EVERY `recordedVote` off a HAMDT's `/actions` with no question filter (the mirror of the anchored Senate `SENATE_AMDT_Q`), so `119-hamdt-40`→`house-119-1-187` and `119-hamdt-175`→`house-119-2-122` rendered an "On Ordering the Previous Question" procedural tally through the amendment-framed `VoteLine` as the amendment's own outcome (green agreed dot from `deriveDisposition("Passed")`) — wrong on the number and the outcome. **HO 551:** the decisive-form gate `HOUSE_AMDT_QUESTION_LIKE = "On Agreeing to the Amendment%"` — derived from the **full 92-row enumeration** (89 "On Agreeing to the Amendment" + 1 ", as Modified" + 2 procedural, vocabulary CLOSED), NOT the two offenders — added to `lib/amendment-vote-key.ts` beside the Senate one (the asymmetry survived because the House had no counterpart there); applied via a JOIN to `votes` in the INSERT's LIKE; the 2 bad rows deleted one-time, scoped to the enumerated procedural set (dry-run confirmed exactly 2), both offenders keeping their real "On Agreeing to the Amendment" vote, `119-hr-8800` 19-amendment control byte-identical. **HO 552:** the gate collapsed "votes row not synced yet" (pending) and "synced, wrong question" (settled) to one no-link, but 551's own comment falsely claimed the absent case self-heals on re-walk — the queue predicate (`update_date > walked_at`) can't deliver it (a just-voted HAMDT already spent its `update_date` bump getting queued), so a decisive link dropped permanently; Lindsey Graham (`G000359`, R-SC, class 2) **died** (served 2003–2026); Gov. McMaster appointed **Darline Graham (`G000608`)** as caretaker until 2027-01-03. Upstream `legislators-current.yaml` already reflected it (dropped `G000359`, carries `G000608` as the sitting SC class-2 R). CBT was stale — `G000359` still `is_current=1`, `G000608` absent, `S-SC-2026.incumbent_bioguide_id=G000359` — fixed by the **sanctioned `sync:members` → `sync:crosswalk` → `backfill:races`** (the HO 412 `is_current=1`-filtered upsert). Re-dumps confirmed `G000608` added (`is_current=1`, class 2), `G000359` flipped (`is_current=0`), `S-SC-2026` incumbent moved to `G000608`; the **HO 410 audit re-ran fully green** (§1/§2/§4 all 0/0), and the live `/electoral` + `/race/S-SC-2026` now render Darline Graham (100 current senators intact). SC is class 2, so the HO 411 class-derived `next_election_year=2026` was **unaffected** (no `senate-special-elections.json` entry — SC is on-cycle, not an off-cycle special like OH/FL). **Orthogonal, not chased:** `S-SC-2026` carries a **pre-existing NULL `rating`** (surfaced during the fix — a separate low item, unrelated to the incumbent). Hero untouched, feed expand panel byte-identical (the HO 507 containment rule held). **This extends the HO 328 vocabulary arc** the 507 tombstone called complete: the three browse surfaces converged on `.mc-*`, the two detail pages now converge on a second shared shell. - ~~**Playwright smoke-crawler CI wiring → Phase 2 Playwright-in-CI**~~ — **CLOSED HO 504 by explicit tombstone with a REPLACED criterion**, not by meeting the original one (the convention's second door — "an item leaves OPEN LOOPS only by meeting its close-criterion or being explicitly tombstoned"). Opened Jun 30 (HO 379), carried through five HOs. **Original criterion:** the e2e job runs green in CI against a **real DB** with the interaction hardenings. **Replaced with:** an unattended **prod** crawl (`.github/workflows/e2e-prod.yml`, HO 504) on `deployment_status` + daily schedule + `workflow_dispatch`, **no fixture, no secrets**, off the per-commit path. **Why:** the HO 503 probe found the specs guard with `test.skip`-on-empty, so an under-seeded fixture **passes while exercising nothing** — false-green, strictly worse than the red-blindness it was meant to cure (oddities) — and pre-merge protection buys minutes in a single-committer flow that already diff-gates + prod-verifies every change, at the cost of a hundreds-of-row artifact maintained forever against a 132-statement schema. The bucket count (73 tests, A 41 · B 28 · C 2 · D 2 · E 0, 95% nominally servable) said build it; the failure-mode analysis said stop, and it was right. **Fallback considered and NOT taken** (recorded so it isn't re-litigated): a few-dozen-row fixture backing **only** the (A) route crawl tagged `@ci`, never the guarded interaction tests — available if pre-merge #418 protection is ever explicitly wanted. **The probe's one open question — does every (A) route 200 on a migrated-but-empty DB, or 500 on a `rows[0]` access — is MOOT under this verdict** (no fixture is built), not a dangling measurement someone should go take. Hardenings: #2 (console allowlist) + #3 (bill selector) shipped in `fc42872`; #1 (freeze-the-marquee) retired as moot-under-BotID. One surviving thread — the `schedule` first-fire — stays in OPEN LOOPS. Detail: HO 503 report + HO 504.
- ~~**`queryEnactedThisWeek` cold-stall → enacted-helper hint (HO 481)**~~ — SHIPPED `9c19557`. The 5th and last known sibling of the HO 405/407/480 OR-lure, flagged-not-touched by the 480 sweep because it's a shared HO 232 helper (the ENACTED THIS WEEK banner + the week-over-week delta both consume it). Hint-first, no migration: `INDEXED BY idx_bills_enacted` on both `queryEnactedThisWeek` and `queryEnactedPriorWeekCount` — the partial index (`WHERE stage='enacted'`) qualifies because the query carries `AND stage='enacted'`, collapsing `MULTI-INDEX OR` over `idx_bills_dash_stage` + temp-btree → `SCAN bills USING INDEX idx_bills_enacted` (cold 617ms / 106ms, well under the 10s `boundedFetch` wall; identical rows/count vs unhinted). Low-severity by construction (the `stage='enacted'` seek keeps it off the full corpus, so it never tipped the wall — closed for hygiene, to shut the known instances of the class). **Corrected premise:** HO 335's assumption that this rode `idx_bills_enacted` cleanly was wrong — that index keys `(congress, latest_action_date)`, has no `stage_changed_at`, so a residual in-memory sort remains (trivial at the enacted row count). - ~~**`/api/sync` trailing-step timeout → lead-query hint sweep (HO 479 probe / HO 480 fix)**~~ — SHIPPED `b440b82`. **The overrun was mis-diagnosed by its own symptom:** 32 of 40 ticks timed out at the 55s cap, but the trailing step wasn't slow-Gemini (HO 479's hypothesis) — it was the HO 405/407 **cold-DB-stall**. `gatherLeadData`'s transitions query, OR-lured (`is_ceremonial=0 OR IS NULL`) into a `MULTI-INDEX OR` over `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` (~the whole 16.8k corpus) + a temp-btree sort, **ignored the perfect `idx_bills_stage_changed_at`** and cold-stalled past the 10s `boundedFetch` — retrying to 10+10s and *failing* the (non-fatal) lead step, so the dashboard lead had been **silently going stale on every real-delta tick**, worse than the visible timeout implied. HO 479 (read-only probe `scripts/diagnostic/sync-trailing-cost-479.ts`) pinned it; HO 480 swept `gatherLeadData` — `INDEXED BY` on `transRs`/`introRs`/`topicRs`, the 407 remedy applied to the sibling function 407 missed — collapsing lead from the 14–20s stall band to ~1–3s cold, wall now ~33–40s under the 55s cap. **Deferred (own HO):** timeout rows carry no per-step timings — `wrapCronRoute` discards the handler payload on the `Promise.race` reject — a fleet-wide contract change, not needed once the route stops timing out. The 4th lead read (`queryEnactedThisWeek`) mis-plans too → new OPEN LOOP (closed HO 481). See roadmap "Also (HO 480)". Docs: HO 480.

Endpoint is unauthed, `force-dynamic`/`no-store`, not `wrapCronRoute`-wrapped, 503s on a check failure. **The push layer is out-of-repo:** point an uptime monitor (UptimeRobot / cron-job.org) at `/api/health`, alert on non-200 — the only thing that catches a silently-stopped cron (Vercel can't; no invocation to error on). - ~~**Ideology diagnostic — `party_code` vs caucus, not registration (HO 430)**~~ — SHIPPED `d061284`, the second of the two ideology-arc tail cleanups. `scripts/diagnostic/ideology-coverage-419.ts` compared Voteview `party_code`→letter against `members.party` by string equality, structurally false-positiving on independents who caucus with a major party (DW-NOMINATE `party_code` follows **caucus**, `members.party` follows **registration**). Fixed with the **neither-match invariant** — flag a member only when Voteview's letter matches **neither** their registration **nor** their caucus — backed by a 3-entry `INDEPENDENT_CAUCUS` map (`K000383` King→D, `S000033` Sanders→D, `K000401` Kiley→R, bioguide-keyed, fail-loud: a new caucusing independent surfaces as a disagreement until added). **NOT** a caucus-only swap (that would newly break King/Sanders: VV=I vs caucus=D disagree) — it's neither-match. Line relabeled "ICPSR party-switch quirks (note, not fixed here)" → "genuine party mismatches (registration/caucus gap suppressed)". **Premise correction (diagnose-first paid off):** the backlog claimed King, Sanders, *and* Kiley all tripped the diagnostic; the **before** run showed **only Kiley** did. King/Sanders code Voteview **328 → I**, which already matches `members.party='I'`, so they agreed and never flagged — correcting the earlier "coded 100/D by Voteview" read for those two (recorded in a new oddities entry, HO 422's note left as the historical record). Only Kiley (registered I since 2026-03-09, caucuses R → Voteview `200`/R) diverges from registration — the HO 422 caucus-vs-registration distinction confirmed on live data. **After:** 0 disagreements on the clean roster with all three independents present. **Diagnostic-only** — `members.party='I'` for Kiley is correct (HO 422, NOT A BUG); no `sync-members`/`members.party` touch. `tsc` clean. - ~~**Summarize cron doom loop — bounded queue read + concurrency (HO 405 probe / HO 406 fix)**~~ — SHIPPED `82fb6bc`, live on prod, drained end-to-end. **HO 405** diagnosed two co-occurring causes; **HO 406** fixed both. **Mode 1 (aborts):** HO 383 dropped the queue read's SQL LIMIT to fetch the full null set for the app-side stage-priority sort; at 1,374 nulls it became `SCAN bills USING INDEX idx_bills_update_date` over all 16,623 rows and tipped the 10s `boundedFetch` (the 07-02/06-29 55s soft-timeouts + the 06-30 20s error). Fixed with a partial index `idx_bills_summarize_queue ON bills(update_date DESC) WHERE summary IS NULL`, forced `INDEXED BY` — the statless planner mis-plans into a `MULTI-INDEX OR` on `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` unhinted (verified). Read → `SCAN … idx_bills_summarize_queue`: **5.07s cold** at full backlog, **48ms** drained (both under the 10s wall). The app-side `computeStage` priority sort is preserved — stored `stage` is stale by design (HO 383), so an SQL stage sort can't replace it; the handoff's "push priority into SQL with LIMIT" option was rejected for this reason. **Mode 3 (throughput):** serial ~1.9s/bill cleared only ~23/tick vs ~30/day inflow → a bounded **C=5** worker pool in `lib/summarize-runner.ts` (per-bill 15s AbortController unchanged, so up to C in flight at the 45s deadline still tail out ≤15s — the 45+15=60s ceiling holds), and the cron per-tick cap **50→200** so the budget is the wall. One real prod tick: **80** summarized, 0 fail/timeout, `budgetStopped`, 49.6s. **Drain:** one unbounded `npm run summarize` cleared **1,374 → 7** (the 7 were 15s per-bill timeouts, deferred 24h, retry next tick). **Two gotchas → oddities:** (1) building the partial index over 16k rows itself tipped the 10s `boundedFetch`, so `npm run migrate` aborted it — built once out-of-band with a long-timeout libsql client, `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` no-ops thereafter. (2) the ceremonial filter added to the queue is a verified no-op (`is_ceremonial` is set only at summarize time; sync resets it to NULL), kept as harmless feed-alignment, NOT the inflow reduction the brief assumed. Non-goals held: no schedule change, no Pro upgrade, no model/prompt change. Docs reconciled here (HO 406): backlog (this tombstone + the closed OPEN LOOP, Foundation-96-no-longer-fused note) + oddities ×2. Doc/code: HO 406.

The `/dashboard-v2` cold-start 500 wasn't a cold-start problem at all — it was a whole CLASS of summary-gated fat-`bills` COUNT/aggregates the statless Turso planner mis-plans onto `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` (a MULTI-INDEX OR over ~every row), slow even warm + nondeterministic (112ms↔18s+). It surfaced as live 10s-abort 500s on **`/members`** (HO 277, digest 4101894172), **`/dashboard-v2`** (HO 278, the swap blocker), and **`/bills`** (HO 279). Fixed with additive covering/partial indexes + gated `INDEXED BY` hints (the planner refuses them unhinted): `idx_bills_summary_feed` (shared: getFeedStats / getCorpusStats(true) / getFeedBills COUNT), `idx_bills_summary_stage`, `idx_bills_summary_topics`, `idx_bills_sponsor_agg`. **getFeedStats was app-wide** (masthead, every HeaderBar page) — fixing it protected every inner page. Cold-verified: `/members` 0.885s, `/dashboard-v2` 5× cold 200, `/bills` 0.705s first-hit-after-idle. 9 commits (`859466a`…`b423329`). The v2→`/` swap is unblocked (→ QUEUED). Residual same-class siblings → WATCH below. Doc sweep: HO 280.

- **Homepage `/` cold-start 500** — FIXED 2026-06-14/15 (HO 241; commits `d7c2435` forced indexes + `3880c59` prewarm revert). First `/` hit after Turso idle-evicted the `bills` page cache took ~18–20s and tripped the 10s `DB_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` abort (×2 retry = 20s 500); the recompute couldn't populate the Data Cache before aborting, so it never self-healed. *Root cause:* the ~63MB `bills` table cold-scanned by ~6 homepage queries, and the **statless planner** (ANALYZE blocked on Turso, like VACUUM) grabbed `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` for the `(is_ceremonial=0 OR NULL)` OR — ~13–20s **even warm**. *Fix:* 2 covering indexes (`idx_bills_dash_stage`, `idx_bills_dash_topics`) for the aggregates + `INDEXED BY` hints on 5 feed queries forcing the existing date/news indexes. Cold `/` 500@20s → 200@~2s, **self-recovering via populate-on-read** (the cron prewarm built mid-investigation was reverted — dead problem once the queries are fast). Detour tombstoned: the prewarm + `revalidate:false` approach (it 504'd the sync route stacking cold queries behind the unbounded Gemini lead). See HO 241 ABANDONED banner + `SKILL.md` forced-index note.
